“This is not something worth your time, any of you. Use those precious blades and swords of yours against opponents with hot blood flowing through their veins. Please yield them to me.”

“My sword is not such a precious sword.”

At the Sword King’s words, Geom Mugeuk recalled what had happened before.

“It isn’t the wielder who decides what kind of sword a martial artist’s sword is. Others must decide that. Brother Ak’s sword is a precious sword. It has been ever since it showed the stars to those people underground and sent them safely on their way.”

Since Geom Mugeuk went so far, the Sword King could no longer insist.

“I’ve spent the entire day stepping forward with nothing to show for it.”

With an awkward expression, the Sword King tried to take off his shoes.

“Don’t take them off. If they wear out, I’ll buy you even better shoes.”

The Sword King stubbornly removed the shoes and tied them at his waist.

“No. I like these shoes. Now then, shall I see how much my friend’s skill has improved?”

When the Sword King stepped back, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Ilhwa Sword Venerable also stepped back. In the first place, the first person to step forward had been the Sword King, and they had only moved to help him.

Even if they did not fight directly, simply watching Geom Mugeuk fight would surely help their martial attainments. Perhaps that was why Geom Mugeuk had brought them along.

Srrrng.

Geom Mugeuk drew the Black Demon Sword and slowly walked forward.

The Iron King was still staying off to the side.

A fight of nine against one.

Normally, it would have been an absurd situation.

Though they did not possess complete reason, they were armed with even stronger martial arts and vengeance.

These nine Twelve Zodiac Kings were a force capable of setting out to conquer the murim immediately.

And Geom Mugeuk was about to face them alone.

Even for Geom Mugeuk, a fight like this was something that might happen only once in a lifetime.

The Iron King shouted to the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

“Now, it is time for revenge!”

The one who eagerly received that encouragement and excitement was Geom Mugeuk. He shouted into the air.

“Something exciting! As loud as possible!”

The moment he finished speaking, Geom Mugeuk charged toward the Twelve Zodiac Kings first.

The sight of him rushing toward nine masters whose martial prowess had reached the extreme was truly spectacular.

The Twelve Zodiac Kings spread to the left and right with the Fighting King at the center and met Geom Mugeuk.

Whoooong!

The Fighting King’s fist flew in with enough power to shatter steel.

As Geom Mugeuk dodged the fist, the Blade King’s Red Blade narrowly brushed past the side of his face.

Ten red lines extending from the Blood King’s fingers shot toward Geom Mugeuk.

When Geom Mugeuk avoided the blood threads, the Fist King attacking from behind was struck instead, but he merely frowned once and continued lunging toward Geom Mugeuk.

Amid the exchange of fists and swords, the Killing King’s hidden weapons split the air.

Geom Mugeuk did not counterattack and only dodged. It was as if he intended to see whether he could evade the combined assault of all nine.

All manner of sword lights, astral qi, and killing intent of different natures whirled through that place. The swirling astral qi became a dragon and ascended, and sword energy became a net to cover its opponent. But the dragon could not seize the orb, and the net could not catch its fish.

The Illusion King and the Illusion Woman used illusion arts and opened a door. Evil spirits sprang out from the door.

The Fighting King was leading the charge, and the blood spewed by the Blood King scattered toward Geom Mugeuk. The Fist King’s punch flew in, and the Killing King launched a surprise attack.

At that moment, Ilhwa Sword Venerable could feel it. Coincidentally, the scene resembled the puppets that had attacked Geom Mugeuk together earlier. As if it had foretold fate.

And Geom Mugeuk showed the result of that fate. He used the Wind God’s Four Steps to its limit and escaped the combined assault.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwang!

The attacks struck empty ground.

Watching that sight, the Sword King took one step forward.

He knew Geom Mugeuk had grown stronger, but—

‘He really has become strong!’

He had grown incomparably stronger than when they had last parted.

The higher a master climbed, the slower their growth became. Yet Geom Mugeuk was once again showing a level of skill that forced one to look at him anew. The Sword King wanted to draw his sword immediately and leap into the battle. Just watching Geom Mugeuk dodge made his heart pound like mad.

This battle, which had reached the extreme of skill, was a battle of points and lines.

If a point struck, one died; if a line was drawn, one died as well.

Each point gathered to become a line, and the line scattered again to become points.

Attacks that should have killed him several times over poured down on Geom Mugeuk, but Geom Mugeuk did not die.

Geom Mugeuk’s movements were beautiful. It was truly as if he were performing a sword dance to the music. That was why, while facing the Twelve Zodiac Kings, he was also fighting the soul of the Sound King.

The Sword King, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who were watching, were receiving great inspiration from this battle. A single supposition bloomed in all their hearts.

If it were me?

How would I dodge, and how would I attack? The enlightenment that came from that comparison was as great as the lessons gained from fighting directly. After all, the battle being shown to them belonged to none other than Geom Mugeuk.

At least one thing was certain. They could not fight like this.

Slash! Slaaaash.

Red lines appeared around Geom Mugeuk, surrounding him like a spiderweb.

The Blood King’s hidden trump card, the Divided Blood Net, had been unleashed.

Pa-pa-pa-pa-pak!

Geom Mugeuk’s Black Demon Sword tore through the blood net in an instant and emerged outside it. Seeing Geom Mugeuk’s figure, incomparable to when they had fought before, an even denser blood energy surged from the Blood King’s body.

As the music grew louder and raised the power of the Twelve Zodiac Kings—

Swaaaaaak!

A massive astral qi unleashed by the Fighting King flew toward Geom Mugeuk.

The Chain Wind Cloud Fist, now bolstered by the Dark King’s power as well, struck Geom Mugeuk just as he had torn through the blood net.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwang!

The earth and grass where Geom Mugeuk had stood were swept away, forming a long, straight trench.

It was truly tremendous power.

When the dust settled, Geom Mugeuk could be seen standing at the very front of the trench with his arms crossed. Astonishingly, he had not been pushed back even a single step.

Geom Mugeuk had blocked the incoming attack with three martial arts: the Heavenly Gang Body obtained beneath the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, the Vajra Asura of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist, and the Heavenly Demon Protective Art.

As if he had deliberately chosen not to dodge in order to test his protective art, Geom Mugeuk strode toward the Fighting King and said,

“Your fighting spirit isn’t what it used to be. Did you two have a fight?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s provocation, the Fighting King’s face twisted viciously. It had been an attack combined with the Dark King’s power, certainly stronger than when they had fought last, yet he had blocked it with his bare body.

Whoosh!

The next moment, something wrapped around Geom Mugeuk like a cloth. It was the Curtain of Darkness cast by the enraged Dark King.

Puuuk.

But compared to when he had suffered it before, the Black Demon Sword emerged far faster and tore through the Curtain of Darkness.

“Back then, you said that as long as you were in the darkness, I could never win with this technique. Let’s see if that still holds true.”

Whoosh!

In an instant, the surroundings darkened, and a single line was drawn.

The Fourth Form of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art: Dark Flash.

The Dark King said leisurely,

“Young Cult Leader, I told you clearly, did I not? Dark-type demonic arts will never work on me…”

Srrrrk.

Thud.

Her neck was severed, and her head rolled to the ground.

The Dark Flash that had reached mastery had cut off the Dark King’s head.

But though her head had been severed, her soul did not disappear. With an expression of disbelief and shock, her head hurled curses at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk understood.

‘Cutting off the head isn’t enough to extinguish them.’

Seeing the Dark King’s severed neck, the Twelve Zodiac Kings unleashed astral qi all at once.

Bang! Bang! Kwaaang! Kwaang!

The Sword King’s heart sank as he watched. Such powerful attacks were concentrated on one point that he even wondered whether he should have gone to help.

When the dust settled, Geom Mugeuk’s figure appeared.

Before Geom Mugeuk stood a pure white wall of astral qi.

The Third Form of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art: Great Demon Wall.

Having achieved mastery of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art, he could now even make the Great Demon Wall small enough to protect only himself.

When even that attack was blocked, the performance rang out loudly enough to tear the ears. The melody screamed. It was asking why they could not win when it was empowering them this much.

An even more intense energy poured from the eyes of the Twelve Zodiac Kings. It seemed they were now about to launch attacks that surpassed their limits.

By contrast, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze as he looked at them was calm.

“I’d like to fight more, but I can’t waste any more inner force. I’ve confirmed well enough just how strong I’ve become.”

Had his internal energy already run out? The two Demon Venerables, who knew just how profound Geom Mugeuk’s internal energy was, looked at each other and exchanged glances. Did the Great Demon Wall demand that much internal energy?

That was not the reason.

Geom Mugeuk bade farewell to the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

“At least for today, you are without sin. You were only dragged to a place you should never have come. Now, return to the place from which you came. There will never again be a day when you return.”

Then he spoke incomprehensibly to the three behind him.

“Stand directly behind me. I still can’t control it as precisely as Father, but at the very least I’m capable of releasing it forward. For some reason, I also have a feeling I can unleash it without destroying this place.”

Geom Mugeuk looked back and added,

“If I fail to control the force, I’ll leave the rest to you.”

Hyeolcheon Doma’s eyes widened in shock.

“What are you trying to do?”

Geom Mugeuk grinned at Hyeolcheon Doma, then thrust the Black Demon Sword forward.

Flash!

The Sixth Form of the Nine Flames Demonic Sword, Heavenly Demon Annihilates the World.

Light burst from the Black Demon Sword.

The entire world turned white. The sun exploded, and that explosion was a soundless one.

Within that light, their final forms could be seen.

The arms of the Fist King, crossed to block the incoming light, were disappearing. Along with his arms, his body, too, dissolved into the light.

The sun engraved upon the black saber thrust out by the Saber King vanished as it met the true sun.

The Assassin King threw himself away to conceal his presence, but there was nowhere to hide.

The severed head and body of the Dark King, and even the darkness that still lay sunken around her, vanished as though melting into the light.

The Illusion King and Hwanyeo vanished as well, like lies within an illusion. The Fighting King’s thirst for revenge melted away, and the Blood King’s blood melted away. The device attached to the Iron King’s chest that had threatened all their lives melted without leaving a trace.

Even their souls were utterly annihilated.

Now, nothing remained there.

Not a blade of grass, not a shard of stone. Not even a single scrap of iron that had once been the bones of the Twelve Zodiac Kings remained. The performance that had been resounding all this time had now ceased as well. The soul of the Sound King had been completely annihilated.

The Nine Flames Demonic Art, brought to great completion—its final technique.

Heavenly Demon Annihilates the World.

In this moment when the Heavenly Demon destroyed the world, there was no being that could survive.

It was the moment all of the Twelve Zodiac Kings, save for the Sword King and the Fire King who had sided with Geom Mugeuk, were wiped out.

Before this overwhelming martial might, the three behind him could not utter a word. The two Demon Venerables had even forgotten to pay their respects to the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

Geom Mugeuk gazed at the empty space in deep emotion.

‘I did it!’

He had succeeded in unleashing the Heavenly Demon Annihilates the World, brought to great completion, without destroying the Spacetime Transposition Art.

The three behind him stared at Geom Mugeuk’s back with eyes filled with admiration and reverence. It was reverence for the Nine Flames Demonic Art, and reverence for the martial artist named Geom Mugeuk.

“Since they’ve returned to their own world.”

Geom Mugeuk drew up the last remaining handful of his internal energy.

“Let us return to our world as well.”

Snap.

Chapter 761: Youngest, where are you?
-------------------------------------
“It seems a formation has been activated!”

The one who shouted was Jihan.

Geomugeuk, the two Demon Venerables, and the master who had been called Brother Ak vanished from before their eyes in an instant.

If only they had disappeared, it truly would have been the worst possible situation, but fortunately, the enemies facing them had all disappeared along with them.

In truth, it was not surprising. In this unbelievable battle, where even dead souls had been summoned, there was nothing strange about them vanishing into a formation. Everyone assumed that someone had deployed one.

Jihan said to Taesu,

“If you’re going to get out of this room, now is your chance.”

If the ones who reappeared were not Geomugeuk and his party but the iron-masked man, everyone here would die.

“What about you?”

At Taesu’s question, Jihan answered,

“I will wait for the Young Cult Leader.”

It was not that, having become his subordinate, he had no choice but to wait. It was a decision imbued with the resolve to share life and death with the Young Cult Leader.

—Are you not also a sword that has fought alone for a long time?

No one had ever said such words to him in his life. Those words Geomugeuk had spoken kept circling in his mind.

Taesu knew Jihan’s suggestion that he leave came from goodwill, but it did not sit well with him.

“I’ll wait too.”

You’re not leaving yourself, so why are you telling me that? Just what do you take me for!

But the reason he could not say those words aloud was because he felt cowed.

Until he had come here, he had thought that, as the one carrying the treasure map, he was the protagonist. Even if not the protagonist, he had believed he was at least an important character.

But he was not. The young man carrying the packs had been the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, and the old man and woman with him were Demon Venerables.

The martial artist over there who had identified himself as Pung was a man sent by the Sado Alliance Leader, and the other man was said to be the head of a family that managed the tombs of successive Sado Alliance Leaders.

The only one who had seemed relatively approachable was Jihan, but he had already earned Geomugeuk’s trust.

Though he knew that in a perilous situation like this, the more important one’s status was, the more dangerous it became, he nevertheless felt an inexplicable sense of alienation.

Suddenly, he remembered what Geomugeuk had said.

—You have an enormous amount of pride.

Now he felt he understood clearly why he had said something that would hurt his father.

He wanted to live a life where he drew attention. A life where he was the protagonist. A life where he did not have to hear, The chance to run is now, so if you’re going, go quickly.

And that was a life he could never achieve while living as a thief.

Jihan was not the only one who urged someone to escape. Pung, the martial artist of the Sado Alliance, advised Baekchong Family Head Dan Sojin.

“Family Head, you should withdraw now.”

If he was the head of the family that managed the tombs of successive Sado Alliance Leaders, then he was someone Pung had to protect.

But for whatever reason, Dan Sojin did not leave his place.

“I am fine.”

Dan Sojin could feel it. This was not something he could avoid simply by getting through this moment.

If the one who emerged from the formation was not Geomugeuk, then even if he fled now, he would die in the end anyway.

The dice had already been cast. He could only hope that the dice he had thrown, even while exposing himself, would show the number he desired.

How much time passed like that?

The dice that had been rolling came to a stop.

Just as they had vanished, they suddenly appeared.

“Ah!”

A cry of joy burst from Jihan’s mouth.

Geomugeuk, the Sword King, and the two Demon Venerables had appeared safe and sound. And not one of them was injured.

Everyone felt relieved at the sight of them.

“You have returned?”

Jihan welcomed Geomugeuk with a deeply moved expression. Taesu, of course, as well as Pung and Dan Sojin, were greatly pleased.

The one most delighted among them was Dan Sojin. In this matter, not only his own life but the survival of his entire family had been at stake.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon said to Geomugeuk,

“Recover your internal energy first.”

“Yes, Elder.”

Geomugeuk sat cross-legged on the spot and began circulating his qi. Then the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, and the Sword King stood guard around him.

It was a scene that made one truly feel that Geomugeuk was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

The Sword King, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable were recalling the scene they had witnessed within the Space-Time Displacement Art. How could they easily forget that battle?

They remembered the sight of Geomugeuk evading the attacks of all twelve Zodiac Kings.

If Taesu or Jihan had seen that fight, they might have felt that the opponents were nothing special because Geomugeuk had avoided them so easily.

But they knew. They knew how difficult it was to avoid each and every one of those moves. Geomugeuk had fought all those people who unleashed such threatening attacks.

And then there was that one move they could never forget.

That final move that annihilated everything would be a sight they would remember until the day they died.

Geomugeuk, having finished circulating his qi, opened his eyes. Seeing the three of them guarding him, Geomugeuk made a fuss.

“Tell anyone in the martial world who has ever received protection from more incredible people than this to step forward!”

As Geomugeuk rose to his feet and was about to chatter away, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon moved first.

“First, let’s get out of this unpleasant place.”

The Iron King was dead, but the mechanisms on all sides were still open. There was no need to remain in a place like this.

They left the room and then exited the treasure-map tomb entirely. Through the passage they had entered by, they swam out through the pond as large as a lake.

After being in the stifling underground, coming outside felt refreshing.

Geomugeuk shouted in good spirits,

“Now I feel alive!”

It was not just that he felt alive. They had all truly come out alive.

Everyone’s gaze focused on him. Seeing their relieved expressions, Geomugeuk finally felt that this battle was over.

Geomugeuk looked up at the sky.

The Sword King and the two Demon Venerables, as well as the remaining four, followed Geomugeuk and looked up at the sky.

After they had looked up at the sky like that for a long while, Geomugeuk took care of Taesu first.

“Are you all right?”

Taesu had been thinking that he was the most unnecessary person here, so when Geomugeuk took care of him first, that single sentence made his heart surge.

“Yes, I’m all right.”

“I have a guess as to what it is you want to say to me. Do you still feel the same way?”

He had thought he wanted to live as Geomugeuk’s subordinate.

“I’m not sure.”

Taesu answered honestly. Geomugeuk nodded.

“Meet your father, think it over carefully, and decide slowly.”

In his life before regression, Geomugeuk had formed a connection with Taesu’s father, Taegon, and had been indebted to him. That was why he intended to give Taesu a chance.

“You can come a year from now, or you can come ten years from now.”

“May I really do that?”

At his trembling question, Geomugeuk nodded.

“But you must know this. Taking you into our cult may be possible because of your connection with me, but once you enter the cult, from that point on you must live your own life, one unrelated to me. It may even be difficult for you to see my face even once. That applies to Jihan over there as well. I will only be the trigger. The one who lives the changed life is you. Do not forget. Even if your environment changes, if you do not change, nothing will be different.”

Taesu looked at Geomugeuk in silence.

“When you are ready to take responsibility for your own life, come find me.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader. First, I must go see my father.”

He thought he should first apologize for the words he had said that day that had hurt his father.

After Taesu bowed respectfully to Geomugeuk and the others, he left the place.

Whether he would come to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, or live on treating this day as a memory from one time in his life, no one could know.

Geomugeuk thought it would be fine even if he did not come. Would it not be all right for there to be at least one thief who stole only from evildoers and helped the poor?

Geomugeuk went to Dan Sojin and Pung of the Sado Alliance.

“Martial Artist Pung, return directly to the alliance and report what happened.”

His mission was to report on this treasure-map incident, so now all that remained was for him to return and make his report.

“And as for the mechanisms installed in the Sword Tomb, the Sado Alliance should take care of the aftermath.”

Even if it was not the real tomb, it was nevertheless a place known as the tomb of Sado One Sword Ju Gasin, so Geomugeuk thought it was only right that they handle it.

“Yes, we will take care of it.”

Pung, who had walked some distance away, looked back at Geomugeuk and said,

“Thank you. If the Young Cult Leader had not been here, I would not have returned alive.”

He had not directly played an active role, but having witnessed all of it was his contribution.

“If I had not been here, they would not have been here either. So if anything, I should be the one apologizing to you, Martial Artist Pung. I am glad you are safe.”

Pung gazed at Geomugeuk for a moment, then cupped his fist respectfully and disappeared.

Now the only one remaining was Dan Sojin.

“Thank you for saving my life.”

He treated Geomugeuk with the utmost courtesy. It was not simply because Geomugeuk had saved his life. From the way he treated Jihan and Taesu, Dan Sojin could tell what kind of person he was. He was someone worthy of respect.

“I know that there is something they are trying to find in the tomb. By any chance, does the Young Cult Leader know what kind of object it is?”

“I do.”

But he could not tell him the truth exactly as it was.

“It is something utterly useless to anyone else. It is an object that has meaning only to them and to me.”

Judging by the faces of the enemies mobilized this time, Dan Sojin could guess that the object was truly important.

In his heart, he truly wanted to take Geomugeuk there and tell him to search for it. What Geomugeuk had saved was not only his life but his entire family.

“Do you remember that you agreed to grant me one request if I helped you, Family Head Dan?”

“I said I would grant it if it was a request I could fulfill.”

Dan Sojin anticipated Geomugeuk’s request. Naturally, it would be a request to let him enter the tomb. In a situation like this, what else would the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult ask of him?

But that was the judgment of a man who still did not know the person named Geomugeuk very well.

Geomugeuk’s request was truly something he had never imagined.

“When the Sado Alliance Leader passes away someday, please see to it that he is well attended.”

“!”

Dan Sojin was inwardly greatly shocked.

“Is that the request the Young Cult Leader intended to make?”

“It is. Please attend to him especially well.”

The Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was worrying about the Sado Alliance Leader after his death?

“Did you not wish to enter the tombs of the successive Sado Alliance Leaders?”

“Of course I want to enter.”

“Then why do you not demand that?”

Geomugeuk had already read his resolve.

“You intend not to reveal the location of the tomb even if you must give up your life, do you not?”

Dan Sojin had intended exactly that. He had meant to say that he would grant any other request, but not that one.

“That is correct. It is a duty handed down by our family law from long ago, so it is not something I can decide arbitrarily.”

“I thought as much. Would it not be a waste to throw away such a precious request on someone like that?”

No matter how important it was to obtain the bead, it was not something he should do by pressuring Dan Sojin to learn the location.

“Instead, I intend to search for the location of the tomb in my own way. It is truly important to me, and it will also be for the sake of your Baekchong Family. If I do not take possession of it, those bastards will target it until the very end.”

The process of obtaining the previous five beads had all come about through fate and connections. He had not obtained them out of greed, nor by forcing things recklessly.

“If my destiny is connected to it, I will obtain it.”

Dan Sojin would rather Geomugeuk discover the tomb himself.

‘But you will not find it easily, Young Cult Leader.’

And even if he did find it, it was not a place one could enter easily.

Whether he knew that thought or not, Geomugeuk was speaking in a carefree manner.

“Ah, and later, please take good care of my friend Sain as well. Sain will probably be handled by the next-generation family head, yes? When you pass down your position, please make sure to tell them I ask it of them.”

Dan Sojin could tell that those words were a sincere request.

The Young Cult Leader was not the person he had thought he was. Perhaps the dice he had thrown this time had numbers written on them that he had never imagined.

“Seeing you, Young Cult Leader, makes me think you are like the wind. I hope that from now on as well, you continue to live a fine life entrusting yourself to the wind like this.”

Dan Sojin bowed his head respectfully in farewell and left the place.

Geomugeuk turned toward the Sword King and the Demon Venerables.

When his eyes met the Sword King’s, Geomugeuk beat him to the punch.

“Don’t even dream of it. This time, you must come with me to our cult. Our Brother Ak has a woman waiting for him with all her heart!”

Just as the Sword King held back his answer for a moment,

the Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke abruptly.

“Let us go together.”

The Sword King looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon said nothing more.

After gazing at him for a moment, the Sword King, for whatever reason, answered readily.

“Yes, let’s do that.”

Naturally, Geom Mugeuk voiced his complaint.

“What! When I asked you to go, you wouldn’t even answer me!”

“It’s different with you.”

“No, you can’t go! Bare feet, horsetail hair, sword riffraff—all are forbidden from entering the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

It was clear that the Sword King’s willingness to sacrifice himself for him had earned him tremendous points with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

A thought suddenly occurred to Geom Mugeuk. The free-spirited Sword King and the book-loving Blood Heaven Blade Demon—perhaps, unexpectedly, the two might get along quite well.

“Sword Venerable.”

“What is it?”

“What do you think of enshrining your maternal uncle here on Cheonmok Mountain?”

Inside the long pouch hanging from Geom Mugeuk’s leather bag were his remains.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable did not ponder for long. His uncle had roamed the martial world all his life, so it seemed fitting to lay him to rest here on Cheonmok Mountain, where he had undertaken his final life-and-death adventure.

“Let us do that. It is a good idea.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon said to Geom Mugeuk,

“You truly never forget or overlook anything.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had even forgotten the fact that they had recovered the body of Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s uncle from the Sword Tomb.

“Someday, there will come a time when I forget and overlook things every time as well.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jihan and said,

“When that time comes, you’ll have to look after me.”

“Yes, I will keep that in mind.”

He answered energetically, but inwardly he was worried. I’m older than the Young Cult Leader, so wouldn’t I be even more scatterbrained by then?

For Geom Mugeuk’s sake, he thought he would have to live younger and keep his wits even sharper.

And so they buried the body and made a grave on a sunny hill of Cheonmok Mountain.

“Please go down first. I’ll look around here for a while before coming down.”

The Sword King asked, wondering if perhaps—

“Do you think the grave of the Evil Dao Alliance Leader is here?”

“Yes, I’m certain it’s somewhere on this mountain. It may take a few days, so please rest comfortably.”

The reason Dan Sojin had stepped forward personally, and the reason she had asked him for help, must have been because the pressure from those people had risen right up to her throat.

“I’ll go down first.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned away, and Ilhwa Sword Venerable and the Sword King followed him down. At least for today, the three of them had no desire whatsoever to protect Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk said to Jihan,

“Take good care of those three.”

“Yes!”

And so Geom Mugeuk was left alone on Cheonmok Mountain.

He looked around several peaks. Yes, it would not be easy to find.

But he had things those bastards behind the scenes did not.

He had the secret coffer, and within his body were the five energies.

Geom Mugeuk’s shout toward the last remaining energy echoed and rang out.

“Youngest, where are you? Your brothers are here.”

Chapter 762: It Was My First Time Being Greeted by a Martial Artist
-------------------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk was glaring sharply at something.

After staring for a long while, he soon rubbed his eyes.

“Good grief, my eyes hurt.”

He had been using the Divine Eye Art to peer intently through the cliff across from him.

He had been checking to see if there might be a hole or some mechanism in the cliff, but no matter how he looked, there was nothing special there.

Geom Mugeuk started walking again.

“Youngest, just where are you?”

The first place Geom Mugeuk had searched was the pond on the peak opposite the pond where the sword tomb had been. After all, those two ponds were like a pair of eyes, representing Cheonmoksan.

Since there had been a fake sword tomb on this side, perhaps there might be an entrance leading to the real tomb at the pond on the opposite peak?

Unfortunately, there was not. He searched every inch beneath the pond, but found no passage.

After that, he began searching the nearby peaks one by one.

It was no easy task.

As if they would hang a signboard saying, “Tomb of the former Sado Alliance Leaders.” It would surely have been made so that even if it were right before his eyes, he would pass by without seeing it.

Could he really find that secretly hidden tomb in this vast Cheonmoksan?

Even so, he could not let impatience drive him to race here and there with lightness skill as he searched. He had to sense any changes in his body or vibrations from the secret coffer.

The problem was this. If that final bead was hidden deep inside the tomb, would the energy in his body or the secret coffer even be able to react?

Until now, the secret coffer had only reacted when he approached a bead.

The one and only hope was that this bead was the final energy.

Geom Mugeuk focused all his mind and climbed the mountain while examining his surroundings.

As he walked, he emitted his qi. The energy stretched out in all directions like a spiderweb, probing the area around him. He could even sense insects rustling beneath rotten leaves, but there was no other special energy.

Yes, that was only natural. It would be stranger if he found it easily.

After walking for a long while, he heard the sound of water. Geom Mugeuk went down to a valley and washed his face.

His own face was reflected in the stream.

Over that face, the face he had worn in old age before his regression overlapped.

Compared to the hardships of that time, this was nothing.

“Now then, let’s go find the youngest!”

But although he searched every inch of the place until late, he could not find the entrance to the tomb.

Someone unused to searching might grow anxious, wondering if they had missed a spot. What if they had overlooked it here and ended up wasting effort elsewhere?

Even so, one had to move on without lingering attachment.

Geom Mugeuk knew from experience. A place missed once would not easily catch the eye no matter how many times one looked at it again.

So one must not look back, but move on to the next place. Only by pushing forward like that could one search without growing exhausted.

“Don’t worry. Finding things is what I’m better at than killing people.”

* * *

That night, Geom Mugeuk camped alone.

Until now, most of his camps had been with others.

But today was a camp meant only for himself. He lit a bonfire and made a place just for himself. Sitting on soft leather, he roasted meat for himself and drank wine.

Crackle, crackle.

He did not dislike this time spent drinking a cup while watching the burning bonfire. No, he enjoyed it. He had originally been someone who liked being alone.

Even the solitary times that had been hard in his life before regression, thinking back on them now, had not been painful because of loneliness.

‘What if I can’t find the materials for the Great Art? What if my life ends while I’m still only searching for the materials?’

It had been difficult because of anxieties like those.

But in the end, he had overcome them well. He had endured and come all the way here.

So he deserved to hear these words. Especially at the end of this journey, during which he had dealt with all of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

“You’re doing well, Mugeuk.”

Geom Mugeuk praised himself.

After his regression, he had praised others until his mouth went dry, but he had lived forgetting to praise himself.

He could not remember when he had last so openly praised himself. Had there ever been such a time?

“Mugeuk, you really are doing well.”

Only the stars filling the sky were sending sparkling applause for this precious praise.

* * *

The One-Flower Sword Venerable stood alone, looking up at the night sky.

“Can’t sleep?”

When she turned around, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was walking toward her.

After parting from Geom Mugeuk and descending the mountain, they had stayed at an inn in the village. The place she was in now was the entrance to the village, a little distance from the inn. She had stepped out briefly for a walk.

“I can’t seem to sleep tonight.”

It was not simply because her sleeping place had changed.

“I keep thinking of the way the Young Cult Leader fought.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked as though he understood her. He was the same. It would probably stimulate and torment them for some time.

“How about you, Brother?”

“What do you mean?”

He thought she was asking about the Young Cult Leader’s martial arts, but her question was about something else.

“This trip outside the cult. I don’t think I’ve ever had a journey this enjoyable in my life.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon nodded. He felt the same. It had been a journey with Geom Mugeuk and the One-Flower Sword Venerable; how could it not have been enjoyable?

“That’s because Mugeuk was so considerate.”

This time, the One-Flower Sword Venerable nodded in agreement.

“The Young Cult Leader likes you very much, Brother.”

“That brat? No way.”

Though he said that, how could the Blood Heaven Blade Demon not know Geom Mugeuk’s feelings? How could he not know the brat’s heart, trying to bolster his pride before the One-Flower Sword Venerable with every word he spoke? How could he not know after seeing him pile up all those elixirs that restored youth?

Yes, the fact that he could understand such feelings was probably a gift of travel that could only be known by going outside the cult together.

“You’ve changed a lot too, Brother. In the past, you wouldn’t have asked that man to come with us.”

When he had suggested to the Sword King that they go together to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, she had been inwardly surprised. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was not that kind of person. Naturally, she had thought he had made the suggestion for the Young Cult Leader’s sake.

“It wasn’t because of the Young Cult Leader. It was because of you.”

“Because of me?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon said something unexpected.

“I thought you might want to fight him one more time.”

The One-Flower Sword Venerable looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon with an unreadable smile.

“It was the first time I’d felt that way. Three people who use swords gathered together, and my skill was last?”

She could accept that the Young Cult Leader was stronger than her. He had learned the Nine Flames Demonic Art, and Geom Mugeuk was someone more special than anyone else.

But the Sword King was a different case. She had met him as an enemy, and he was someone who used the sword just as she did.

And yet she had not been able to defeat him. In the end, it had looked like a draw, but she knew. He had yielded one move to her.

“Shall I confess something?”

At her words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon flinched in surprise. She had never said such things in her life.

“There was a time when I came to hate the sword.”

Perhaps the greatest gain the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had obtained from this journey was his relationship with her. She had come to reveal her inner thoughts like this. They had grown quite comfortable even before, but not to this extent.

“Come now, a Sword Venerable hating the sword?”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s joking rebuke, the One-Flower Sword Venerable smiled faintly and drew her sword.

“No matter how much I trained, there was no progress in my swordsmanship. It felt as though I had become tightly wedged somewhere, blocked off. Just thinking about it made me feel suffocated.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon knew. Since her youth, she had been fiercely competitive, and her desire to become stronger had been great.

“At that time, I was irritated often, and I didn’t want to see people either. I didn’t show it outwardly, but my inner self was a complete mess.”

She looked at her face reflected in the sword. Her face from that time rose up, overlapping with it. That time when her makeup had grown thicker and thicker.

“Back then, the Young Cult Leader pierced through what had been so tightly blocked in one breath and raised the ceiling of my martial arts. Once the ceiling rose, every problem was solved in that instant. That was when I realized. All my problems had been because of my swordsmanship.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon regretted that he had not been able to help her then.

Thinking of it now, speaking with her comfortably like this and sharing their inner thoughts had not been such a difficult thing.

He only had to go to her first, apologize, and accept her heart.

Why had that been so difficult back then? Looking back now, it was truly nothing at all.

“But on this journey, the Young Cult Leader blew away that raised ceiling altogether. So I don’t need to face that man again.”

She looked up at the night sky.

“Because now, above my head, there is only the sky.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon understood what she meant. She intended to go all the way to the end with the sword.

“Is it a blessing? Or a curse?”

He had said it out of concern for her, but apparently it had been a blessing.

The One-Flower Sword Venerable smiled as she spoke.

“The Young Cult Leader blew away our ceilings, but in return he breathed youthful vitality into us as well, didn’t he? Let’s begin again.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon knew that her words about beginning again had to do with martial arts. But at least in that moment, he wondered what it would have been like if they had carried a different meaning.

He did not reveal those feelings, however. Though the ceiling had been pierced, she still kept an invisible barrier between herself and him. She was someone who stepped back one pace if he stepped closer by one.

“Don’t lose to that sword-wielding riffraff!”

And so the two Demon Venerables talked on, unaware that the night was deepening.

* * *

The next day as well, Geom Mugeuk wandered through the mountains.

He emitted spiderweb-like energy from his body to search for any other presence, and when he stood somewhere with a clear view, he examined the cliffs across from him with the Divine Eye Art.

It was bleak and exhausting work, but Geom Mugeuk silently searched for the entrance.

“How do you like it? Is the view good there?”

On the sunny hillside halfway up the mountain across from where Geom Mugeuk was looking, he could see the grave of the One-Flower Sword Venerable’s maternal uncle.

“You know, don’t you? That’s quite an auspicious site too. Since ancient times, an auspicious site should have a mountain behind it and water flowing before it…”

Among the books he had read at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s house, there had been one on feng shui. Just as he recalled that passage from the book—

A thought struck Geom Mugeuk, and his eyes widened.

“Ah, that’s right!”

Geom Mugeuk immediately soared into the air.

“…the mountain ridges to the left and right should seem to embrace the auspicious site, the wind should be hidden, and the water should curve. The soil should be firm and not damp, and should be red or golden in hue.”

That was the content about auspicious sites he had read at the time.

“I have to find an auspicious site!”

No matter how important secrecy was, it was still the tomb of the former Sado Alliance Leaders. When they first searched for a place, they must surely have chosen one according to feng shui.

It was obvious, yet he had failed to think of it until seeing the grave of the One-Flower Sword Venerable’s maternal uncle reminded him.

Geom Mugeuk flew among the many peaks of Cheonmoksan, searching for the place that might be the most auspicious.

And he found a place that fit.

“This is it!”

It was none other than Seoninbong, the Immortal Peak. It was located in what could be called the most auspicious site in Cheonmoksan.

Geom Mugeuk went straight below Immortal Peak. He could not know whether the tomb was here, but it would still be the wise choice to examine the most likely place first.

Geom Mugeuk climbed the mountain while examining his surroundings even more carefully.

As he climbed, he met an old man coming down. He was an herb gatherer carrying an herb bag.

“Good day.”

Geom Mugeuk lightly bowed his head in greeting as he passed.

The old man asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Are you perhaps a martial artist?”

“I am.”

“I was surprised because this is the first time a martial artist has greeted me first.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and cupped his fist to him.

“On behalf of all the rude martial artists until now, I apologize.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s unexpected response, the old man became flustered and waved his hands.

“There is no need for that. Your words alone make me embarrassed and grateful.”

Since they had exchanged greetings, the two stopped for a moment.

“The scenery here is truly beautiful.”

Beside them, a field spread out on a slant. It was wide open in every direction, and grasses grown up to an adult’s waist swayed in the wind.

“This is a place called Pungwon, the Wind Garden, because it is a place where one can play with the wind.”

“Play with the wind?”

Just then, a long blade of grass carried by the blowing wind fluttered along with it. It went this way, went that way, rose and fell; strangely, the wind blew from many directions.

“Isn’t it quite amusing?”

Had he not greeted the old man, he would not have learned the name or the place.

“Then go carefully.”

After saying farewell, the old man continued on his way. Watching his retreating figure, Geom Mugeuk looked toward the Wind Garden.

The blade of grass from a moment ago was still playing there like a child.

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to start climbing the mountain again, he suddenly recalled what Dan Sojin had said before they parted.

—Seeing you, Young Cult Leader, makes me think you are like the wind. I hope that from now on, you will continue living a wonderful life, entrusting yourself to the wind.

‘Could it be?’

Could it be that he had left him some clue?

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned once more toward the windswept field. A sloping field. There was no way the entrance to a tomb would be in a place like this.

If he had not met the old man, he would have passed this place without giving it much thought. It was far too open and exposed to contain anything secretive.

Was it because of Dan Sojin’s final words? For some reason, he felt drawn to it.

Watching the blade of grass entrust itself to the wind and fly this way and that, Geom Mugeuk too walked toward the place. He pushed his way into the grasses that rose up to his waist.

And just as Dan Sojin had said, he completely entrusted his body to the wind.

Whoooosh.

At the wind blowing from behind, he walked forward. When the wind changed and blew from the left, he walked to the right. Both his body and his steps were lighter than ever.

Then, when the wind stopped, Geom Mugeuk also stopped where he stood.

Entrusting one’s body to the wind! It was easy to say, but by no means easy to do. One had to feel the wind perfectly in order to move accurately. One had to stop precisely when it was time to stop, and immediately respond to wind blowing from another direction.

Geom Mugeuk moved exactly as the wind blew.

At this moment, Geom Mugeuk became one with the wind. It was as if he were dancing with it.

Geom Mugeuk continued to move. A thought arose that this madness had gone far enough, but he continued entrusting his body to the wind.

How long had he moved like that, following the wind?

When he stopped after following the final gust—

With a brief sound of wind, swish, Geom Mugeuk’s figure vanished. If someone had seen it, they might have thought he had collapsed into the grass.

But Geom Mugeuk was not there.

Geom Mugeuk did not know. If the place where he had moved this way and that were viewed from above, the flattened grasses had formed a single pattern.

It was a star with five points—the symbol of the Five-Point Wind Star Formation.

Whoooooosh.

At a single gust of wind, the grasses that had been lying flat instantly returned to normal, as if nothing had happened.

With the mystery gone, only the blade of grass from before fluttered in the wind.

Chapter 763: Hey, Long Time No See
----------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk stood in a new space.

In the darkness.

He activated his Divine Eye Art and looked around. He was standing before a staircase leading downward.

When he had entrusted his body to the wind, a wondrous formation had transported him here.

‘This is the place!’

Geom Mugeuk could tell that this was the entrance leading to the tombs of the past Alliance Lords.

‘Thank you, Family Head Dan.’

Dan Sojin truly had left a clue in his farewell. He could not break his family law, but he must have hoped for it—that the dangerous object would be taken away from the tomb.

Of course, he likely had not thought Geom Mugeuk would actually find this place.

The location of this place was, in every sense, a strategy of emptiness and reality.

If it were a secret cemetery, one would naturally assume the entrance was hidden deep somewhere in rugged mountains.

Who would ever imagine that the entrance was hidden in such an open, unobstructed place?

And Geom Mugeuk was sincerely impressed by the formation.

A formation one could enter only by fully entrusting one’s body to the wind?

Truly, it was the first time he had experienced a formation that carried such elegance and refined taste.

Whoever had made it, at least one thing was certain. The person who had created this formation in the past must have been called a master of formations. He was likely someone known and respected by all who learned and used formations even now.

At that, Geom Mugeuk grew tense inside. Down below, formations made by such a person would be waiting.

Geom Mugeuk carefully began descending the stairs.

Already, he could feel the mysteriousness exuded by the space itself. It was an energy that could not be compared to the fake Sword Tomb where he had fought the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

The farther he went down the stairs, the brighter his surroundings became. And at the bottom of the stairs, a long corridor awaited him; at its far end was another staircase leading underground.

‘The first trial!’

Geom Mugeuk examined the walls on both sides of the corridor. Countless holes dotted the floor, walls, and ceiling. The moment he set foot in that corridor, mechanisms would activate, and hidden weapons or blades would spring out. Fortunately, it was not a formation but a mechanical trap.

After carefully inspecting the mechanism, Geom Mugeuk made his decision.

‘I’ll break through with Swift Step.’

He had no intention of damaging the mechanism out of some needless desire to show off his skill. After all, he had come here as a quiet guest. He intended to leave without anyone knowing he had ever visited.

After calming his mind with a deep breath, Geom Mugeuk launched himself forward. He, whose speed had recently reached an extreme, now moved with full intent.

Shiiiiik!

At the same time, the mechanisms all reacted at once.

Shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk!

In a flash, Geom Mugeuk landed at the end of the corridor. His breathing was not disturbed. He had arrived before he could even take a single breath.

The corridor he had passed through had hundreds of spearheads jutting out from all directions like a spiderweb before returning to their original positions. Behind the charging Geom Mugeuk, the spearheads had followed like waves, like flames. But they could not catch up to lightning.

Sreung, seureureung, seureureung.

Geom Mugeuk’s breakthrough had exceeded the highest speed of an intruder imagined by the person who had made the mechanism. No matter how great an expert was, surely he could not be faster than this. But Geom Mugeuk was faster still.

In every sense, Geom Mugeuk’s movement art had surpassed even the imagination of the mechanism’s creator.

Having passed through without leaving a single scratch on the trap, Geom Mugeuk descended the stairs again.

The second underground floor.

There, the second trial awaited.

The second trial that greeted Geom Mugeuk was far more dangerous than the previous one. Of course, that was if its target had not been Geom Mugeuk.

The moment he stepped off the last stair, Geom Mugeuk could feel it.

A single strand of poison entering with his breath.

‘Formless Poison!’

It had made anyone who came this far focus on what might be waiting in the passage ahead, then poisoned them at the end of the stairs. Quietly, without letting them know where or how the poison had been administered.

This trial, which used extremely expensive Formless Poison, was saying this:

Just die here.

Even an expert who safely passed the first trial would not have been able to survive here.

Geom Mugeuk lightly broke through the first and second trials, then descended the stairs again.

The third underground floor.

At the third trial, a formation activated.

Ssssss.

The place where Geom Mugeuk stood changed. His surroundings turned into a desolate field, and around him, illusions like pitch-black shadows began to appear. Illusions wielding all manner of weapons rushed toward Geom Mugeuk.

As if wondering what this was supposed to mean, Geom Mugeuk tilted his head.

Swaaaaaak.

Geom Mugeuk cut down the illusions charging at him from every direction. An immense number rushed in, but Geom Mugeuk was no longer who he once had been.

Sword energy that flew from the Black Demon Sword swept through them. Even when using the same internal energy, the number he killed was different.

The illusions continued charging over the corpses. It was a number that an ordinary expert’s internal energy could never endure, but the person who had created this formation had also miscalculated the numbers. Surely there was no one who could withstand this many enemies? There was.

Puaaaak!

When Geom Mugeuk cut down the final illusion, the formation was broken.

Sssssss.

In every sense, Geom Mugeuk’s ability overwhelmed the trials. The three trials each demanded speed, resistance to poison, and internal energy beyond human limits, and Geom Mugeuk was like the natural enemy of these trials.

Yes, it could not be helped. They would never have imagined that someone this strong would enter.

When he broke the third trial, an enormous stone gate appeared before his eyes.

He had finally reached the tomb.

When Geom Mugeuk opened the stone gate and entered, a magnificent interior revealed itself.

The incomparably high ceiling overwhelmed all who stepped inside, and stone statues stood in every direction.

There was a statue depicting a martial artist wearing a sword, and another statue holding a saber. One statue had its fist thrust forward, while another was seated in a grand armchair.

From their dignified appearances, every one of them, he could tell that they were the past Lords of the Sado Alliance.

And murals were painted on the walls in every direction.

Had an Orthodox-Unorthodox Great War broken out?

Flames were blazing, and corpses filled every side. In the midst of them, a martial artist stood tall. He too was likely one of the past Lords of the Sado Alliance.

And in the center was a display cabinet, where the legacies left behind by the Lords of the Sado Alliance had been placed. There were martial arts manuals, and there were also divine weapons and rare armaments they had used.

They were the martial arts learned and weapons used by Alliance Lords of each era, so one did not need to look to know how precious they were.

And among them was that thing.

Placed atop a snow-white cloth, it was surely the final purple bead, the Jajeong.

“Our youngest is over there!”

Geom Mugeuk strode toward it.

Then he picked up the Jajeong and silently looked down at it.

The sixth and final bead.

To obtain the energy of this bead, those behind the scenes had made every effort imaginable.

Geom Mugeuk looked down at it with a deepened gaze.

Crash.

Suddenly, he hurled the Jajeong he was holding to the floor.

The Jajeong shattered to pieces.

Sssssss.

Even as purple energy spread in every direction, Geom Mugeuk merely watched in silence.

It was then.

“Why are you doing this?”

A hidden door opened in the wall, and someone rushed out.

Amazingly, it was Dan Sojin, the head of the Baekchong Family, who managed this tomb. It seemed he had been hiding there, secretly watching what was happening inside.

“Was that bead not what you were searching for?”

“It was. I fought them because of this.”

“Then why did you break it?”

Looking down at the shattered Jajeong, Geom Mugeuk calmly said,

“Because it was fake.”

Dan Sojin asked with a startled expression,

“It was fake?”

Geom Mugeuk silently nodded.

“How did you know it was fake?”

Geom Mugeuk quietly stared at him, then for some reason answered honestly.

“I obtained the other beads before this. Those beads were hidden in places difficult to find. But the bead here was displayed openly, as if to show it off. It was in the most conspicuous place.”

“You broke it for that reason alone? Could it not have been displayed openly for some other reason?”

“Of course, it wasn’t only because of that.”

When he had received the final bead, neither the secret coffer nor the energy within his body had reacted at all. Could that make sense? It was the final energy—there was no way there would be no reaction.

Shocking words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“This tomb here is fake as well.”

Dan Sojin wore an expression of disbelief.

“Fake? I am here, so what in the world are you saying?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Dan Sojin.

“It is fake because you are here.”

“!”

Dan Sojin’s expression hardened.

“What exactly do you mean?”

“The moment I saw you, I became certain.”

Dan Sojin did not hide his displeasure.

“Is this how you repay the grace I showed you?”

He was beyond dumbfounded, now enraged.

Geom Mugeuk’s calm explanation followed.

“The wind formation created at the entrance was an extraordinary formation.”

It truly had been the first time he had experienced such an incredible formation.

“And yet, compared to that, the formation inside was far too ordinary. The formation that brought me through the entrance was that remarkable, but the trials guarding the inside were merely things like these? Spearheads being fired, poison being released, illusions attacking within a formation. They were common mechanisms and formations one could see anywhere.”

Geom Mugeuk said as if concluding the matter,

“I thought that if it was the person who made the entrance formation, he would absolutely never have made such ordinary formations.”

That was also why Geom Mugeuk had tilted his head while breaking the formation of the final, third trial.

“So I thought about it. If he had truly made a formation here, what kind of formation would he have made? If someone were to enter this place, it would surely be because they were aiming for the legacies of the past Alliance Lords here. Then one legendary formation came to mind.”

One formation flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“The Wish-Fulfilling Illusory Formation!”

It was an ancient formation that allowed those trapped within it to obtain what they most longed for.

To someone who wanted treasure, it gave treasure; to someone who wanted martial arts manuals, it gave manuals.

But the moment they took possession of it, they would never escape the formation. As they rejoiced in obtaining it and wandered within the formation, their real body would eventually die from exhaustion of true qi.

There was only one way to break it.

The moment one obtained it, one had to reject it. Just as Geom Mugeuk had shattered the Jajeong earlier.

That method of breaking it was the most difficult method of all for human beings.

“I was already inside the formation from the first trial.”

From the moment he had first fallen into the darkness, the formation had already activated.

“A Linked Activation Formation.”

It was a level of skill befitting the person who had made the wind formation. Whoever he was, he was truly someone worthy of being called a god of formations.

“What is even more astonishing is that even though I rejected what I wanted, the formation was not broken.”

At that moment, Dan Sojin displayed an attitude different from before.

“Young Cult Leader, you truly are remarkable!”

As if all of this had been a test, he exclaimed in deep admiration.

He took another box from his robe and gave it to Geom Mugeuk.

“Here is the real bead. I apologize for testing you, Young Cult Leader. I wanted to confirm whether you could break the Wish-Fulfilling Illusory Formation.”

Geom Mugeuk received the box and opened it. Inside was a purple bead that shone even more brilliantly than the one from before.

“Truly remarkable.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s admiration, Dan Sojin asked with a curious expression,

“Is that bead truly so remarkable? What role does it play, exactly?”

“I wasn’t talking about the bead.”

Geom Mugeuk picked up the bead.

Crash.

This time as well, he shattered it without hesitation.

Dan Sojin stared at Geom Mugeuk in shock.

“He created not only a linked formation, but also a formation that requires a double breaking.”

“A double breaking? What does that mean?”

Then Geom Mugeuk revealed a shocking truth.

“It is you. You are part of the formation, and you are the second method of breaking it, arranged to prevent the formation from being broken.”

“!”

“It even seems that you yourself do not know you are playing such a role.”

Dan Sojin shouted angrily,

“What kind of mad nonsense is that?”

Then Geom Mugeuk abruptly asked,

“What kind of person are you?”

“What?”

“Try to recall what kind of person you are.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk asked again.

“You are a loyal person. Someone who would willingly give his life to protect this tomb. Those final words you gave me were the best you could do, and the utmost consideration you could offer. You must have done it after making a truly great resolve. And yet you would wait for me here? You found that hard-to-find bead and kept it in your robe, then tested me? In this sacred tomb that you value more than your life? Are you that kind of person?”

At that question, Dan Sojin became confused.

“I… I am not that kind of person.”

The moment he admitted it,

Ssssss!

Dan Sojin’s form began to disappear. He truly had been the second method of breaking the formation. If someone else had broken through to this point, the method of breaking it would not have appeared as Dan Sojin, but as someone else with whom that person had formed a bond.

A person who entered this place not only had to pass the three trials within the formation, but also had to reject what they desperately desired.

Even if they rejected it, they then had to reject a second temptation as well.

Ultimately, it was a formation that could never be broken by anyone who had intruded with any greed from the start.

Thud.

At that moment, a single booklet fell where Dan Sojin had disappeared.

—Heavenly Qi Compendium of Peerless Formations.

Geom Mugeuk opened the booklet and saw these words written inside.

—I leave the enlightenment of my final years to the later generation who has broken my linked formation. Gui Xuzi.

Geom Mugeuk did not know of Gui Xuzi. But if he was someone who had created at least these two formations, then was this secret manual he left behind not the Nine-Flame Demonic Art of the formation world?

Geom Mugeuk carefully tucked the booklet into his robe. He considered obtaining such a fortuitous encounter at a time like this to be entirely the will of the heavens.

“I will use it so that it may help the world.”

Only then did the formation begin to vanish.

Srrrk.

The surroundings around Geom Mugeuk changed. The place that had been the inside of the tomb disappeared, and he was back in the darkness where Geom Mugeuk had first fallen.

And before his eyes, he saw an enormous stone gate.

As if to prove that it was the entrance to the true tomb, the face of the wolf—the guardian statue standing on the path to the Sado Alliance Lord’s Hall—was carved at the center of the stone gate.

It looked incomparably valiant, as if guarding the souls of the previous Alliance Lords.

“Hey, it’s been a while.”

Geom Mugeuk pushed the door with both hands.

Grrrrrr.

It was the moment when the many-layered history of the Sado martial world opened. Even the scent of time was different from before.

Chapter 764: Cry Louder
-----------------------
A vast space unfolded before his eyes.

Its overwhelming scale could not be compared to the fake tomb he had seen earlier within the formation.

A place where the leaders of the Alliance throughout history came to rest forever after death.

Geom Mugeuk stood on the boundary between the living and the dead and greeted them in a loud voice.

“I am Geom Mugeuk, Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Though the path I walk differs from yours, I revere the great lives of my seniors, who protected what they held dear with a single sword. In the murim of this era, there is a force attempting to disturb the order of the Evil, Demonic, and Orthodox paths and seize control of the murim. I have come here to stop them, so I ask that you forgive this junior’s unpermitted steps today.”

After Geom Mugeuk bowed his head respectfully and paid his respects, he stepped inside. Then he added a remark very much like himself.

“I like and respect the current Sado Alliance Leader. And I’m friends with the next Alliance Leader! So please look kindly upon me!”

Geom Mugeuk stood at the entrance for a moment, overwhelmed by the power of the long, ancient years, and looked around the hall. The scent of incense seeped through the dry, heavy air.

The interior was vast, but he thought he would have enough time to look around. Dan Sojin would not be able to come and go from this place freely either. He, too, would likely only be able to enter on fixed dates.

But what mattered was not time. If he was fated to find it, he would do so even in half a shichen; if he was not, then even ten years in this place would not be enough.

He could only hope that the final bead was fated to him.

The interior had been built in a huge circular shape, and in the center at the rear stood a stone statue of a martial artist seated in a grand preceptor’s chair.

Though it was only a stone statue, a powerful authority could be felt from it. It seemed likely to be the figure who had founded the Sado Alliance. Blood jade had been set into both of its eyes, making it frightening just to meet its gaze.

Before it stood an altar where rites could be offered, and around that again, dozens of pillars had been erected.

Writing was inscribed on each pillar, records of the Alliance Leaders throughout history.

One pillar for one person’s life.

Where and when they were born, what martial arts they learned, how they became the Sado Alliance Leader, what they accomplished after taking the position, and when and how they died—all of it was recorded there.

Each and every one of those pillars was the history of the Sado Alliance.

Geom Mugeuk slowly read the contents.

Reading someone’s life, especially words left behind like a record, was an extremely tedious thing.

But Geom Mugeuk knew. There was always something to learn from another person’s choices. Life was not long enough to make all those choices oneself.

And so Geom Mugeuk read through the most important choices the Alliance Leaders had made throughout their lives, and the results of those choices.

When he finished reading one person’s life, he read the life on the next pillar, and then the life of another Alliance Leader.

He laughed, and he frowned. There were Alliance Leaders who pleased him, and there were also Alliance Leaders who made him wonder how such a person could possibly exist.

As he continued reading the pillars, one name caught Geom Mugeuk’s eye.

Ju Gasin.

It was him—the foremost master of the Sado path and the Sado Alliance Leader from three hundred years ago.

When the sword tomb beneath the lake where he had fought the Twelve Zodiac Kings earlier had also become known as Ju Gasin’s tomb, masters had once gathered there in the past.

But his true remains had been enshrined here.

Geom Mugeuk read Ju Gasin’s life. How he learned martial arts, how he became the foremost sword of the Sado path, and how he became the Sado Alliance Leader.

—After becoming Alliance Leader, he always kept a wolf called Jeokrang, the Red Wolf, by his side.

A Sado Alliance Leader who traveled with a wolf? Somehow, it struck him as impressive.

Then, among Ju Gasin’s deeds, one passage seized his gaze.

—Together with the Muhwang Divine Sword, he defeated Cheonui Palace.

‘Ah, so Ju Gasin was there too!’

That day three hundred years ago, during the final bloody battle between the Murim Alliance and Cheonui Palace, it had not only been the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult that came. The Sado Alliance had intervened in that fight as well. And the one who had done so personally was Ju Gasin, the Sado Alliance Leader of that time.

It was clear that the final bead being kept here was no coincidence, but the continuation of that destiny from three hundred years ago.

Had he not read the contents of this pillar in detail, he would never have known.

Geom Mugeuk read all the remaining pillars as well. There was still no pillar for Baek Jagang. After his death, his pillar would surely be erected somewhere here too.

‘Will my name be able to appear as one line in the Alliance Leader’s history?’

—Together with the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, he destroyed the lineage of Cheonui Palace!

I hope so, Alliance Leader.

After reading the history of all the pillars, Geom Mugeuk moved toward the place where the legacies of the past Alliance Leaders were kept.

The unique secret manuals they had used were placed upright in display cases. They were truly divine arts and supreme techniques that would throw the murim into an uproar if even a single volume were to leak out.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk passed by without even opening them. What interested him were the objects displayed in front of them. He wondered if the bead might be mixed in among them.

All sorts of weapons, protective armor, gauntlets and hidden weapons, even the clothes and shoes the Alliance Leaders had enjoyed wearing. All manner of objects were displayed there.

Since there was nothing written to say what each item was or to whom it had belonged, he could not tell who had left which object behind.

Geom Mugeuk did not touch the objects either.

No one was watching, but he maintained the courtesy he ought to preserve as much as possible. He merely looked closely from nearby in case the bead was mixed in among them.

However, even after he examined all the objects, the Bigwe did not resonate.

‘Right. They wouldn’t have hidden it this easily.’

Thinking back on what he had obtained until now, it would not be easy to find. He had obtained the Black Essence from So Baekta, who had become the next leader of the Pungcheon Cult, by saying, I’ll choose this.

At a glance, it seemed he had obtained it far too easily, but if one considered the process that had allowed him to say those words, it had never been easy at all.

This time, Geom Mugeuk walked toward the surrounding wall.

Stone statues of martial artists stood along the wall, swords planted against the floor, as if guarding something.

Murals had been painted on that wall. They depicted various scenes of people who appeared to be former Sado Alliance Leaders.

There were scenes of them fighting bloody battles while drenched in blood, shouting with banners raised on battlefields piled with corpses, and splitting cliffs with swords.

There were scenes of them running while carrying children, and even scenes of them brutally killing someone. All manner of varied images were painted there.

Countless paintings covered a large portion of the broad wall, making the mere sight of them magnificent.

The stone statues stood encircling the murals at regular intervals.

There were far too many of them to say they had simply been placed here as symbols guarding the tomb. Moreover, the distance between each statue was precise, without even a hair’s breadth of error.

‘Who are you standing there to protect?’

Geom Mugeuk placed his hand on the wall between two of the statues. The wall was so thick that even a master could not know what lay beyond it, but Geom Mugeuk, whose martial arts had reached the pinnacle, could sense what was past that thick wall.

‘There is a space behind the wall.’

Geom Mugeuk looked at the stone statues standing all around the circular wall.

‘So the coffins of the Alliance Leaders are indeed kept between each of these statues!’

These stone statues were a kind of guardian statue protecting each Alliance Leader’s coffin.

Even if he tried to open it, there was no door in the wall. There was not even a trace that could be called a door. They must have made it so that it could be opened and closed, so there had to be some unknown, intricate mechanism.

‘What if the bead is inside someone’s coffin?’

That would be a truly difficult situation.

Finding the final bead was more important than anything, but even so, he could not break the wall and open a coffin.

Not because of Dan Sojin, but because of Baek Jagang and Bi Sain, he could not do such a thing.

‘Please, let it not be hidden in there.’

Geom Mugeuk circled along the round wall and examined the guardian statues first.

‘Please resonate, Bigwe! Let the statue’s eyes glow with purple light and pop out, let that ornament hanging from the sword hilt drop off with a click!’

But that was only Geom Mugeuk’s wish. The Bigwe did not resonate even by the time he had examined all the guardian statues.

He examined the stone statue seated in the grand preceptor’s chair as well, and even flew into the air to inspect the ceiling, but he could not find the bead.

‘Could it not be in this tomb?’

No, that could not be.

—If the Sado Alliance Leader does not die, the murim will be destroyed.

It was something for which even a prophecy had been handed down.

They had clearly intended to kill the Sado Alliance Leader of this era, discover the location of this place, and take the bead.

‘It is definitely inside here.’

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned once more to the walls between the statues.

‘Those are the only places left now.’

There had to be a mechanism somewhere that opened the door. If not, he would cut the wall without leaving a visible trace if he had to—

The moment his thoughts reached that point, Geom Mugeuk realized something. Unlike himself, he was being far too impatient. Even now, without realizing it, he had clenched his fist tightly. Because it was the last remaining bead, his heart was hurrying.

‘The beads until now weren’t things I obtained by trying to forcefully seize them.’

Geom Mugeuk opened his fist. He had to open his hand if he was going to grasp the bead with it.

Geom Mugeuk flopped down on the spot.

“Aigoo, Alliance Leaders. This unworthy junior will rest for a moment. Demonic Cult bastards are naturally this impatient. Please understand.”

Geom Mugeuk’s voice echoed through the hall. Sitting there like that, Baek Jagang suddenly came to mind.

He missed the man who had looked at him with those small eyes. Alliance Leader, please come and find it for me!

“When time passes, you too will rest here, Alliance Leader.”

Baek Jagang’s figure would also be painted on the mural.

Much later, would Bi Sain be painted there too? Once he left this place, he would never be able to return, so it was a painting he would never see.

“If I really miss you too much, I might sneak in here.”

Imagining Bi Sain arguing about why he only pictured the case where Bi Sain died first, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

Regaining his composure like that, he looked around. Geom Mugeuk gazed at the murals. Until just a moment ago, because he had been searching for the bead, he had paid attention only to the vibration of the Bigwe rather than to the paintings.

‘Ah, Ju Gasin must be somewhere in these paintings too.’

He might not know the others, but he wanted to find Ju Gasin. After all, he was the only person here he knew. If the bead had been hidden in this place, it would surely have followed his will.

But because he could not tell who the Alliance Leaders in the paintings were, it was impossible to find Ju Gasin.

The objects were the same, and so were the secret manuals. Not a single name was written down. What an unfriendly Sado Alliance.

As Geom Mugeuk sat there appreciating the murals, something entered his sight.

It was a painting of a wolf with magnificent red fur.

“Hey, you’re really handsome!”

Just as he was about to pass it by, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back to the wolf.

At the same time, a single line came to mind.

—Even after becoming Alliance Leader, he kept a wolf called Jeokrang, the Red Wolf, by his side.

If it was Jeokrang, then it was a red-furred wolf!

Geom Mugeuk sprang to his feet and approached the painting.

After studying the wolf in the painting closely, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes widened.

The wolf wore magnificent armor around its neck and body, and in the center of the armor encircling its neck, a bead was embedded.

‘It’s a blue bead!’

The bead he had obtained from the steel wolf statue at the Sado Alliance before had been the Blue Essence.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Alliance Leader standing beside the wolf.

A middle-aged man was depicted wearing his formal robes properly, dignified as if setting out to war. A martial artist who suited the wolf perfectly.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes widened even more. A bead was embedded in the belt around the man’s waist, and its color was purple.

‘This man is Ju Gasin!’

The bead on the wolf’s armor being blue could have been a coincidence, but even the bead on this waist ornament could not be a coincidence.

‘That day, Ju Gasin obtained two beads.’

So he hid one in the steel wolf statue, and the other? Where did you put the other one?

Geom Mugeuk threw himself back toward the place where the relics had been gathered and searched for the belt Ju Gasin had been wearing.

“So it was here!”

There was no bead embedded in that belt. But Geom Mugeuk sensed it. From this belt, he could feel some inexplicable profound energy.

“Pardon me for a moment, Alliance Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk carefully picked up the belt.

“Perhaps we might have met that day.”

But the Bigwe had sent him back to reality before that battle three hundred years ago. If he had been there, he might truly have met him.

After looking down at the belt in silence, Geom Mugeuk fastened it around his own waist. However, nothing happened.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back to the painting.

‘Could it be?’

Geom Mugeuk slowly walked over and stood beside the steel wolf statue. He stood exactly as Ju Gasin did in the painting.

Sssrk.

It was at the very moment he felt the metal ornament of the belt being drawn toward the steel wolf statue by magnetism.

A clacking sound seemed to ring out from within the wolf statue.

Srrrng!

One section of wall between the guardian statues on the far side swung wide open. What slid out from the opened door was a coffin. Both the door and the coffin that moved forward had been made with an extraordinarily intricate mechanism.

Geom Mugeuk instinctively knew.

‘It’s Ju Gasin’s coffin!’

Charrrng.

The panel covering the coffin slid smoothly aside and down, opening wide.

With trembling eyes, Geom Mugeuk walked toward it.

The moment he looked inside the coffin, his chest was swept up in emotion.

Ju Gasin’s white bones lay within the coffin. The skeleton had both hands gathered over its chest, and between those two hands lay a single bead.

The final bead he had sought for so long, the Purple Essence.

The bead, revealing itself to the world after three hundred years, freely emitted a purple radiance.

Wooooong.

The Bigwe in his chest began to vibrate. It was stronger than any vibration he had felt until now.

“Resonate louder, Bigwe!”

At the same time, the five energies that had been sleeping within Geom Mugeuk’s body awakened all at once.

“We finally found the youngest!”

Chapter 765: A Fragrant Scent Instead of the Smell of Blood
-----------------------------------------------------------
