Geom Mugeuk bowed his head respectfully, paying his respects to the white bones of Ju Gasin, who had been the leader of the Sado Alliance three hundred years ago.

“Three hundred years have passed since the era in which you were active, Alliance Leader. I cannot know what happened at the time, but I believe these orbs were divided and safeguarded for the sake of peace in the murim.”

Of course, it might not have been for the sake of peace in the murim that the orbs had been divided. There must have been entanglements, each side keeping the others in check.

“I will be sure to protect the peace that you and the other seniors sought to defend with these orbs.”

Geom Mugeuk carefully lifted the purple orb resting in the skeletal hand. The moment he held the Purple Crystal, the Bi-gwe that had been vibrating continuously within his robes began to tremble even more fiercely.

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head to the white bones.

“Thank you.”

As if it had been waiting for that, the coffin lid closed on its own.

Clatter.

Then it returned to the place where the original coffin had been stored, and the secret door closed. Not even a trace of a door remained on the wall between the guardian stone statues.

Geom Mugeuk took the Bi-gwe out from his robes. More than at any other time when it had absorbed the energy of an orb, Geom Mugeuk was tense.

The Bi-gwe was smiling as always. It felt as if the day he had drawn that smiling face on it had been only yesterday.

“Bi-gwe, it’s the youngest!”

Wooooong.

He placed the purple orb atop the vibrating Bi-gwe.

Srrrrrk.

The Purple Crystal was sucked into the Bi-gwe as though a soft liquid were being absorbed.

Woom!

After vibrating powerfully once, the Bi-gwe rose into the air. Normally, it would spin in midair, but today was different.

Shaaaaaaa!

A purple radiance burst from the Bi-gwe.

At that, the five energies awakened within his body also began to emit their own colors all at once. The five energies became light, piercing through Geom Mugeuk’s body and pouring outward.

Six colors of light churned through the air.

It looked as though six dragons were coiling around Geom Mugeuk.

Had anyone seen it, they would have thought it a dream—a truly fantastical sight.

The six energies swirling around Geom Mugeuk gathered into the Bi-gwe.

Shoooooook!

Having absorbed the six energies, the Bi-gwe still floated in the air.

Shaaaaaaa.

The Bi-gwe expelled the six energies, now intertwined like skeins of thread, toward Geom Mugeuk.

Like sword qi being unleashed, the lights stretched out in a straight line.

When Geom Mugeuk had accepted all of that energy, he saw it.

Sssssss.

The Bi-gwe, having completed all of its duties, was vanishing from the air like smoke.

‘No, Bi-gwe!’

Until the very last moment, the Bi-gwe was looking at him and smiling. Moments of the conversations he had shared with it came to mind, and Geom Mugeuk’s chest tightened. He did not want to part with it like this, but if this was the fate between him and the Bi-gwe—

‘Bi-gwe, thank you for everything until now.’

With a bright smile, the Bi-gwe disappeared. It was a farewell to the friend who had been with him all this time.

The Bi-gwe had vanished, but the six energies it had expelled were racing through Geom Mugeuk’s body.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa!

The six energies, finally gathered in full.

Geom Mugeuk could instinctively feel just how tremendous the potential of this power was. Its very nature was fundamentally different from ordinary elixirs. No, to begin with, it was not something that should be called an elixir at all.

Geom Mugeuk began to circulate the mental cultivation method he had obtained from the large Bi-gwe. The very method he had acquired when he returned to three hundred years in the past.

—In moving true qi, one must be as free as the wind, yet as heavy as the earth. One must surge hotly like fire, yet be able to stop like ice. One must be as deep as darkness and as powerful as lightning. If you can unite these six energies as one, you will be able to possess the greatest power of all ages.

The six energies that had entered him entwined as one scattered all at once, each taking a different meridian.

The true qi moved along paths he had never imagined. It flowed in reverse, and it moved along routes that, by common sense, seemed certain to cause internal injury. It was a mental cultivation method that shattered in an instant all the martial principles he had known.

After circling through the meridians of his entire body along new paths, the six mysteries gathered at his dantian in the final moment.

The very instant the mental cultivation method ended and the energies merged into one—

Flash!

Radiance burst from Geom Mugeuk’s entire body. The light was so intense that even the Divine Eye Art could not look upon it.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk felt a refreshing energy he had never experienced in his life.

If he had to choose one word to describe the sensation, it would be this:

Mystery.

The energy that had merged into one did not disappear, but instead enveloped his dantian.

Was that why? A mysterious energy flowed together with the inner power emerging from his dantian.

‘My inner power has completely changed.’

To describe that change merely by saying it had become purer was far too inadequate.

It was then.

He felt as if his whole body were floating up into the air, and dizziness washed over him. It was likely because of this new energy in his dantian.

Geom Mugeuk sat down on the spot and assumed the lotus position.

He tried to calm his mind and stabilize his body, but he still felt dazed.

How should he describe this feeling?

It was like standing in place and watching a gigantic tidal wave surge toward him from far away. He should flee, yet he could only stare blankly. He wanted to move, but his body would not budge.

He felt his heartbeat gradually slowing, but his body did not respond. The Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art remained still, and his instinct for danger did not react.

‘What on earth is about to happen?’

This was truly the first time his body had ever been in such a state. Had he perhaps been afflicted by poison or a soul-bewitching art? Impossible. It was certainly a side effect of the energy he had accepted.

The moment the gigantic tidal wave in his mind crashed over Geom Mugeuk—

Crack!

“Urgh!”

A short scream burst from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth. He was a man who would not so much as blink at most pain, yet his face was already twisted in agony.

Crack-crack-crack.

From Geom Mugeuk’s body came again the sound of bones snapping.

“Uuuugh!”

Geom Mugeuk screamed. The bones throughout his body began to break one after another. Something absurd was beginning to happen.

He tried to raise his inner power to stop it, but the energy inside him would not move. It felt as though this were not his body.

The energy surrounding his dantian!

‘Was this energy actually a trap?’

Had it been an arrangement meant to harm him?

No, that could not be. There was no way a trap would have been laid so difficult to discover.

But then why on earth was this happening?

Crack-crack-crack.

“Aaaaaaargh!”

The bones inside his body moved on their own.

Crack-crack-crack! Crunch-crunch-crunch!

The muscles and tendons attached to the bones stretched, then contracted again.

“Aaaaaaaaaaah!”

It was pain no human could endure.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk was dreaming.

The ground was full of blood. Looking around, he saw that he was in front of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Those collapsed while bleeding were the guards of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Among them was Hwi. Hwi, who had blocked the doorway, was dead while sitting propped against the wall. His eyes had not even closed.

‘Uncle.’

Geom Mugeuk knew instinctively.

‘It’s that day.’

But before his regression, he had never made it this far on that day. Before then, he had been struck down by Hwa Mugi and brought to the brink of death.

That was why the scene before him now was an imagination he had created.

After closing Hwi’s eyes, Geom Mugeuk opened the doors of the Heavenly Demon Hall and stepped inside.

Splash.

Blood was pooled thickly across the floor. The Path of Blood had become a true path of blood.

Far in the distance, he could see the grand preceptor’s chair. Instead of his father, who had always sat there, another person was seated upon it. On the floor before it, his father sat facing this way. Geom Mugeuk instinctively knew that his father had already passed away.

‘Father!’

He knew it was a dream. He knew it was a scene created by his own unconscious mind. Even so, Geom Mugeuk felt as if his heart were being torn apart.

Hwa Mugi sat upon the grand preceptor’s chair, looking this way.

That arrogant, lofty gaze was exactly the gaze from the day he had killed him.

Geom Mugeuk knelt before his father.

He did not know what kind of battle had taken place that day, but—

“This time will be different, Father.”

At that moment, Hwa Mugi extended his hand.

Whoosh.

Geom Mugeuk’s body flew helplessly into his grasp. It was Void Seizing of tremendous power.

Hwa Mugi asked in a cold voice.

“What will be different?”

Looking at him, Geom Mugeuk said calmly,

“You’ll find out later.”

A sneer seemed to form at the corner of Hwa Mugi’s mouth.

Then, without hesitation, he broke Geom Mugeuk’s neck.

Crack.

“Aaaaaaaaaaah!”

Screaming, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes flew open.

Crack-crack-crack! Crack!

Inside the tomb, lying on the cold floor, the bones within his body were still searching for their new positions.

* * *

How much time had passed?

When Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes, he felt heat blazing throughout his entire body. Truly, he had never been in such pain before.

But the changes within his body continued.

If earlier his bones had been completely reconstructed, this time it was his flesh.

Riiiiip.

His muscles tore, and new muscles regenerated.

Riiiip! Rip!

Geom Mugeuk had truly endured the agony of bones being carved away, but the pain of muscles tearing was likewise a pain difficult for any human to bear. Even so, he clenched his teeth and endured.

He lost consciousness, then came to again, only to lose consciousness once more.

Even while that happened, the old house was being demolished and a new house was being built.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa.

The true qi that exploded from his dantian raced through the meridians of his entire body. Every blocked meridian was opened. Every meridian through which true qi flowed became wider and sturdier. It was a transformation of the meridians different from the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art.

When all the flesh and meridians inside his body had been reconstructed, another change occurred.

Every pore in his skin opened, and the turbid qi and impurities accumulated in his body began to be expelled.

An absolutely unbearable stench wafted out. It was a foul odor he could hardly believe had come from his own body.

And that was not all.

All the hair on his body fell out and began to grow anew. His skin peeled away, and new skin formed. It was molting.

Geom Mugeuk could not tell whether this was a dream or reality. In truth, he continued to dream.

Splash.

Once again, Geom Mugeuk entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

As expected, the floor was thick with blood.

The moment he looked at the grand preceptor’s chair, Geom Mugeuk was startled.

The person seated there was not Hwa Mugi, but his father.

And below him sat Hwa Mugi.

Hwa Mugi was dead, seated in the spot where his father had been in the earlier dream.

When Geom Mugeuk looked up with a joyful expression, his father on the grand preceptor’s chair spoke.

“Now only the unification of the murim remains.”

The moment he heard those words, Geom Mugeuk woke from sleep and his eyes flew open.

He was still lying on the floor of the tomb.

Geom Mugeuk heard it. The sound of his own blood flowing through his veins. It was truly a strange experience. He heard the beating of his heart, and vividly felt the touch of the air brushing against his body. Smells he had not sensed inside the tomb stabbed into his nose.

Amid that overwhelming flood of senses, Geom Mugeuk lost consciousness again.

How much more time had passed?

This time, Geom Mugeuk was standing among countless stars. In the deep darkness, he saw an illusion of innumerable glittering stars passing him by.

The largest star flew toward Geom Mugeuk.

The moment that star collided with him, Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes.

Geom Mugeuk could tell that he was now fully awake. Just how many days had passed?

Geom Mugeuk slowly raised himself. The maddening pain had vanished as if it had been a lie. The five senses that had thrown him into chaos had also settled.

‘I feel like I could fly away.’

His body was truly light. This must be what people meant when they said something was as light as a feather.

But soon, he frowned at the foul odor coming from his body.

His body was covered in filthy grime.

Snap.

Geom Mugeuk immediately opened the Space-Time Transference Art.

It was that very place in the snowy field where a warm hot spring lay.

Geom Mugeuk flung off his clothes and threw himself into the hot spring.

Splash!

Geom Mugeuk washed his body clean in the warm water.

Once was not enough, so he created another new hot spring and washed again.

After finishing his bath so thoroughly, he stepped out of the hot spring and snapped his fingers once more.

A large bronze mirror appeared before Geom Mugeuk, large enough to reflect his entire body.

As Geom Mugeuk looked at his face and body, his expression filled with astonishment.

His body had changed.

It was not drastically different from before, but his frame had clearly changed. His shoulders, arms, and legs—their breadth, thickness, and length—had changed.

His skin was clean. Not a single trace remained of the wounds he had suffered before.

When he touched it, it was smooth as jade and soft as a baby’s skin.

But Geom Mugeuk could feel it. The muscles beneath this skin were incomparably stronger than before. This skin, too, was no ordinary skin.

And that was not all. His already keen eyesight had grown even sharper, while his hearing, smell, and touch had become far more sensitive. To exaggerate only slightly, if he focused, he felt he could hear even the sound of ants crawling far away.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk was certain.

‘I truly underwent Bone-Exchange Rebirth (換骨奪胎)!’

The moment his bones twisted, he had thought perhaps that might be the case.

But Bone-Exchange Rebirth was the greatest of all fortuitous encounters a martial artist could experience.

He had wondered whether such a miracle could really have come to him.

What he had undergone was a transformation in which his old body died and he was reborn in a new one, pain endured for the sake of perfection.

With a trembling heart, Geom Mugeuk picked up the Black Demon Sword lying on the floor and drew it.

Shiiik, shiiik!

Geom Mugeuk swung his sword within the Space-Time Transposition Art.

The sensation of swinging the sword had changed completely. Until now, he had thought his body was optimized for martial arts, but that had been a mistake—an arrogance.

‘It feels even better!’

His body had changed into one perfect for unleashing martial arts.

Geom Mugeuk’s face seemed to brighten radiantly, and then his laughter rang out thunderously through the world within the Space-Time Transposition Art.

His joy was not merely because he would now be able to face Hwa Mugi better.

A perfect body, new senses, and even a clearer mind.

It was because he had drawn closer to perfection as a martial artist.

Geom Mugeuk, too, was a martial artist. Born with the Heavenly Martial Physique, he was truly a martial artist among martial artists.

Killing Hwa Mugi was not the whole of his life. No matter how difficult a mountain’s summit was to conquer, he was not going to live there forever once he reached it.

The gift bestowed by the energy of the secret coffer was not limited to Bone-Exchange Rebirth. The energy of the secret coffer, still merged into one, was wrapped around his dantian, and a wondrous energy lingered within his internal power.

Shwaaaaaak.

Geom Mugeuk unleashed the Nine Flames Demonic Art. Was it because of the mysterious energy carried within his internal power?

With less internal power than before, he produced even greater might.

When he circulated his energy and replenished his internal power, the newly formed internal power continued to contain that mysterious force.

It was truly as if he had obtained a new dantian.

He could not yet know for certain whether this power merely made his internal power more efficient and more potent, or whether it would have some other effect, but he believed it would help in his battle against Hwa Mugi.

Geom Mugeuk even washed his clothes clean and dried them with yang heat energy before leaving the Space-Time Transposition Art. A fragrant scent rose from his body.

‘After enduring that pain, I should at least smell good.’

Geom Mugeuk stood before the wall into which Ju Gashin’s coffin had entered earlier. He bowed to him with solemn courtesy.

“If the day comes when I see you again, I will greet you properly then.”

Geom Mugeuk walked to the entrance of the tomb through which he had come, stood at the boundary between life and death, and spoke loudly to the successive leaders of the Evil Dao Alliance.

“I will live with this fragrant scent instead of the smell of blood. I swear I will live that way!”

Chapter 766: You Are Not the Young Cult Leader
----------------------------------------------
The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was reading a book by the window of an inn room. Moonlight seeped in through the open window. It was an exceedingly cold light when it shone upon a blade, but in this moment, illuminating the pages of a book, it was so very soft and gentle.

“Aren’t you worried?”

The one who broke his deep contemplation was the One Flower Sword Venerable, who had been sitting at the table drinking tea.

It had already been seven days since Geom Mugeuk had failed to return. Geom Mugeuk was someone for whom the saying “no news is good news” suited perfectly, but after seven days without word, she had begun to worry inwardly.

At her concern, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned a page with an unconcerned expression and asked back.

“Who?”

It sounded almost as if he were answering, “Who’s worrying about whom?”

“That’s exactly why I’m more worried. He isn’t someone who would be this late.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon raised his head and looked out the window. The night was deep, and there was not a single passerby on the road. In truth, though he did not show it outwardly, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was worried inside as well.

If Geom Mugeuk had gone to kill someone, he would not have been this worried. He had stayed behind to find the tombs of the past leaders of the Evil Dao Alliance, but there was no way that sharp-witted Geom Mugeuk would still be looking for them now. He should have either found them in a few days or already given up and returned.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon closed his book.

“Will worrying make someone who won’t come return? Let’s go get some air.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable also set down her teacup and rose.

“I was feeling stifled anyway.”

The two left the room. Just then, Jihan came out from the room next door.

“Is there anything you need?”

From the first day they had taken rooms at the inn, he had been beside himself looking after the Demon Venerables and the Sword King. He carefully saw to their meals, and if a drunkard so much as raised his voice, he hurried over and drove him out.

As the Blood Heaven Blade Demon passed him, he seemed to suddenly remember something and turned back to say,

“You don’t have to fuss this much.”

“Yes, sir!”

When they came out of the inn, the One Flower Sword Venerable said casually,

“You’ve changed so much.”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s look asking what it was this time, she said,

“You even show concern for your subordinate now.”

“Concern, my foot. It’s just annoying.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon walked on indifferently.

The change could be seen just from the way he walked. He was matching his pace to hers. In the past, his steps had been as quick as his impatient temper.

“Am I the only one who’s changed?”

At that, the One Flower Sword Venerable had nothing to say. Her state of mind had changed, of course, and even her appearance had changed as well.

“The Young Cult Leader cast a spell on us. He turned our twilight into sunrise.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon thought the One Flower Sword Venerable’s metaphor could not have been more fitting. Especially because, with this journey outside the Cult as the turning point, he had begun to feel as though he wanted to start everything over again. Yes. One did not stop because one grew old; one grew old because one stopped.

As they walked along exchanging all sorts of words like that, they saw the Sword King up ahead, sitting alone on a rock and drinking.

When the Sword King rose and greeted them, the two returned the greeting lightly.

For the past seven days since coming down from Cheonmok Mountain, they had been awkward with one another. In the underground tombs of Cheonmok Mountain, at least they had shared a common enemy, but now that Geom Mugeuk was gone, it felt uncomfortable to eat or drink together, so they had lived almost like strangers.

It seemed they would pass by awkwardly again today as well.

“Got any wine left?”

For some reason, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon expressed his intention to join him.

The Sword King first drank some wine himself, then held the wine bottle out to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. It was an action meant to show that the wine had not been poisoned.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon accepted the bottle and gulped down the wine.

“I don’t suspect you.”

Of course, the reason was Geom Mugeuk.

“Because you’re someone that child trusts.”

How could the Sword King be any different? If not for Geom Mugeuk, no matter how much they had stepped forward for his sake, he would never have associated with the two Demon Venerables. No, to begin with, there would have been no occasion for them to be together like this.

“May we sit for a moment?”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s question, the Sword King readily answered,

“Of course.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the One Flower Sword Venerable sat side by side on the rock where he had been sitting.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon took another sip of wine, then handed the bottle back.

In truth, the reason the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had stopped was because of the One Flower Sword Venerable. He knew she had gained a great realization from her duel with Ak Gunhak.

He thought she might want to speak with him, but could be keeping her distance out of needless consideration for him.

The One Flower Sword Venerable said to the Sword King,

“I saw you training every night.”

The Sword King had devoted himself to training every night. Judging only by his appearance or air, he seemed like someone who would never train, and yet he swung his sword until dawn.

“It was because of you, Sword Venerable.”

“Because of me?”

“Yes.”

The Sword King acknowledged and respected the One Flower Sword Venerable’s swordsmanship.

At this rate, she would eventually catch up to him.

Among all the people he had met so far, she was the only one who had given him that feeling. Geom Mugeuk, of course, was an exception.

Instead of saying that she would catch up, the Sword King put it this way.

“I felt that if we fought again, I might lose.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable knew. In their earlier fight, he had yielded a move to her.

“I wanted to bring about the result you mentioned, but if you train every day like this, it seems it will be difficult.”

“Weren’t you out training as well when you saw me?”

Because they held respect for each other, their gazes toward one another were gentle.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon listened to their conversation in silence. In the past, he would have been displeased by the One Flower Sword Venerable talking with another man. All the more so if that man was an absolute master like the Sword King.

He would have clung to her and felt jealous.

But now he knew. In order to win someone’s heart, rather than trying to possess them, one had to become someone with whom they could rest comfortably.

Sincerity would get through. And if it did not, then they had never been his person to begin with.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked up at the moon. From afar came the howling of wolves and the cries of owls. The conversation between the two as they naturally reviewed the previous fight and discussed swordsmanship was pleasing to hear.

How long had they been sitting there like that? From far away, at the end of the road, a familiar voice reached their ears.

“Come now, isn’t the atmosphere far too nice without me? You should have been fighting and raising a ruckus. You should have been desperately waiting for me to come back!”

Smiles appeared on all three of their faces at the same time.

The One Flower Sword Venerable said to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon,

“You were right. Who’s worrying about whom?”

Just as Geom Mugeuk drew near,

the Blood Heaven Blade Demon flinched and pointed his blade at Geom Mugeuk.

“Wait!”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s warning, Geom Mugeuk stopped walking.

“What is this? Were you already won over while I was gone?”

Then the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked coldly,

“Who are you?”

The first to notice the change in Geom Mugeuk was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. After all, he had seen Geom Mugeuk more often than the Sword King or the One Flower Sword Venerable.

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s warning, the Sword King and the One Flower Sword Venerable also sensed that something about Geom Mugeuk had changed.

“Elder, it’s me.”

Under the moonlight, Geom Mugeuk fully revealed himself.

“No. You are not the Young Cult Leader.”

It was clearly Geom Mugeuk, but there was a strange sense of incongruity. He felt different from the man they usually saw.

“It’s me, me. The very me who, whenever I go out of the Cult and come back, throws aside the masked fellow, the drunkard, and the poisoner, and runs to you first, Elder. I’m the Sword Venerable’s one and only sparring friend, and the very person who sincerely admires our Brother Ak’s bare feet!”

The gazes of all three focused on Geom Mugeuk.

The way he spoke was the same, but he had clearly changed. Had he grown taller? Or had his shoulders broadened?

The three most noticeable changes were his skin, his eyes, and the fragrance flowing from his body.

His skin looked clear and smooth like jade, and an incomparably pleasant scent came from him.

His eyes had always been clear and deep, but now Geom Mugeuk’s gaze seemed as though it could look into even the deepest abyss. The scent from his body made one want to remain by his side.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon took three or four steps toward Geom Mugeuk.

As he quietly examined Geom Mugeuk, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s eyes grew wider and wider.

“You… don’t tell me you underwent Bone Rebirth?”

At his trembling question, the Sword King and the One Flower Sword Venerable were also startled. It was truly something they had not expected at all. Geom Mugeuk did not hide the fact. No, he could not hide it.

“As expected! Our Elder recognized it first.”

Geom Mugeuk made a show of exaggerating.

“My bones were grinding away, my flesh was tearing apart. I really thought I would die from the pain.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked back at the One Flower Sword Venerable and the Sword King. The One Flower Sword Venerable’s voice transmission came to him.

—We may be witnessing one of the most important moments in the history of murim.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon wholly agreed with her voice transmission.

The Sword King, too, could not hide his shock. He had personally seen that Geom Mugeuk was someone with the fate to absorb the secret coffer, but even so, Bone Rebirth?

It was truly far too extraordinary a fortuitous encounter, and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke for the feelings of all three.

“I cannot fathom what task murim intends to entrust to you that it is doing this.”

It was a will of the heavens that only Geom Mugeuk knew.

With a smile, Geom Mugeuk spoke of the life he would live after fulfilling that will.

“There hasn’t been a Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult in all murim history whose life goal was to loaf around and eat well, has there? It’s about time one appeared.”

“How grandly do you plan to loaf around?”

“You’ll be joining me later, won’t you? See for yourself then.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable cut in and said,

“Young Cult Leader, will you let me join as well then?”

“Of course.”

“You promised. Don’t ignore me just because I’ve grown old.”

“That’s my line. You mustn’t say you’re too busy with martial arts training to play! And Sword Venerable, if you mingle with us, you’ll look like a friend.”

Words that were pleasant no matter how many times she heard them were then delivered to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon as well.

“Look at how our Elder’s wrinkles have disappeared. I’ll have to call you big brother from now on. Brother Blade Demon!”

With an expression that said he was spouting nonsense, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon shook his head and asked,

“What about the matter you went for?”

“It ended well.”

Even without asking, Geom Mugeuk’s gleaming skin spoke of the result.

“Let’s return to the Cult early tomorrow.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the One Flower Sword Venerable nodded. The journey that had begun with the three of them riding out together in a carriage was finally coming to an end.

Geom Mugeuk pattered over to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and thrust out his face.

“Now, now, this isn’t something you get to see often. Touch this cheek here! It’s soft, baby skin. I’m telling you, try pulling it once.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon rose in disgust.

“We’ll continue our walk.”

“Yes.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable rose after him.

“No, where are you going! I’m telling you, it’s really smooth! This is your only chance to tug the Young Cult Leader’s cheek! No, even if you’re leaving, you should congratulate me before you go. Congratulations. You worked hard enduring it! You’ll become a legend of murim!”

But the two Demon Venerables pretended not to hear and quickened their steps even more.

Once they had moved away from Geom Mugeuk, only then did the One Flower Sword Venerable reveal her shock and admiration.

“I thought there would be nothing left to surprise me on this journey. Bone Rebirth? So it was possible after all.”

It was an absolute fortuitous encounter said to have been obtained by only a few people throughout the long history of murim.

Even having seen it with her own eyes, she could hardly believe it. If the other party had not been Geom Mugeuk, she would have thought it was some trick with ulterior motives.

“He’s insane. Or the heavens are insane.”

“Both are insane.”

Considering Geom Mugeuk’s actions until now, there was no other way to describe it.

Though they spoke that way, the two Demon Venerables were greatly pleased. Geom Mugeuk was someone they had to protect, even at the cost of their lives, now and in the future. More than anyone, they wished for that Geom Mugeuk to grow stronger.

“The ones behind this chose the wrong opponent.”

At a young age, he had achieved great completion in the Nine Flames Demonic Art, and now he had even undergone Bone Rebirth. Who, indeed, could be a match for Geom Mugeuk?

At her words, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon offered a different perspective.

“Perhaps the reason the heavens are giving the boy such a fate…”

Because he could not imagine that Geom Mugeuk would lose to anyone, he wondered if there might be another reason such fortuitous encounters were being bestowed upon him.

“The Cult Leader may truly push ahead with the unification of murim.”

The only being Geom Mugeuk could not defeat.

Could it be that such a fortuitous encounter had been granted to Geom Mugeuk because he stood in a fate meant to stop the Cult Leader’s ambition? That thought occurred to him.

The One Flower Sword Venerable also knew well that the Cult Leader would not easily give up his dream.

“If that is the case, then for the Young Cult Leader, that fight will be a far harder one.”

The One Flower Sword Venerable added worriedly,

“When that time comes, whose side should we stand on?”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk watched the two Demon Venerables disappear into the distance.

Contrary to the Demon Venerables’ worries, in this moment Geom Mugeuk was not thinking of his father at all.

All of the Twelve Zodiac Kings were dead.

“Now the real one will come out.”

The Sword King, who did not know of Hwa Mugi’s existence, thought that “the real one” referred to the Dark Palace Lord. Could the Dark Palace Lord, having lost all his subordinates, truly face this Geom Mugeuk?

“Brother Ak.”

The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Even if you’re going to leave when the time comes, stay and enjoy yourself to your heart’s content at our Cult first.”

“Why do you think I’m leaving?”

“Because everyone who asks that ends up leaving. Don’t give me pointless hope. It hurts.”

He wanted to keep the Sword King by his side no matter what. But what came before that feeling was the desire for him to live the life he wanted.

“I won’t stop you from leaving. But leave later. Much later. Spend plenty of happy days with me, then leave when you think, Ah, it’s no fun being with this fellow anymore. I’m sick and tired of these Demonic Cult bastards.”

Instead of answering, the Sword King looked up at the moon in the night sky.

‘If I have to leave when being with you is no longer fun, I’ll never be able to leave for the rest of my life.’

In truth, the Sword King’s heart was split in half. As much as he wanted to leave far away and live quietly among people who did not know him, he also wanted to stay beside Geom Mugeuk, who had been granted this astonishing fate, and watch what kind of life he would live.

“Whether I leave or settle down, first, what we need to do is…”

Though he knew perfectly well who it was, the Sword King pretended not to and added,

“Go see just how beautiful the woman you mentioned is.”

Chapter 767: I Want to Meet the One Who Gave Birth to a Son Like You
--------------------------------------------------------------------
The next morning, a carriage was prepared in front of the inn.

Jihan had risen at daybreak and made thorough preparations for departure. He fed the horses their fill and cleaned the carriage until it shone. Now all that remained was to climb aboard and leave.

But the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Ilhwa Sword Sovereign did not get into the carriage.

“Go on ahead. We’ll enjoy the breeze a little longer before returning.”

During their walk the previous night, Ilhwa Sword Sovereign had said something unexpected.

—Since we’ve come this far, let’s stop by Taihu for the first time in a long while before we return.

Taihu lay in Jiangsu Province, north of Zhejiang. In the past, when Geom Ujin and the Fist Demon had roamed the jianghu, the four of them had once met there, together with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Ilhwa Sword Sovereign. He could not remember exactly what had happened then, but a faint memory remained that it had been quite enjoyable.

It was one of the few memories of youth she had shared with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

—I’m curious how much it has changed.

—Let’s go.

There was no hesitation in the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s decision. No matter how enjoyable a journey with Geom Mugeuk might be, it could not compare to a journey alone with Ilhwa Sword Sovereign.

Normally, Geom Mugeuk would have made a fuss, saying, “Where are you two going by yourselves? Take us with you too,” and carried on with his usual banter. But this time, he obediently let the two of them go.

“It has been a long time since you came out, so please take care of everything you wish to do before returning. No, you need not come back for a year or so. After being away from the cult for a long time myself, I realized something. Infuriatingly enough, the cult runs perfectly well even without us.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon and Ilhwa Sword Sovereign exchanged a light nod of farewell with the Sword King.

As the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned away, he sent a voice transmission to the Sword King.

—The Young Cult Leader likes you very much. Stay by his side as much as you can.

Without waiting for an answer, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon moved on, and Ilhwa Sword Sovereign followed after him.

Geom Mugeuk stood for a moment, watching the two of them from behind. Please make many memories before you return.

“Now then, let us be on our way as well.”

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King boarded the carriage, and Jihan climbed onto the driver’s seat. There were three of them, but he was returning with two people different from those he had left the cult with.

Geom Mugeuk stuck his head out the carriage window and shouted loudly.

“Let’s go! To the main cult!”

He shouted with great spirit, but the carriage did not move.

“Why aren’t we going?”

With a slightly flustered face, Jihan turned back and said,

“Because I don’t know where the headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult is.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and shouted,

“When you’re not sure where to go, just run first!”

Inside the carriage, the Sword King, who had heard it as well, smiled. Those words had been meant for him too.

Is it truly all right for me to go to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? To himself, still hesitating.

What are you worrying about? You go first!

And so the carriage began to pick up speed, racing toward the place where a new life awaited.

* * *

The Dark Palace Lord, Hwa Mugyeong, was wrapped in darkness.

Was he having a nightmare? From within the darkness that coiled around his body like smoke, a low groan seeped out.

The darkness surrounding him writhed as if in turmoil.

“Urgh!”

Gaye, the Divine Maiden Palace Lord, who had been sitting with her eyes closed a short distance away, opened them.

“Did you have a bad dream?”

Sssssss.

As the darkness enveloping Hwa Mugyeong parted, his face was revealed. His face was deeply furrowed, and beads of sweat clung to it. From his expression, one could guess what sort of dream it had been.

Out of habit, Hwa Mugyeong raised his head and looked up at the ceiling. Countless constellations were already shining there, but fortunately, compared to before he had fallen asleep, no new star had lit up.

After giving his final order, Hwa Mugyeong had not left this room at all.

This room, where the great secret coffer stood, where a mural of six orbs was painted on the rear wall, and where the constellations on the ceiling revealed how much of the seal had been undone.

“You should be the one dreaming.”

Hwa Mugyeong spoke in a voice with a deep resonance.

Gaye could guess what he meant. He was saying that she should be the one receiving prophecies.

Yes, at a time like this, a prophecy was desperately needed. They could not even begin to sense where this situation was flowing.

Sssssss.

Just then, a pitch-black hole appeared in the floor, and Hoe, Hwa Mugyeong’s hands and feet, rose up through it.

Before he even gave his report, the result could already be read in that brief silence and in his eyes.

“We failed.”

The first to react was Gaye. A deep sigh escaped from where she sat behind them.

“Haa.”

They had failed to find Jajeong, the final orb. Naturally, they must have failed to extract the energy within Geom Mugeuk’s body as well.

It was the moment their last hope vanished.

She had thought Hwa Mugyeong’s rage would explode, but unexpectedly, he was calm. That made him all the more frightening. At this point, nothing he did would be strange.

“Did you summon all the souls of the dead?”

“Yes.”

“And still you failed?”

Hoe answered with a gloomy expression.

“All the souls were annihilated. And Ak Gunhak survived and joined the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

Hwa Mugyeong did not grow angry over that matter. If this mission had failed, then that was only natural. From the beginning, Ak Gunhak’s betrayal had been expected, and he had been sent for the purpose of summoning the souls.

Hoe prostrated himself flat on the spot.

“Before the seal is fully undone, I will find the energy of Jajeong by any means necessary.”

How, when even summoning the dead had not accomplished it? This was not something that could be solved with loyalty alone.

It was at that very moment.

Flaaaash.

Light burst from the secret coffer depicted in the mural behind Gaye. Astonishingly, the place that shone was the final remaining circle.

“The energy of the Purple Dao has appeared in the world.”

The purple light that filled the room dyed the faces of those within, who had fallen beyond surprise and into shock.

“Could it be? Are you saying the Young Cult Leader found even the final energy?”

The one who answered Hwa Mugyeong’s question was the mural behind them.

Flaaaaaash!

The six circles began shining all at once, and light poured from the square shape at the center. Once the central square shone, the six circles surrounding it lost their light.

“The Six Dao Primordial Energies have merged into one!”

Five of the energies had been with the Young Cult Leader. If all six had merged into one, that meant—

“The Young Cult Leader has absorbed the power of the Six Dao Primordial Energies.”

Her voice, which had rung out like a cry, faded.

“……The power of the Six Dao Primordial Energies now belongs to the Young Cult Leader.”

A heavy silence flowed through the room.

Hwa Mugyeong slowly looked up at the ceiling and, toward the sky that lay beyond it, spoke coldly.

“Is this all your will amounts to? Is this pathetic reality what you left behind three hundred years later?”

The prophecy handed down through the generations since three hundred years ago had been wrong. That power had not gone to them, but to the Young Cult Leader.

Until now, even when the Young Cult Leader had snatched away the power of the orbs, Hwa Mugyeong had regarded that itself as fate. A fate in which the Young Cult Leader would gather them with great effort, only for Hwa Mugyeong to seize them in the end.

Because there had been a prophecy that the Palace of Heaven’s Will would rule the murim.

“Is this your Heaven’s Will? You damned sky! Do you think I will give up because of this? Don’t make me laugh! This is not your will, but mine, and the will of all of us who have struggled for three hundred years!”

As if answering his cry—

Pat.

Another star lit up.

Watching it, Hwa Mugyeong’s two pupils trembled.

Then, as though he had resolved something—

Srrrrrr.

The energy of darkness that had been wrapped around Hwa Mugyeong vanished in an instant.

Hwa Mugyeong was completely revealed.

Layer upon layer of wrinkled traces of time covered his body.

It was the first time Gaye had seen him with the darkness so entirely gone. Until now, darkness had always lingered around him in some form.

And she understood why he had cast off the energy of darkness.

Because very soon, darkness itself would emerge into the world.

If he could not control it, then even if he had to submit to it, he would achieve his objective no matter what. He, who had ruled darkness, had resolved himself to be ruled by it.

He had not yet given up.

* * *

The carriage continued racing toward the headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Geom Mugeuk deeply enjoyed his time with the Sword King. He cooked with his own hands and fed him, and at night, they lit campfires in scenic fields.

They spoke of martial arts while feeling the cool breeze that entered through the carriage window, and more than anything else, they could demonstrate them directly.

“Brother Ak, it has been a long time. Why don’t you have a bout with me?”

Geom Mugeuk was curious to see what changes there would be when he fought with a body that had undergone rebirth. And there was an utterly excellent person with whom to test it. He could not simply let the opportunity pass.

And he did not allow Jihan to watch the match.

“It is not because the martial arts are so extraordinary that I am hiding them. It is because if you see them, they will instead hinder your future training.”

For it to be helpful, the difference in skill had to be within reason. Watching a bout between himself and the Sword King would only obstruct Jihan’s training.

In a windswept field, Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King stood facing each other.

Both of them knew well how precious this moment was. Search all of the murim, and there would be no chance to have such a spar against such an opponent.

The Sword King silently gazed at Geom Mugeuk. Even though Geom Mugeuk had not yet drawn his sword, the Sword King could feel it from his stance alone.

‘The pressure is different.’

It was completely different from when they had pointed their swords at each other at the end. And so the first words he spoke to the opponent before him flowed from his mouth.

“Shall we fight for real?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled gently.

“Why are you being so frightening?”

Shing.

Geom Mugeuk lightly drew the Black Demon Sword.

Geom Mugeuk standing there, and Geom Mugeuk after drawing his sword, gave off yet another feeling entirely.

‘You are the more frightening one.’

Because he felt that with that face full of mischief, Geom Mugeuk might truly be able to kill him.

The Sword King also slowly drew his iron sword.

The two of them, watching for an opening with deep gazes, moved at the same time.

Clang!

Sword met sword.

Geom Mugeuk did not launch the next move.

The moment their swords collided, Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes and sank into thought.

With no choice, the Sword King waited for him to open his eyes. From this sight, he could tell.

‘He has already surpassed me.’

From the one strike he had delivered with all his sincerity, Geom Mugeuk had undoubtedly gained some realization.

Perhaps through that single exchange, Geom Mugeuk was drawing out hundreds of exchanges in his mind.

How much time had passed?

Geom Mugeuk opened the eyes he had closed. The swords of the two men were still crossed.

“Why are you so strong?”

As expected, it meant he had realized something from that single exchange.

“You should replace that iron sword with a treasured sword!”

The Sword King had never once thought of changing swords. This old iron sword had been enough.

“I want to see you holding a treasured sword, Brother Ak.”

Geom Mugeuk was telling him to wield stronger swordsmanship with a stronger sword.

Carrying an old iron sword was certainly a striking sight, but even that striking quality was bound within a frame. He had to break that frame to become stronger. Now was the time to put on shoes.

The Sword King could instinctively feel it.

When the time came for him to take off the shoes he had put on again, he would no longer even need an iron sword.

“I will obtain a treasured sword for you.”

“I have a lot of money.”

“I will obtain something money cannot buy.”

“You buy me shoes, you buy me a sword, you even introduce me to women. Why are you doing this to me?”

Geom Mugeuk withdrew his sword and walked toward the carriage as he said,

“If I want to tie Brother Ak tightly to my side, I must put in every effort. And that effort has not even begun yet!”

* * *

The carriage ran and ran, and at last arrived at the headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

The moment Jihan saw the majestic buildings beyond the high walls, his heart pounded as if it would burst.

‘I’m finally here.’

The demonic practitioners standing guard at the main gate exuded truly terrifying demonic energy.

From far away, he had rehearsed it in his mind countless times.

‘This is the carriage carrying the Young Cult Leader. No, that’s not right. This is the carriage the Young Cult Leader is riding in, move aside! Should I say it that forcefully?’

He could not ask Geom Mugeuk about something like this, so Jihan’s heart only kept thumping.

And since it was his first time at headquarters, he thought they might tell him to get down and inspect him in various ways. He had all sorts of worries and imaginings, but the actual result was anticlimactic.

The gate opened, and the demonic practitioners spoke respectfully.

“You may proceed straight down this road.”

They already knew Geom Mugeuk was riding inside.

The moment they entered the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, an exclamation escaped Jihan’s mouth on its own.

“Ah!”

A vast, open space spread before him.

Buildings had been erected endlessly throughout that space, and between them towered terrifying statues of evil spirits.

Countless demonic practitioners came and went through the place. It felt as though they had opened a gate and stepped into another world.

Geom Mugeuk stopped the carriage as it slowly proceeded forward.

“Since we are here, let us get down and walk while looking around the main cult.”

Geom Mugeuk, the Sword King, and Jihan got down from the carriage.

When the passing demonic practitioners saw Geom Mugeuk, they respectfully cupped their fists and greeted him.

“We greet the Young Cult Leader!”

“Have you been well?”

“Yes!”

To the demonic practitioners who recognized him from afar and greeted him, Geom Mugeuk waved back one by one.

“I am not creating a pleasant atmosphere to make Brother Ak grow attached to this place! Please do not misunderstand!”

“I wasn’t thinking that.”

Geom Mugeuk within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was exactly as the Sword King had expected.

He was not the sort to treat experts well and treat subordinates roughly. And the Sword King could feel that the bright expressions directed at Geom Mugeuk were not false.

Geom Mugeuk asked Jihan,

“What do you think?”

“I still feel dazed.”

“From now on, this place is your home.”

It did not feel real that he had attacked the man who would one day become the master of this tremendous place, and that he had become that man’s subordinate.

Thus overwhelmed by the grandeur of the Divine Cult, they finally arrived at the Heavenly Demon Hall.

“You wait here. There is someone I will introduce to you later.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

After having Jihan wait, Geom Mugeuk looked at the Sword King and said,

“Brother Ak, let us go.”

The Sword King looked up at the statue of an evil spirit standing tall at the entrance of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

He truly had never imagined that the day would come in his life when he would meet the Heavenly Demon.

“Are you nervous?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Sword King shook his head.

“No.”

“As expected! Brother Ak, your nerve is the best.”

That was not the reason. Geom Mugeuk had missed the mark completely.

“It is not that I am nervous. I am frightened. Do you see my hand trembling here?”

“Why?”

“Because I am about to meet the man who gave birth to a son like you.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and answered,

“Do not worry. Am I not the blue that surpassed the indigo, the tiger son of a tiger father, the later wave of the Yangtze? And my father is unexpectedly ordinary and affectionate.”

The two of them entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Far off, beyond the Path of Blood, Geom Ujin could be seen seated in the grand chair.

Already suffocating beneath his presence, the Sword King muttered under his breath,

“You damned liar.”

Chapter 768: Why Are You Asking Father That?
--------------------------------------------
It was not a presence he felt simply because he knew the other party was the Heavenly Demon.

Even if he had run into him on the road, he would have felt the same. Even amid a crowd of hundreds, he would have recognized him at once.

A presence that could not be hidden. No, a presence he simply did not bother to hide.

“Father, your son has returned after going out from the cult!”

When Geom Mugeuk greeted him in a loud voice, for some reason, the Sword King put on the shoes he had been carrying at his waist.

“Come, Brother Ak. Let’s go.”

He was as bright as ever, but Geom Mugeuk was more tense than he had ever been.

First, how would his father react to his Bone Rebirth?

Next, would he accept the Sword King? If he did not, what should he do? It was too much to simply say, He’s just my ponytailed friend, so please don’t mind him, when the Sword King’s skill and very existence were so immense.

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King walked side by side along the Path of Blood and arrived below the grand chair.

The Sword King cupped his fist respectfully and paid his respects.

“I am Ak Gunhak. Thanks to your son, I have the honor of meeting a venerable figure of the murim. It is an honor to meet you.”

There was neither goodwill nor hostility in Geom Ujin’s gaze. His impassive eyes turned toward the Sword King’s feet.

“I heard you mostly go about barefoot?”

It seemed Geom Mugeuk had spoken about him.

“I put them on because I thought it would be discourteous to meet an honored person barefoot.”

It was not that he was watching himself because the other party was stronger than he was.

Originally, he had intended to take them off before entering, but when he heard Geom Mugeuk calling for his father, he changed his mind.

It was courtesy he showed not because the man was the Heavenly Demon, but because he was Geom Mugeuk’s father. Putting on shoes was the utmost courtesy he could offer.

Geom Ujin and the Sword King’s gazes tangled in midair. No matter how strong the Sword King was, he could not compare to his father. But he was skilled enough to provoke his father.

That was why Geom Mugeuk’s heart trembled even more as he watched from the side.

The friends he made were Bi Sain and Jin Hagun—every single one of them probably not to his father’s liking.

‘Father, this man is also my friend. Please look kindly upon him.’

Geom Ujin’s gaze, which had been fixed on the Sword King, turned to Geom Mugeuk.

At last, what was to come had come.

After silently looking down at his son, Geom Ujin rose from his seat.

He slowly descended the stairs. He was neither fast nor slow, and not a single footstep sounded.

To Geom Mugeuk, he was the father he always saw, but for the Sword King, it was different.

With every step Geom Ujin took closer, the Sword King’s heart pounded. Each step felt as though the presence of the Heavenly Demon struck his heart with a fist—thud. If he had moved according to instinct, he would have retreated one step for every step the man approached.

Geom Ujin came right up in front of Geom Mugeuk.

Even the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had recognized his Bone Rebirth, so there was no way his father would fail to notice.

Sure enough, a sneer formed at his father’s lips. The sneer Geom Mugeuk liked so much.

The emotion contained in that sneer today was this:

Now you’ve gone so far as to undergo Bone Rebirth and show up?

Yes, if he was going to sneer, he would sneer. His father was not the sort of man to feel jealousy.

Geom Mugeuk raised his hand high and confessed.

“I’ll say this in advance, but I really am your son, Father. I may be an unfilial son who underwent Bone Rebirth before you, but I am definitely your son!”

Then Geom Ujin reached out his hands.

Srrk. Grip.

Geom Ujin seized both of Geom Mugeuk’s arms with both hands.

It was an unexpected action, and Geom Mugeuk flinched in surprise.

“If you wish to test whether a body that has undergone Bone Rebirth can be torn apart or not, please stop! If you tear me, Father, I will be torn!”

Fortunately, there was no test of whether a body that had undergone Bone Rebirth could be torn apart.

Srrk.

Geom Ujin’s hand touched Geom Mugeuk’s shoulder. Starting from there, he began to examine his son’s body.

His arms, shoulders, waist, back, and even his legs—he touched and checked every part one by one. Not roughly, but with great care.

How much his bones had grown, how his skin and muscles had changed, what the balance of his body was like.

Geom Mugeuk found his father’s touch unfamiliar. Thinking back, aside from when he had been very young, his father had never laid a hand on his body.

His father’s touch was like that of a physician examining a patient, and it held affection. He was personally confirming whether anything had gone wrong.

Even knowing that feeling, Geom Mugeuk was inwardly tense. His father might suddenly attack him. And then he would say this:

You underwent Bone Rebirth, so I thought you could dodge at least this much.

His father was more than capable of doing that. When he thought of the time he had told him that he had mastered the Flying Heaven Sword Art and a gust of finger wind had flown at his face, he had to be ten times more tense today.

But what came flying at him today was a hundred times more shocking than that finger wind.

After examining his son’s body, Geom Ujin’s gaze turned toward the Sword King.

“This man…”

Then, looking back at Geom Mugeuk, he said coldly,

“Kill him.”

It was an order to kill the Sword King.

Not only Geom Mugeuk, but even the Sword King was startled. He had expected that the Heavenly Demon would not welcome him, but he had never imagined he would tell his son to kill him.

“No, Father. We may like killing people, but we’re not so far gone that we kill someone who has just arrived and greeted us warmly, are we?”

As if to show that it was not an order given by mistake, Geom Ujin said nothing.

“He’s uglier than you, Father, so there’s no way he stole a woman from you. Was he perhaps someone who ran off with your money when you were young?”

Of course, neither flattery nor jokes worked.

The Sword King wondered how Geom Mugeuk would deal with this situation. Like his father, the son was someone who could astonish people.

Geom Mugeuk let out a deep sigh.

“Brother Ak, if it were an order from someone else, that would be one thing, but I cannot disobey my father’s order.”

What? So he was going to kill him?

As if he truly intended to carry out the order, Geom Mugeuk drew the Black Demon Sword.

At this unexpected turn of events, the Sword King stared at Geom Mugeuk with an incredulous expression.

“Young Cult Leader, I even put on shoes, and this is how you mean to treat me?”

Because he was before the Cult Leader, the Sword King spoke to Geom Mugeuk more respectfully than usual. Even though that Cult Leader had given the order to kill him.

“What can I do? It is my father’s order.”

Realizing that Geom Mugeuk standing before him was utterly serious, the Sword King soon accepted the situation.

After looking once around at Geom Ujin and the Heavenly Demon Hall, the Sword King looked back at Geom Mugeuk. To die by Geom Mugeuk’s hand in the Heavenly Demon Hall, with the Heavenly Demon watching?

“Yes, this is a fine enough end.”

The Sword King also drew his sword. He had already tried to die for him beneath Mount Cheonmok. That death had merely been delayed a little. Thinking so, his heart grew at ease.

The two stood facing each other, swords leveled.

“Young Cult Leader, you must never let your guard down. I intend to display every bit of my skill.”

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. The Sword King was willingly prepared to fight him and die.

Yes, was the reason he liked the Sword King merely because he tied his hair back and went around barefoot? It was because of this manner of his—so detached even before death, despite possessing such tremendous skill. If it were me, I wouldn’t be able to die like that simply because my skill would be too precious to waste.

For a moment, taut tension flowed between them.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze, which had been fixed on the Sword King, turned to Geom Ujin.

“You should say it now. ‘My son, that is enough. I have fully understood that you value my order even over friendship.’”

Geom Ujin still only looked at Geom Mugeuk without a word. He was truly pushing through the order to kill him.

“Then please tell me the reason you want him killed.”

But Geom Ujin did not tell him the reason either.

“No, this really is too much!”

Geom Mugeuk returned the Black Demon Sword to its sheath.

“You know I cannot kill him, don’t you?”

“If you disobey, I will make you step down from the position of Young Cult Leader.”

His father’s eyes were sincere no matter who looked at them. The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk with a tense heart. Surely he would not give it up.

“I will give up the position of Young Cult Leader.”

At that, the Sword King quickly interjected.

“Young Cult Leader, I am not a man worth that much.”

“Of course not.”

“What?”

“I am not giving up the position of Young Cult Leader because Brother Ak is worth as much as that position. This position, which determines the fate of countless people in our cult, is not something that can be compared to any other value. Even my life is not worth as much as this position.”

If those words were for his father, the next words were for the Sword King.

“I wish to kill him, but I do not have the confidence to kill Brother Ak.”

Geom Mugeuk did not put forward his relationship with the Sword King here.

How could I kill a friend? He could not approach it with such sentiment. It would only have the opposite effect on his father.

Also, this choice preserved the Sword King’s dignity.

It was not that he could not kill him because they were close; it was that he could not kill him because he was strong.

It was consideration for the martial artist known as the Sword King.

“Father, this man is the strongest enemy I have ever fought. I have not even seen his final move yet.”

Even before his father, the Sword King was still the man who had become the First King of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

If his stature as a martial artist fell and his pride was wounded, he could not remain by Geom Mugeuk’s side. Especially not in front of him. If such a thing happened, he would leave.

“Your son might die, Father!”

Even after he went this far, his father did not back down.

“You have your older brother, so it will be fine. I will avenge you.”

“I refuse. I cannot bear to see my brother become Cult Leader. It would make my stomach twist.”

“If you do not kill that man, you will not become Cult Leader anyway.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at his father. Now it was time to ask.

“Why are you testing me?”

“Testing?”

“Yes. Because you are not someone who would give such an order, Father. If you truly wanted this man dead, you would have killed him yourself.”

Astonishingly, it truly had been a test. And the reason was something he had never imagined.

“I had to confirm whether you were still the same.”

“What?”

Unexpected words followed. It was something even Geom Mugeuk had not known.

“When it comes to Bone Rebirth, the changes to the body are not all that important.”

Geom Mugeuk looked down at his own body.

“You say that after seeing this smooth skin and smelling this fragrance? And is that all? My frame has changed, and my whole body feels as light as if it could fly. What on earth could be more important than this?”

Geom Ujin stared piercingly into his son’s eyes and answered.

“Your mind.”

“!”

“Bone Rebirth completely changes even the blood vessels inside your head. Do you know why there are so few people in this gangho who have undergone Bone Rebirth? Because even if they endured the pain of the flesh, during the process of their minds changing, they either became madmen or died.”

His father was usually a man of few words, but when it came to martial arts, he was a father who spoke without stinting.

“Has nothing changed about you?”

“It feels as if my mind is clearer than before. My thoughts move better, and my memory seems to have improved too.”

He thought that might have been why, during the first move of his spar with the Sword King, he had sunk into thought.

“What matters is how you changed. Separate from your mind becoming clearer, you may have changed into someone with a strange personality. But seeing your pathetic response…”

Having finished his explanation, Geom Ujin’s expression softened.

“Fortunately, it seems nothing has changed.”

It meant that his Bone Rebirth had been perfect.

In truth, Geom Mugeuk guessed that his father’s test had not been for that reason alone. There were many other ways to confirm whether his mind was intact.

And yet, he had specifically ordered him to kill the Sword King. Why? He had wanted to confirm how the Sword King would react.

Had the Sword King’s response passed his father’s test?

And since their relationship had already become entangled, had he not deepened that relationship even further?

At his father’s order, the Sword King had willingly offered up his life, and Geom Mugeuk had shown that he would give up the position of Young Cult Leader.

His father must have known that Geom Mugeuk would never kill the Sword King.

Geom Ujin turned and walked back up to the grand chair as he said,

“Congratulations, my son.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk stared at his father with a dazed face.

Congratulations, my son.

Those mere four words shook Geom Mugeuk’s heart. He could feel that the congratulations were sincere.

“Thank you, Father.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed his head and expressed his gratitude.

‘I had no intention of marrying in this life either, but Father keeps shaking my resolve.’

Geom Mugeuk did not let this good mood pass him by.

“Father, in celebration of my Bone Rebirth, please grant your son one request.”

With an incredulous expression, Geom Ujin asked,

“What request?”

It was truly both shameless and bold.

“Please bestow the White Flower Sword upon me.”

For an instant, silence flowed.

“You mean the Baekhwa Sword, one of our cult’s Four Great Treasured Swords?”

“Yes.”

The Baekhwa Sword ranked fourth among the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Four Great Treasured Swords, alongside the Heavenly Demon Sword, the Black Demon Sword, and the Death Spirit Sword.

If the Black Demon Sword was rough and destructive, the Death Spirit Sword harbored a dark and wicked energy, while the Baekhwa Sword possessed a gentle and noble aura.

“I would like to give the Baekhwa Sword to Brother Ak here as a gift.”

The Sword King was startled. When he had heard that a treasured sword would be found for him, he had never imagined it would be one of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Four Great Treasured Swords. He himself knew of the Baekhwa Sword.

“Young Cult Leader, that sword is far too much for me.”

The Sword King politely declined.

“On the other hand, it’s also a sword that suits Brother Ak very well.”

“I am not such a noble person. I will accept only your feelings, Young Cult Leader.”

Just then, his father gave an order into the empty air.

“Bring the Baekhwa Sword.”

At those words, the Sword King was taken aback, and Geom Mugeuk looked up at his father with a smile.

‘I’m in trouble too. I’m already worried about how I’ll stand in Father’s way when that day comes.’

But for now, the Sword King came first.

“Please don’t give some new order like, ‘Kill this man with that sword’!”

Even as he heard Geom Mugeuk’s joke, the Sword King was flustered.

He had truly never once been flustered even before the Dark Palace Lord, yet today, because of this father and son, he had no idea how many times he had been startled and thrown off balance.

‘So? They’re really giving me that precious Baekhwa Sword? One of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s Four Great Treasured Swords?’

Fine, if he conceded a hundred times over, Geom Mugeuk might give it to him. He was the sort of person who could do such a thing.

But the Heavenly Demon was giving him the Baekhwa Sword? When he had even met the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult as an enemy.

Meanwhile, Hwi appeared there carrying a sword case.

Geom Mugeuk exchanged a greeting with Hwi with his eyes.

‘Uncle, it’s been a long time.’

Hwi’s masked eyes curved slightly in a smile. Those eyes seemed to say:

‘Congratulations on your rebirth, Young Cult Leader.’

After handing over the sword case, Hwi vanished without a sound.

When Geom Mugeuk received the case containing the Baekhwa Sword, Geom Ujin said,

“It is a congratulatory gift.”

“Thank you, Father.”

After bowing his head respectfully in gratitude, Geom Mugeuk handed the sword case to the Sword King in front of his father. Of course, he did not forget to add a joke.

“Open it carefully. It might explode.”

The Sword King silently looked down at the sword case he had received. He felt dazed. He had never dreamed he would come into possession of such a precious sword so easily.

“Why are you bestowing such a gift upon me?”

When the Sword King asked Geom Ujin, Geom Mugeuk cut in.

“No, I’m the one giving it to you. Why are you asking Father? Look at me!”

But the Sword King was still looking at Geom Ujin.

Geom Ujin answered with eyes that showed neither favor nor hostility, as ever.

“Let us say it is because you looked good wearing those shoes.”

“No, I’m the one who gave him those shoes too!”

You are all madmen. He could not bring himself to say it outright.

The Sword King bowed his head politely and conveyed the sentiment in a refined form.

“I now understand well how someone like the Young Cult Leader could have been born.”

Chapter 769: Do You Look Down on Me for Being a Martial Arts Instructor?
------------------------------------------------------------------------
At the words, “Now I understand how someone like the Young Cult Leader could be born.”

“You gave me a sword, yet you are cursing me?”

At his father’s words, the Sword King was slightly flustered. *You people are all madmen!* Had his inner thoughts been exposed even though he had phrased it mildly?

“Please do not misunderstand. What I meant was that after meeting the Cult Leader, I understood how such an excellent son could have been born.”

Because it was before his father, the Sword King earnestly made excuses, but Geom Mugik knew. That his father had spoken in jest. And the fact that his father had joked with him meant one thing.

He had passed.

The Sword King had passed in his father’s eyes. Indeed, what parent could dislike someone willing to lay down their life for their child?

“Of course it is the highest praise, nothing but the highest praise. You have worked hard, Father. To raise a son so excellently.”

The father made an expression as if to say, “How dare you say such a thing with your own mouth.” Had the Sword King not been beside him, he would have undoubtedly said it aloud.

The Sword King respectfully bowed his head to convey his gratitude.

“I shall not forget the grace you have bestowed upon me.”

How much value this sword held for him was not important. One had to see how much value this White Flower Sword held for the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. It was one of the four greatest treasures that had been bestowed.

Geom Mugik also bade farewell to his father.

“Father, I shall come to see you again later. This time, I have far too much to tell you.”

As always, whenever he left the cult and returned, he would tell his father what had happened. He could not leave untold the thrilling tales of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the One Flower Sword Venerable.

“Let us have dinner together.”

His father probably had no idea how moving it was for this son that those words now came so easily.

Geom Mugik exited the Heavenly Demon Palace together with the Sword King.

As they walked down the long corridor before the Heavenly Demon Palace, the Sword King stopped.

He stood briefly, gazing at the evil ghost statues supporting the pillars lined along the corridor.

“What is it you truly want from me? Do you want me to fight for you? Or do you want me to become your subordinate?”

Their gazes entangled in the empty air.

“If you wish for me to become your subordinate and fight for you, would you heed it?”

The Sword King did not answer. It was a question not easily answered, yet also perhaps one easily answered.

“Honestly, I would like to throw a tantrum and ask just that of you. Because once a master-servant relationship is formed, Brother Ak would stay with me for life.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugik spoke.

“But I prefer being friends with Brother Ak. Even if you were to suddenly leave, and I spent every year wondering where you are and what you are doing, I would prefer staying friends with Brother Ak. I do not like seeing Brother Ak beneath someone else.”

The Sword King’s gaze deepened. So that was why. That was why it could also be easily answered. The opportunity to serve such a person would surely not come without the fortune of heaven.

“Even so, this sword is too excessive a gift.”

Geom Mugik dispelled that burden thus:

“That is a gift not for Brother Ak, but given for my sake.”

“For yourself?”

“Yes. For myself. I wish to live among splendid and good people.”

“What does that have to do with this sword?”

“Because that sword will make Brother Ak a stronger and more splendid person.”

The Sword King had believed that one became truly dashing and free only when discarding precious swords.

And now Geom Mugik was saying the opposite.

That by wearing a precious sword, one grew stronger. That one became freer and more dashing.

“If I could not be among such splendid people, I would likely have been living while dreaming the same dream as Brother Ak.”

The Sword King let out a light sigh.

“That dream…… is not such a good dream.”

If you know that, why did you try so hard to leave, Brother Ak? Wake from that dream. And dream a new one!

But he did not speak those words. The Sword King would know his heart well enough.

Geom Mugik’s gaze turned to the sword box the Sword King was holding.

“Why don’t you draw the sword once? You ought to give me a chance to show off.”

The Sword King opened the sword box and drew out the White Flower Sword. The scabbard of the revealed White Flower Sword was elegant and luxurious.

Shwiiiik.

The moment the sword was drawn, it felt as if the surrounding air changed in an instant.

It was a sword that radiated a brilliance that did not dazzle the eyes, exuding an elegant and gentle nobility.

Just imagining the Sword King attacking with that sword was enough to stir fear.

“It is the only one among the four treasured swords of our cult that does not harbor demonic energy or ghostly energy.”

When the Sword King infused his inner power, the sword let out a low cry.

Humm.

A stream of energy flowing along the blade mingled with the Sword King’s breath.

The Sword King, feeling the sword with his eyes closed for a moment, opened them with a pleased expression.

“It is truly a fine sword.”

Indeed, how could it not be fine? Especially since the sword’s temperament suited the Sword King’s own so well, he must have felt it was even better.

The Sword King drew the iron sword he had been wearing at his waist.

Clang.

He flicked it with his finger, and it snapped clean in half.

“What, is this really a sword that breaks so easily?”

“It is a sword I bought for a single nyang at a street market.”

Geom Mugik shouted as if dumbfounded.

“Even so, to discard it so heartlessly. It must have been steeped in countless memories. My friend, this man has abandoned you.”

“What of it? If it breaks or is lost, one simply buys another.”

The Sword King sheathed the White Flower Sword at his waist where the iron sword had been.

“It is my sword now. Don’t ask for it back.”

Geom Mugik stood for a moment, watching the Sword King’s back as he strode away.

How could there be no memories attached to that old iron sword? Breaking the sword was the Sword King’s answer to the gift of the White Flower Sword.

That he would truly wear this sword.

It was his answer to Geom Mugik’s goodwill.

“Now that we’ve paid our respects to my ordinary and amiable father, it is time to go meet the one who has been yearning for Brother Ak with bated breath! Jihan, you wait a bit. Let us handle the urgent ones first.”

* * *

Swords simultaneously split the air at the training ground.

Those training there were the Ghost Shadow Three Squads, the assassins of the Gain Sect.

“Keep your backs straighter!”

The woman instructing them was Chairan.

Shwiiiik!

Cutting, thrusting, and blocking.

They were basic movements of swordsmanship, but their swords held power, and their movements lacked even an inch of error. They were building stamina and learning the basics of swordsmanship without using internal energy.

Having entered the Ghost Shadow Division, they were no longer assassins.

Perhaps if they only carried out operations focused on assassination, but if deployed in full-frontal warfare, their assassination arts were at an absolute disadvantage.

Thus they had to learn swordsmanship usable in full-frontal combat. The problem was the lack of suitable swordsmanship to learn. She had learned some swordsmanship briefly in her youth, but it was not martial arts of a level to pass on to her subordinates.

Therefore, for now, she was making them repeat only basic swordsmanship training.

“Oooh!”

Low exclamations of admiration could be heard from somewhere.

Dozens of demonic practitioners were watching them train.

Some were passing by, and some had come deliberately. The beautiful women’s training was truly the talk of the cult grounds.

Conversely, there was also a woman secretly watching them.

She was none other than Sipchilgyo, performing her martial arts. She continuously scanned the onlookers, wondering if the Crescent Moon Masked Guest might be among them.

Since they were in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult together, she had thought she would see the Crescent Moon Masked Guest far more often.

But instead, she had no chance to see him. They had become members of the Ghost Shadow Division, and he had entered the Valley of Evil Men, their dwelling place.

Living busy lives as they were, she simply had no opportunity to meet him. She had tried strolling around the cult grounds for no reason, and even gone near the Valley of Evil Men, but she could not meet him.

Nor could she enter the Valley of Evil Men just to meet him.

“Stop looking, he is not there.”

The one who whispered to her was Ogyo, swinging her sword nearby. She, who was closest to Sipchilgyo, knew that Sipchilgyo liked the Masked Guest.

“They say the Masked Guests hardly ever come out of the Valley of Evil Men.”

Perhaps that was why. Even passing by, they had never met any Masked Guests.

“He might have taken off his mask and be watching me right now.”

Could the Crescent Moon Masked Guest be among those people?

“Wake up from your dream!”

Ogyo had unknowingly raised her voice, and Chairan heard the sound.

Ogyo, meeting eyes with Chairan, bowed her head in apology and immersed herself in training.

Thus the arduous training ended.

Some of the onlookers clapped and even cheered.

They dared to do so only because they did not yet know these women. Moreover, since they were only practicing basic swordsmanship, they looked easy to handle.

Depending on one’s perspective, this could have been offensive, but no one paid it any mind.

Rather, they enjoyed their gazes and reactions. These were people they could all kill if they so wished, being assassins. Then the one who clapped loudest would die first.

Just as they caught their breath and were about to disperse, Chairan called out to Sipchilgyo.

“Shall we talk?”

“Yes!”

The two walked out of the training ground, conversing.

“Have you met him again?”

Before coming here to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, during the World’s Greatest Beauty Contest, Chairan had sent her to meet the Masked Guest. Of course, it was Geom Mugik who had kept his promise to arrange their meeting, and Chairan had merely sent her, but at that time, Chairan learned of her own relationship with the Crescent Moon Masked Guest.
