“No, I have not met him yet.”

Sipchilgyo spoke words she did not mean.

“I do not think now is the time to be thinking of men.”

How could Chairan not know that those words were not sincere?

As time passed like this, her heart would cool, and it would simply flow away into memories. Just as she herself thought of Akgunhak.

“Go find him.”

“What?”

“Go to the Valley of Evil Men and find him. You belong to an organization directly under the Young Cult Leader. That side is directly under the Demon Venerable. We have no reason to kneel before them.”

Sipchilgyo looked at Chairan with a surprised face. Did people change when their positions changed? She had never expected Chairan to give such advice.

“Will you go with me?”

“No. I can go alone.”

Sipchilgyo found courage she did not have. Since Chairan had personally given such advice, she could not just stay still.

“I will go right away.”

Chairan stopped her as she rushed.

“Go to my room and open the wardrobe; there is an azure palace dress. Wear that and go. It will suit you well.”

“How dare I?”

“Why? Shall I go with you and dress you?”

“No!”

“Go on.”

“Yes.”

Sipchilgyo bowed politely and ran off, then turned back from afar.

“Thank you so much.”

Bowing at the waist, Sipchilgyo ran off again.

Even with just a glance, her excited heart could be felt.

Yes, that is what it means to like someone. When you truly like someone, it shows like that.

Chairan looked up at the sky.

A blue sky without a single cloud. Should I have a drink with Ilgyo later?

Then someone spoke from behind her.

“People who push other couples together usually end up drinking alone, after all.”

Startled, she turned around to see Geom Mugik standing there with a smile.

Truly, no one could match this man when it came to startling people.

“Even though you cannot handle your own affairs.”

At Geom Mugik’s provocation, Chairan recalled past events instead of greeting him.

“Do you remember? That day by the lakeside where that child and the Masked Guest met, you said, Young Cult Leader—you said you would help that child elope, and that you would find my love too. Why have you not found it? Please find my love.”

Geom Mugik could sense it. That she was comfortable enough to make such jokes. It also meant she was adapting well to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“If I find him for you? You will not even be able to confess that you like him.”

“Why do you think I cannot? Our Young Cult Leader still does not know me well.”

At her boasting, Geom Mugik put on a mischievous yet meaningful smile.

Then Chairan tilted her head.

“But your skin?”

As a genius of cosmetics, she immediately felt that Geom Mugik’s skin had become clearer and cleaner.

“What did you apply? You smell good too? And now that I look, your shoulders seem a bit broader as well.”

“Our Squad Leader Cha will survive well in the organization.”

Geom Mugik brushed it off as mere flattery, but Chairan felt it was not.

*This person, something has changed.*

Geom Mugik changed the subject.

“I heard you have been teaching swordsmanship to your subordinates these days.”

“I am only teaching the basics for now. But since my main martial art is not swordsmanship, I am worried about the future.”

Then Geom Mugik brought up something unexpected.

“It just so happens. I know a good swordsmanship teacher.”

At his words, Chairan sent him an expectant gaze.

“It is a teacher who taught swordsmanship at a martial academy. He has produced very excellent disciples.”

Naturally, disappointment flashed across Chairan’s face.

“No matter what, even if my main martial art is not swordsmanship, would I not be better than some martial academy teacher?”

“Are you looking down on a martial academy teacher?”

Chairan said with a laugh.

“At my level, I can look down on them a bit.”

“Too harsh. The martial academy teacher listening will feel hurt.”

Geom Mugik turned around as if there were someone behind the building.

“Please bear with it, broad-minded teacher.”

Chairan naturally thought Geom Mugik was joking. Someone who knew her and her subordinates’ skills would never bring some martial academy teacher to instruct them.

Chairan looked in the direction Geom Mugik was looking and spoke.

“Go back! We are women you cannot handle. As you can see, we are rude and hopeless.”

“Do not be like that, this is a teacher I went to great lengths to invite.”

Geom Mugik walked in that direction, looking behind the building.

“Please do not be too disappointed. These days, disciples looking down on their teachers is nothing new, is it?”

Chairan walked in that direction too. Had it not been Geom Mugik, she would never have indulged such a joke.

“Just which martial academy produced this person……”

The moment she turned the corner and looked in the direction Geom Mugik was looking!

An exclamation of shock burst from Chairan’s lips. Truly, a shriek escaped her unbidden.

There stood Akgunhak, his hair tied tightly, barefoot. It was truly Akgunhak. The very one she had longed to see so much.

“This is the one who taught at Hwangryong Martial Academy. I studied under him, and your Daeju studied under him, and this squad leader studied under him. Would these not make excellent disciples?”

Geom Mugik’s words did not even reach Chairan’s ears. Her heart pounded as if it would burst, and her cheeks and ears flushed red.

“……What, how, why?”

Chapter 770: I Went to See You Again Today
------------------------------------------
Had she ever been this flustered in her life?

Cha Iran could say with certainty.

This was the first time she had ever been so flustered that her face burned, so hot she could feel the heat herself.

When her face flushed crimson, the Sword King felt apologetic. He had known full well that Geom Mugeuk would tease her, yet he had played along with him.

—You must wait here without moving. You can’t come out halfway.

—What prank are you trying to pull now!

He had let himself be dragged along as if he had no choice, but inwardly, he had been curious. Did she truly like him? Geom Mugeuk had repeated it several times and made such a fuss about it that it did not seem like mere nonsense.

And seeing her reaction now, he realized that she really did like him. It was unexpected. All they had done was exchange greetings and a bit of light conversation now and then. She had liked him?

“It has been a while.”

At the Sword King’s greeting, Cha Iran forced herself to steady her heart and calm down.

“It has been a while.”

An unmistakable tremor could be heard in her voice. Even as she greeted him, only one thought filled her mind.

‘How much of what I said earlier did he hear? Surely he didn’t hear everything?’

She had shouted so loudly about finding her love. Please say you didn’t hear it! This was all the Young Cult Leader’s scheme. If it had been anyone but the Sword King, she would have grabbed him by the collar and shaken him back and forth.

“I never thought I would see you here.”

“Nor did I.”

“How did this happen?”

At Cha Iran’s question, the Sword King could not answer. How could he briefly explain the circumstances that had brought him all the way to the main headquarters of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

Instead of the Sword King, who hesitated to answer, Geom Mugeuk spoke for him.

“He came to our cult because I said you wanted to see him.”

Thinking that he was teasing her rather than Ak Gunhak, she snapped.

“Please stop making fun of me already!”

He had brought Ak Gunhak here and still kept playing dumb. And now he was trying to carry the joke to the very end.

Yet it seemed it was not entirely a joke. The Sword King cut in and spoke calmly.

“Coming to see you was one of the reasons I came to the Divine Cult.”

At those words, her heart, which had just settled, began to pound again. Her heart was telling her that she did indeed like him.

Of course, the Sword King did not reveal any private feelings.

“You and I are the only ones who survived, so I thought we ought to meet at least once.”

At those words, Cha Iran realized that of the Dark Palace Lord’s twelve subordinates, only Ak Gunhak and she had survived.

“In the end, they all died.”

Her gaze turned back to Geom Mugeuk. Back when they had been carrying out various operations in the Dark Palace, the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Leader had not even been on their radar.

Yet he had gradually become the center of every incident, and in the end, everyone except her and the Sword King had been swept away by his hand. She had truly never imagined that all of them would fall not to the Cult Leader, but to the Young Cult Leader.

‘You truly are someone who knows no impossibility.’

On top of that, he had kept his promise. The promise to save her love. He had brought him out from the terrifying grasp of the Dark Palace Lord and made him stand before her.

“Are you hurt anywhere?”

The Sword King shook his head.

“I am fine. Are you all right?”

“Yes. I’m perfectly fine.”

Once she began speaking with the Sword King, the trembling in her heart actually subsided.

She asked cautiously.

“By any chance, did you hear what I said earlier?”

The Sword King nodded. Since things had come to this anyway, she could not keep blushing in embarrassment forever.

“It was just a joke I often played with the Young Cult Leader here, so please don’t mind it.”

Then Geom Mugeuk cut in.

“Won’t you regret that?”

Geom Mugeuk knew. If this fluttering moment ended just like this today, it would not be easy to create such an atmosphere again. Because her face would never turn this red again.

“I’m asking if you won’t regret it later. I should have told him how I felt back then. Since our relationship was going to end like this anyway, why did I hide my feelings so much? It was only a matter of being embarrassed once. No, in truth, being unable to convey your feelings is what’s embarrassing.”

Cha Iran could not refute him. Because she felt certain she might indeed come to regret it that way.

“Why are you trying so hard to bring us together?”

“Because both of you are good people.”

At that moment, the Sword King cut in.

“That is a lie. He is doing this because he enjoys seeing us flustered. So he can keep teasing us.”

“Brother Ak! That is a misunderstanding.”

“What misunderstanding!”

Cha Iran asked Geom Mugeuk.

“You said I was a good person, right? Tell me three good things about me.”

“……”

“I knew it.”

Cha Iran stood side by side with the Sword King and formed a united front.

“So we were dragged all the way here for the Young Cult Leader’s momentary amusement and pleasure.”

“If we do not keep our wits about us, we will be played by the Young Cult Leader’s schemes again.”

Geom Mugeuk said with an incredulous look.

“I did mean to bring you two together, but not like this.”

The three of them smiled at the same time. After exchanging such banter, the mood relaxed, and the awkwardness and embarrassment disappeared as well.

Cha Iran’s gaze turned to the Sword King.

“The truth is, I survived because of you. You saved my life.”

At her words, the Sword King asked in surprise.

“What do you mean?”

Cha Iran’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“The Young Cult Leader found out that I liked you, and so he did not kill me.”

“!”

The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk in astonishment.

“Is that true?”

“Brother Ak might like her too, no? No matter how much of an enemy she was, how could I kill someone Brother Ak might like?”

At those words, the Sword King shook his head as if he were impossible to deal with. It must surely have been a situation where lives were on the line.

“Even in the midst of all that, the Young Cult Leader kept trying to let you leave freely somehow. To be honest, I could not understand it. If he liked you that much, he should have held on to you, so why was he trying to let you go? In truth, I still don’t understand it even now.”

Geom Mugeuk said to her.

“Now you will be able to understand. Because the operation has changed. Back then, the operation was to restore this man’s freedom, but now we will carry out an operation to tie him tightly to our side.”

At those words, Cha Iran’s heart trembled slightly. If she could stay with him, there would be nothing more she could ask for.

“Are you holding a strategy meeting while the target can hear everything?”

“Would he be someone we could fool even if we tried? From now on, our operation is to break that man’s wings and cling to his trouser legs. I will grab his left leg, so you grab his right. We pour in both friendship and affection. If that still doesn’t work, once Lord Blade Demon and the Sword Venerable return, we will even pull on that horsetail hair of his.”

To say such things openly in front of the Sword King? To tell her to cling to a man’s leg? Yet she did not feel displeased at all. The Sword King was smiling as well. Truly, in this murim, this was something only Geom Mugeuk could do.

“Fortunately, our Brother Ak will be staying at the cult for the time being. So what I said earlier about teaching martial arts was not a joke either.”

He would not teach her his exclusive martial arts, but even if he only taught basic swordsmanship, it would make her far stronger than if she learned some ordinary secret manual.

“Will you truly teach swordsmanship to me and my subordinates?”

At Cha Iran’s cautious question, Ak Gunhak smiled and answered.

“If you do not mind having me as a martial arts instructor.”

* * *

Ian was training alone in martial arts.

Her sword flowed smoothly like water, then erupted violently like lava. No matter the situation, her eyes never left the tip of her blade.

Right now, she was fighting an imaginary enemy, and after achieving great mastery in the Flying Heaven Sword Art, she had begun practicing imaginary combat in earnest.

The more she grew, the stronger the enemy in her imagination became. She exerted the fullest imagination she was capable of.

That was why this current battle could not possibly be a graceful one.

Dodging the enemy’s attacks, dodging again, even rolling across the ground to avoid them—at a glance, the sight might have seemed comical.

But Ian was serious. She did not employ a sloppy imagination.

She knew that if she trained her imagination incorrectly, it could instead become harmful.

If her body grew accustomed to attacks she could block comfortably, then in real combat, she would fail to block an enemy’s attack.

After sweeping through the training ground in such a bout, she took a deep breath and raised her blade forward. The enemy in her imagination had not yet fallen and was still staring at her.

Then a welcome voice came from behind her.

“Isn’t this too unfair?”

The instant she heard those words, Ian’s expression brightened radiantly.

“You’re already this beautiful, and if you train hard on top of that, you’re too perfect, aren’t you?”

“Young Master!”

When she turned around, Geom Mugeuk was standing there. After quietly gazing at him, she smiled brightly and said,

“Young Master! Congratulations on your body transformation!”

Geom Mugeuk looked surprised.

“Who did you hear that from? Did you meet Father?”

“No, I could tell just by looking.”

Ian had immediately noticed the change in Geom Mugeuk. She had served as his guard since childhood. She perceived changes in his body even more accurately than Geom Mugeuk himself.

“You’ve changed this much. How could I not notice?”

The change was great enough for her to recognize at once that he had undergone body transformation.

“You’re not very surprised.”

“You’re right. Why am I not surprised?”

Ian widened her eyes and tilted her head.

“I suppose I just thought it would be natural for you to undergo body transformation, Young Master. You’re the kind of person who seems like you could step out for a moment, come back, and say, ‘I just saved murim from destruction.’”

As Ian smiled softly, Geom Mugeuk suddenly pulled her into his arms. In the past, he had given her piggyback rides, and she had run to him and hugged him, but lately such things had become rare.

“I missed you, Ian.”

Because he held her tighter than usual, Ian also hugged Geom Mugeuk with all her strength. It must be very hard for you, Young Master.

“Ah, this embrace feels so unfamiliar. Your chest is broader, and you’ve grown taller too. It feels like I’m cheating on you.”

At Ian’s joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed. Yes, there was a fluttering feeling and a comfort that only Ian could give him.

“I heard my body gives off a pleasant scent now?”

“This scent is very nice too, but I think I liked the scent your body originally had better, Young Master.”

“You are probably the only person who would say the original scent is better than the scent of a transformed body.”

“But it’s true.”

Then Ian flinched in surprise and quickly pulled away from Geom Mugeuk’s arms.

“Ah! I smell like sweat.”

She had briefly forgotten that she had just finished training in martial arts.

“Since you brought it up, let me ask. Are you always training in martial arts whenever I come? You should be using the martial arts you worked so hard to learn to beat up some bastard you don’t like, or drinking and making a drunken scene, or suddenly going off on a trip somewhere. I told you to live faithfully to your desires!”

“Wanting to become stronger than anyone else. That is my greatest desire.”

“Then what is your second desire? Your third desire!”

“My next desires are desires I can only fulfill once the first one has been achieved.”

“So I’m asking what they are!”

But Ian never did tell Geom Mugeuk in the end. She smiled and changed the subject.

“Now then, since the Young Cult Leader has returned, I will summon the Ghost Shadow Unit.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded and answered.

“Good. As it happens, there is someone I want to introduce.”

* * *

Sipchilgyo was wearing Cha Iran’s blue palace gown.

Cha Iran’s eye for what colors suited her was precise. She was incomparably more beautiful than when she wore martial attire.

Sipchilgyo took a deep breath, then walked into the Valley of Villains.

She had come near this place before, but she had never once entered inside.

There were no warriors guarding the entrance, but there were gazes looking down at her. Faceless Ones were sitting on the tree branches on both sides of the entrance. Some were crouching, and some were seated comfortably.

Passing by those who watched the entrance, she continued walking inward. She was truly more nervous than when carrying out an assassination mission.

She hoped she might happen to meet the crescent-moon Faceless One while walking like this, but that was out of the question.

Far more Faceless Ones were moving about than when they had previously carried out an operation together. Back then, only the elites had come, but now all of them were here. There were so many masked people that she felt dizzy.

In the end, she gave up searching directly and spoke to a passing Faceless One.

“May I ask you a favor?”

The Faceless One stared at her, then nodded.

“I’m looking for someone. I would like to meet the person with a crescent moon drawn on his mask.”

Then the Faceless One answered.

“Wait here.”

He leaped away. At times, they felt truly bizarre, yet at times like this, the Faceless Ones seemed more normal than anyone.

A short while later, a Faceless One appeared there. It was the crescent-moon Faceless One.

As if he had not expected her to come all the way here, the two eyes behind his mask were filled with surprise.

Sipchilgyo was truly nervous, but since she had already mustered the courage to come this far, she pushed through to the end.

“I know it’s rude to come so suddenly like this… but I wanted to see you. Since you didn’t come to see me, I came to see you myself.”

There were still many gazes watching from around them. But she was not embarrassed.

Then unexpected words flowed from the crescent-moon Faceless One’s mouth.

“I did go to see you. Today as well.”

The moment she heard those words, Sipchilgyo was startled. Soon, she understood. This man had been among those watching earlier. He truly had removed his mask and come to see her.

Sipchilgyo was deeply moved. The words “today as well” meant he had come on other days too. That emotion led to another surge of courage.

“Please show me your home.”

The crescent-moon Faceless One nodded.

The two walked into the Valley of Villains.

At the same time, one of the Faceless Ones who had been watching them used his lightness skill and shot inward like an arrow. He was the one who had heard the crescent-moon Faceless One’s voice transmission.

—Hurry and go clean the house first!

Chapter 771: My Nickname Is That Guy from Evil Valley
-----------------------------------------------------
Jihan stood before the Gwiyeongdae building, waiting for Geomugeuk.

"Wait a moment. Soon, you'll meet the leader of the organization you're about to enter."

"Yes, I understand."

"Though you were once a leader yourself, depending on the chief's evaluation, you may have to start as an ordinary member."

"I am prepared for that."

After making him wait, Geomugeuk headed to the training ground behind Gwiyeongdae.

With a pounding heart, Jihan waited there for the person who would become his leader. What kind of person would they be? Would he be able to adapt well to the organization?

Meanwhile, another thought crossed his mind.

'Still, I once served as the Chuhon Daeju of the Sawangmun. Starting as an ordinary member would leave a rather bitter taste.'

But this was his choice. He would climb back up from the bottom.

Shortly after, a man wearing a blue mask walked over, leading warriors. He was Cheongmyeon, and those following him were the First Unit warriors of Gwiyeong.

The First Unit warriors consisted mainly of those who used swords, and through continuous training, they now possessed the skills and teamwork worthy of being called elite warriors.

Jihan flinched for a moment. He had caught a scent from Cheongmyeon. Not a real smell, but a scent emanating from his qi.

A foul stench had emanated from the Haryong Danju, who had tormented him. Just so, Jihan could smell scents from people's qi.

The scent he caught from Cheongmyeon was precisely:

'The smell of blood!'

Through the scent, he realized what kind of disposition the man possessed. He was no ordinary person.

'So this man is the leader.'

Jihan naturally assumed he was the leader. Someone who revealed such tremendous qi couldn't possibly not be the chief.

The First Unit members of Gwiyeongdae lined up in formation before the building, and Cheongmyeon stood before them.

Cheongmyeon looked at Jihan. When his gaze met the cold eyes behind the mask, Jihan wavered.

'Should I greet him first?'

But with Geomugeuk not present, it was rather ambiguous to greet him first. What could he say?

Just then, Second Commander Seojin entered, leading her subordinates.

From her qi came the scent of incense burned at a funeral parlor. It meant she was close to the dead.

Indeed, the ghostly energy surrounding her was extraordinary. Since returning from the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, she had devoted herself to ghost arts training and had now grown noticeably stronger.

The Second Unit warriors following her were versed in ghost arts. Of course, she hadn't passed on the secret ghost arts exclusive to the Ghost Gate. What she taught was common ghost arts known by all who practiced them.

Creating smoke to hide, or making ghost objects to startle people.

In this way, Seojin differentiated her Second Unit warriors from the others.

'Could this woman be the leader?'

Given Seojin's qi and the unique atmosphere of her subordinates, she too felt like a leader.

Jihan's gaze turned to Cheongmyeon.

'Then who is this masked man?'

He didn't seem like someone who would serve under another.

Then Cheongmyeon and Seojin greeted each other with cupped hands. They seemed to be of equal rank, so Jihan grew more confused inwardly.

Then Chairan appeared, leading assassins. Following the First and Second Units, the Third Unit of Gwiyeongdae had arrived.

The moment he saw her, Jihan knew.

'It's this woman!'

No matter how strong Cheongmyeon was or how special Seojin was, neither displayed the presence Chairan did. Moreover, she was far too beautiful. He had never seen such a beautiful woman in his life.

Moreover, the women following her also possessed outstanding beauty, and they too felt far from ordinary in skill.

Jihan hurriedly averted his gaze and looked forward. He had nearly stared at Chairan in a daze. From Chairan came a bewitching scent that stirred men.

"Have you arrived?"

"I'm a little late."

After exchanging greetings with Cheongmyeon, Chairan also greeted Seojin. Seojin sensed her mood.

"Has something good happened?"

"Me?"

"Yes, you seem to be in a good mood."

"Nothing in particular. Is it because the weather is nice?"

She feigned ignorance, but Chairan was in a better mood than ever. Akgunhak had come, and she had been promised basic swordsmanship training from him. At the thought of being able to see him frequently from now on, her steps were light and a smile formed naturally at her lips.

The sight of them greeting each other as equals confused Jihan even more.

'Is this woman not the leader either?'

How could someone like that not be the leader? No, in fact, she had such presence that he could believe her to be a branch leader, or even a sect leader.

'Ah, I see. It's a gathering of leaders!'

Jihan became convinced of this. Cheongmyeon and Seojin both possessed qi worthy of being leaders.

Then two people walked out from behind the building.

Jihan's gaze, which had been relieved at the sight of Geomugeuk, fixed as if nailed upon Ian, who emerged with him.

The moment he saw her, Jihan lost his soul. Truly, he had never seen such a beautiful woman. He had thought the same when seeing Chairan, but no. This woman was the first.

From her came an incredibly pleasant scent. Whenever he met a good-natured person, he caught exactly that scent. It made her beauty shine even brighter.

Geomugeuk stood before the Gwiyeongdae warriors.

"It's been a while. Have you all been well?"

"Yes! It's been a while, Young Cult Leader!"

The Gwiyeongdae warriors' resounding voices echoed.

Geomugeuk walked up to Cheongmyeon.

"You've improved greatly in the meantime?"

Geomugeuk could tell he had grown stronger just by looking at him.

"The leader has taken great care of me."

After achieving the Bicheon Sword Arts, Ian had taken great care of Cheongmyeon. His entry into Gwiyeongdae wasn't solely because of Geomugeuk. Surely, he himself was part of the reason. So just as with Geomugeuk and the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, he had become a martial arts friend, competing in martial prowess. That was how Ian looked after him.

"I am learning more from the First Commander."

Only then did Jihan realize that the woman who had come out with Geomugeuk was the leader of Gwiyeongdae.

'So this woman is the one who will become my leader.'

He had never imagined she would be so young and beautiful. What was even more surprising were the other three.

'They are mere commanders!'

He was truly surprised. He had no idea it was such an organization. Feeling bitter about starting as an ordinary member? When even people like these were merely commanders of this organization?

Geomugeuk formally introduced Jihan to Ian.

"A friend I made during my recent travels. Whether to accept him into Gwiyeongdae is entirely your decision."

Jihan respectfully cupped his hands and greeted Ian. His fate now lay in this woman's hands.

"I am Jihan."

"I am Ian, leader of Gwiyeongdae."

"It is an honor to meet you."

With everyone's eyes focused on him, Jihan grew even more nervous.

"Where are you from?"

"I hail from Sawangmun, where I served as the Chuhon Daeju. When it comes to pursuing enemies, I am second to none."

"Do you know what kind of organization Gwiyeongdae is?"

"I do not."

"It is a direct subordinate organization for the Young Cult Leader here."

At those words, passion welled up in Jihan's chest.

He had followed Geomugeuk alone to the Cheonma Divine Cult. He had already heard they might not care for him once he arrived. Entering an organization distant from the Young Cult Leader would mean barely seeing him properly, but a direct subordinate organization of the Young Cult Leader!

Ian asked the question she usually asked in such situations.

"Let me ask you one thing. If the Young Cult Leader's orders conflict with the Cult Leader's orders, which would you follow?"

Jihan seemed to ponder for a moment.

"I believe I should follow the Cult Leader's orders."

It was an unexpected answer; she had expected him to choose the Young Cult Leader's orders.

"What is your reason?"

The reason was simple.

"Isn't it that the Young Cult Leader can only become the Cult Leader if I show myself well to the Cult Leader? While I don't know about other organizations, a direct organization shouldn't do anything that goes against the Cult Leader's mood. Instead, once the Young Cult Leader becomes the Cult Leader, no matter how unreasonable an order he gives, I believe I must follow it absolutely."

After answering thus, he added with an embarrassed expression.

"Honestly, I still don't know much about the Divine Cult, and I had no intention of joining before meeting the Young Cult Leader. I came here solely because of my connection with the Young Cult Leader. If I can simply serve him, I want nothing else. Please have me do anything. I will devote my full loyalty."

Geomugeuk said to Jihan.

"You make it sound impressive, but ultimately you're saying you won't listen to me, right?"

Jihan flustered.

"That is not it. That's not—"

Then Ian intervened.

"That's exactly why I like it. Later, if there's ever a need to defy the Young Cult Leader's orders, he'd be perfect to use as a scapegoat. Jihan was stubborn, so we couldn't help it."

Jihan grew even more flustered. Saying such things right in front of the person!

After thoroughly enjoying his flustered appearance, Ian made her decision.

"Jihan, was it?"

"Yes."

"I appoint you as the Fourth Commander of Gwiyeongdae."

Jihan was startled by the words "Fourth Commander."

"I do not know if I am qualified."

Having seen the three commanders earlier, he truly wondered if he deserved to stand shoulder to shoulder with them.

"Yes, I don't know either."

"Then why did you appoint me as a commander?"

"Because I received the Young Cult Leader's recommendation. He must have brought you here because you're capable enough to handle the position."

Jihan could feel it. Her absolute faith in the Young Cult Leader.

"But don't be too moved. A commander is just a title when there isn't anyone in the Fourth Unit yet."

Jihan instead saw that as an advantage.

"Does that not mean I can choose the subordinates I want?"

Ian smiled and looked at Geomugeuk. Yes, this was why he had recommended someone so smart and bold to her.

"Going forward, grow the Fourth Unit into an organization specialized in pursuit arts, utilizing your expertise."

Jihan was overjoyed. Could there be anything better for starting a new life in the Cheonma Divine Cult? A direct subordinate organization of the Young Cult Leader, where he could utilize his expertise?

"I will devote my full loyalty."

Geomugeuk smiled pleasantly and looked at Ian and the four commanders standing before him.

"Gwiyeongdae is finally complete."

Frontline fighters of the First Unit, ghost arts users of the Second Unit, assassination specialists of the Third Unit, and pursuit specialists of the Fourth Unit.

It was the moment a balanced Gwiyeongdae was completed in every aspect.

"We'll be deployed to real combat soon. Prepare thoroughly!"

* * *

"The room is incredibly clean."

She couldn't imagine what had transpired before those words left Sipchilgyo's mouth.

The mask of the Masked Guest who had rushed over upon receiving the message was painted with a rabbit. Perhaps because things were falling into place, this junior, who possessed the fastest footwork, happened to be nearby. Not just any Masked Guest could paint a symbol representing themselves on their mask. Only the old-timers with accumulated skill and years of service could do so.

The Rabbit Masked Guest opened the window and started ventilating. He threw all the clothes piled on the bed and table underneath the bed.

He ran over to ask a neighboring Masked Guest for help.

"Hurry! I need tea, tea utensils, and incense! Also food and good wine!"

"What on earth is going on?"

"Senior Crescent Moon is coming with a woman."

Those words were enough. The neighboring Masked Guest quickly gathered everything asked for.

He lit incense in the room. All leftover dishes were moved next door. He swept the floor and wiped away the dust. Both demonstrated their finest footwork and movement arts.

By the time the two finished cleaning and came out, the Crescent Moon Masked Guest and Sipchilgyo were walking from afar.

Just then, another Masked Guest passed by the house and said with a displeased look.

"What? A woman here?"

Swish.

By the time Sipchilgyo looked over at his voice, the one who had nearly ruined the atmosphere had already been spirited away by the two Masked Guests.

As the two continued walking this way, this time a Masked Guest appeared scratching his rear, wearing only a mask with his top off. As he walked while yawning—

Swish.

He too disappeared as if kidnapped.

Only thanks to such desperate efforts were the two able to arrive at the house while having a calm conversation. They could even hear such words from her.

"Somehow, I had a feeling the room would be clean."

The clothes protruding from Crescent Moon's bed were promptly shoved back underneath with a foot. The garments that had been strewn on the bed were now piled beneath it.

Truly, it was a miracle to have cleaned this much in such a short time.

"A good smell is coming from the room."

"Is that so? I don't notice it."

“I’m thirsty. If you have even cold tea, please give me some.”

“Wait a moment.”

Did he have tea at home? He drank liquor at home, not tea. He could only hope there was something he’d received and put away somewhere.

When the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest opened the cupboard, there was a tea canister.

An unfamiliar tea canister and tea utensils. Beside them was a bottle of liquor, and there were snacks as well. That fellow—he’d even thought of this in the meantime.

The Faceless Guest brewed tea and brought it out with the snacks.

“The tea is good, and the sweets are good too.”

“I’m glad.”

Seeing how pleased she was put the Crescent Moon martial artist in a good mood as well.

“I’ve now dated every man in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“What do you mean?”

“That’s what people will say when they see me, won’t they? Isn’t that the woman from the Valley of Villains? From now on, my nickname will be ‘that girl from the Valley of Villains.’”

After all, she was probably the first woman to come to the Valley of Villains because she liked a Faceless Guest. Sipchilgyo mustered even more of that courage.

“I want to see the face beneath your mask.”

Because she couldn’t like someone whose face she didn’t even know. Even now, if someone heard, they would call her a madwoman. How could she like him so much without knowing who might come out from under that mask?

“I fear you may be disappointed.”

“The longer you put it off, the greater that disappointment will become.”

She was right. He couldn’t go on forever without showing her his face. The Crescent Moon Faceless Guest slowly took off his mask.

A young man appeared from beneath it. Judging only by the way he marked his mask with blood, she had thought he would look like a rugged manly man, but his features were gentler than she had expected.

And the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest had not lied. It really was a face she had seen during training. Among all those grim-looking demon practitioners watching, this face had stood out.

And, astonishingly, she had thought this:

If only it were him.

Sipchilgyo said to him,

“I’m disappointed.”

Just as the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest was flustered—

“I was really nervous, you know. What if you were so ugly I couldn’t handle it? What if some uncle way too old came out?”

Were you going to date me with that face? She had been even more worried because she wasn’t the kind of person who could say such a thing.

“Do you know what I even thought? I’d still go. I’d go with you to the end. Even if you were ugly, I’d go; even if you were twice my age, I’d go. I had steeled myself that firmly.”

“And?”

“You’re far too handsome, aren’t you?”

Relief settled into the tense eyes of the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest.

After gazing quietly at Sipchilgyo, he suddenly said,

“I will take responsibility. I will take responsibility for that girl from the Valley of Villains.”

At the Crescent Moon Faceless Guest’s words, Sipchilgyo’s face brightened radiantly.

Yes, she had fallen for the manliness he had shown when he saved her. Though he looked like a boy, his eyes were truly those of a man.

“You people over there heard that too, right?”

She walked to the window and stuck out her head.

Below the window, four Faceless Guests were hiding. They were the Rabbit Faceless Guest who had come running after receiving the voice transmission, the next-door Faceless Guest who had brought the tea and snacks, the Faceless Guest who had asked why a woman had come, and the bare-chested Faceless Guest. In the next-door Faceless Guest’s hand was a scented candle.

In truth, Sipchilgyo had known for a while that four people were hiding outside the window. As a former assassin, how could she fail to sense the presence of people hiding so carelessly?

“He said he would take responsibility for me. The four of you are witnesses.”

Among the masks looking up at her awkwardly, she discovered one with a drawing on it.

“Rabbit-nim, you definitely heard that, right?”

Chapter 772: For Giving Birth to You and Tying Your Fate to Mine
----------------------------------------------------------------
After parting from the Ghost Shadow Squad, Geom Mugeuk returned to his quarters.

Twelve guards, including the chief guard Jeok Yeon, were waiting for him in full uniform.

“Young Cult Leader, welcome back.”

They stood in formation before his residence and greeted him in unison. Their ringing voices were filled with joy; ever since he had called each of them by name and drunk with them last time, their respect for the Young Cult Leader had deepened even further.

“Have you all been training hard?”

Jeok Yeon answered confidently on everyone’s behalf.

“We have been training diligently for the day we may serve you, Young Cult Leader.”

Indeed, several among the guards had advanced to the next stage in their martial arts, and a few others were in the midst of that transformation.

In the process of discerning their levels, Geom Mugeuk confirmed that his own abilities had improved even more. He could assess their realms with greater detail and accuracy than before.

On the other hand, among the guards, the first to notice the change in Geom Mugeuk was Jeok Yeon.

“But Young Cult Leader, it seems something about you has changed.”

Jeok Yeon had achieved great mastery in the Ghost Eye Art, which allowed him to detect those in hiding. Naturally, he noticed Geom Mugeuk’s change faster than anyone else.

“I suppose my growth spurt still isn’t over. I keep getting taller.”

He said it like a joke, but Jeok Yeon clearly sensed the change. Even so, he did not go so far as to think Geom Mugeuk had undergone rebirth.

“It’s been a while since we met, so I should see how much your skills have improved, shouldn’t I?”

Geom Mugeuk had them display their martial arts. One by one, he corrected their flawed movements.

He pointed out exactly one thing for each subordinate, but how could that one thing be anything ordinary?

The guards were moved once again by his instruction. The Young Cult Leader was the one they were to serve, and at the same time, he was their master.

When the individual lessons were over, Geom Mugeuk said to them,

“Guard duty is good, and training is good too, but don’t forget this. Right now is the brightest time of your lives. Later on, you’ll have to stand guard until you’re sick of it, so for now, find hobbies, meet women, take vacations and travel. Live that way. I don’t want you to spend your whole lives standing guard and then die.”

They heard such words every time, but even so, they could not neglect their training. They wanted to stand proudly at the Young Cult Leader’s side as soon as possible. The more they heard words like these, the sooner they wanted to serve him.

Geom Mugeuk took a bamboo tube from his leather pouch.

“I’m going to the Thousand Poisons Forest now. Prepare to go out.”

He deliberately took the guards with him. They were waiting for him like this, even dressed in uniform. Even if they could not accompany him outside the cult, they should at least accompany him at times like this.

Geom Mugeuk led the guards as he walked through the cult grounds. Passing martial artists greeted him respectfully as they went by. The guards’ chests naturally swelled with pride.

He even brought the guards into the Thousand Poisons Forest.

“It’s your first time in the Thousand Poisons Forest, isn’t it?”

“Yes.”

“If you lose your way here, you can assume you’re as good as dead. It’s vast, and deadly poisonous creatures swarm everywhere.”

The guards walked tensely, pressing close to Geom Mugeuk.

Fortunately, Geom Mugeuk knew the complex paths of the Thousand Poisons Forest better than anyone.

Whenever they came across a poisonous creature he recognized, he explained it to the guards.

“See that snake coiled around the branch over there? That’s a poisonous snake called the Flower-Willow Snake. It’s an essential ingredient in making Flower Poison. Jeok Yeon! There’s a poisonous insect on your shoulder!”

“Please don’t joke abo—aaagh!”

Geom Mugeuk flicked away the poisonous insect that had climbed onto his shoulder with a finger. Everyone pressed even closer around Geom Mugeuk.

Meanwhile, they arrived at the Poison King’s residence. After having his subordinates wait outside, he entered.

The Poison King happened to be mixing poison in the front yard of his laboratory.

“Poison King! I’m here!”

But the Poison King was so focused on his work that he did not even lift his head.

“I knew you would be like this, so I prepared this. You have no idea how hard I struggled to obtain this fellow. Even while fighting to the death against my enemies, I thought only of you, Poison King, and worked hard to protect this poison tube. Poison King, are you listening to me?”

Of course, the Poison King did not so much as pretend to hear him and remained absorbed in his work. He was not deliberately pretending not to hear; he was truly immersed.

Geom Mugeuk slightly opened the stopper of the bamboo tube.

Rustle, rustle.

As the smell of rotting corpses flowed from the tube, the Poison King reacted. Only then did he raise his head.

“This smell? Could it be?”

The Poison King set down what he was doing and rushed over to Geom Mugeuk, peering inside the poison tube.

Inside was a poisonous insect that resembled a centipede. When he had caught it beneath Mount Cheonmok, he had put it away to bring to the Poison King, and he had not forgotten.

“It’s a Corpse-Blood Poison Centipede!”

The Poison King’s face lit up brightly. At that sight, Geom Mugeuk could only shake his head.

‘Our Poison King, who knows nothing but poison!’

The Poison King took out the Corpse-Blood Poison Centipede with chopsticks. Between the chopsticks, the centipede writhed. It was so repulsive and frightening that merely looking at it was enough, yet the Poison King gazed at it with utter affection.

“See the spot on its belly here? The ones with round spots are females. They’re several times harder to obtain than the males.”

Indeed, a round spot could be seen on its belly. He only knew because the Poison King told him; otherwise, he would never have had any reason to look at the belly of such a terrifying, revolting creature.

“You neglected me all this time because Demon Buddha brought you all those poisonous herbs, didn’t you? Now, the age of Demon Buddha is over, and my age has arrived.”

Just then, a voice came from behind him.

“Why do you keep sending away someone who is standing still?”

Geom Mugeuk turned to see Demon Buddha walking out of the warehouse. He was carrying a large poison jar.

“Why are you here? No, what is that poison jar? Was having you dig up poisonous herbs not enough, so now he’s making you move poison jars too? Were you deceived by the Poison King again and having your labor exploited?”

Then unexpected words flowed from Demon Buddha.

“I am learning poison arts.”

“Pardon?”

Demon Buddha said again,

“I said I am learning poison arts from the Poison King.”

“You, Demon Buddha?”

Demon Buddha nodded.

“As I dug up poisonous herbs, I became interested in poison arts. So I’m learning from the basics.”

Demon Buddha learning poison arts was truly something he had never imagined.

“If you start using poison arts too, Demon Buddha, who is supposed to stop you?”

“I’m not learning it to use on others. I’m simply studying.”

Demon Buddha set the poison jar he had brought down beside the Poison King and went back into the warehouse. Geom Mugeuk trotted after him.

“Isn’t it tiring?”

“It was tiring when it was my main occupation. What’s tiring about doing something I can quit at any time?”

Like someone who had attained enlightenment in Buddhism, Demon Buddha’s expression was peaceful.

But Geom Mugeuk sensed a hidden torrent beneath that lake-like serenity. Carving stone statues and learning poison arts. Was something boiling within him so fiercely that he had to work this hard to find repose?

“This side of you feels unfamiliar, Demon Buddha.”

Demon Buddha paused his hands for a moment and lightly sat atop a jar. Geom Mugeuk also sat on the jar beside him.

“True. In the past, it is something I would not have done.”

“Then why did you change your mind?”

After a brief pause, Demon Buddha brought up words Geom Mugeuk had once said.

“You said it before. Not to spend my time carving stone statues. To move among people, understand their hearts, persuade them with passion, and fight with all my strength for my goal.”

“Yes, I did.”

“……But it did not go well. Should I say it felt as though the ember in my heart had gone out? A life from which dreams have vanished leaves behind only habitual routine.”

Demon Buddha looked at Geom Mugeuk and asked,

“How can an extinguished ember be revived?”

Yes, how could a person change so easily? He had once been someone who ran around several times harder than ordinary people with those short legs of his.

Geom Mugeuk seemed to ponder for a moment, then suddenly said,

“How can you revive an extinguished ember? Abandon your attachment to the warmth left in those ashes.”

“!”

In that instant, Demon Buddha’s heart sank. It felt as though Geom Mugeuk had struck him at the core. Yes, he was still rummaging through the ashes in search of an ember.

“Now is not the time to light a fire, but the time to chop firewood. Start anew by gathering wood.”

A surge of emotion passed through Demon Buddha’s eyes. He had only thought of reviving the ambition that had passed, never of starting again from the beginning.

“If there was an ambition suited to that time, then there must be an ambition suited to the present as well. And that ambition does not have to be burned up in a roaring blaze to be magnificent. There is also a kind of elegance in stacking firewood high and not lighting it.”

In Geom Mugeuk’s words, Demon Buddha saw the path he had to take.

As long as his feelings for the Eldest Young Master continued, realistically, there would be no way for him to set his ambitions roaring again. Instead, he would continue longing for the ambition that had passed.

But now, Geom Mugeuk was telling him this: do not think of the ember as having gone out; think of it as choosing not to light the fire. Change from a passive way of thinking to an active one.

Demon Buddha had a premonition that this small difference in thought would greatly change his life.

Demon Buddha turned his head and stared intently at Geom Mugeuk. As if he had been waiting for that very moment, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Has nothing about me changed?”

“Did you cut your hair?”

“No, please look more closely.”

“I don’t know.”

His expression showed that he truly did not know, not that he was joking.

“No, you can find poisonous herbs like a ghost, so why can’t you notice my change? Look at my skin. Smell me too.”

“What would I look at a man’s skin for? Disgusting.”

Demon Buddha got down again and went out carrying a poison jar.

“No, I’m asking you to look just once!”

This time, Geom Mugeuk tried asking the Poison King. But the Poison King was absorbed in extracting poison from the Corpse-Blood Poison Centipede Geom Mugeuk had given him. Unless his father came, it seemed impossible to disturb his concentration.

In the end, Geom Mugeuk withdrew without even getting to boast once about his rebirth.

“I’m busy today, so I’ll just pay my respects and be on my way. I’ll visit again.”

After bowing politely, Geom Mugeuk left the place with his subordinates.

Demon Buddha looked up at the sky and said,

“Rebirth, is it? The Young Cult Leader may truly be someone sent by the heavens.”

Demon Buddha had known everything and merely pretended not to.

Only then did the Poison King raise his head in surprise.

“The Young Cult Leader underwent rebirth?”

The Poison King had failed to notice because his attention was on the Corpse-Blood Poison Centipede. Even if that had not been the case, he was also someone who had no interest in others.

Setting the poison jar down beside him, Demon Buddha said,

“We may have to begin preparing soon as well.”

At the Poison King’s gaze, which asked what he meant, Demon Buddha calmly answered,

“The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult of this era possesses the greatest military strength of any generation in history. Do you truly think the Cult Leader will let this opportunity pass?”

The Heavenly Demon, who had achieved grand completion of the Twelve Stars, and the Young Cult Leader, who had achieved grand completion as well. Even the Eight Demon Venerables had each broken through their own limits.

The Cult Leader was not someone who would stack firewood high and leave it unlit.

“The Cult Leader’s dream is drawing closer and closer to reality. The more the Young Cult Leader tries to stop it, the more I feel that fate may move in that direction.”

* * *

After leaving the Thousand Poisons Forest, the place Geom Mugeuk went next was the residence of Cult Leader Pungcheon.

Seeing that he had even brought all his guards with him, Cult Leader Pungcheon flinched in surprise.

“What is this? Have you come to arrest me?”

“If I intended to do that, would these children be enough? All the elites of our cult would have to be mobilized.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon laughed pleasantly, then stiffened his expression. It always began with pleasant talk like this, and by the time he came to his senses, something had been taken from him.

“What have you come to take from me now, when I’m penniless?”

“Take from you? I came because I wanted to see you, Cult Leader.”

“You came to check if I happened to acquire anything in the meantime, didn’t you?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon looked at Geom Mugeuk with suspicious eyes. Then he flinched in surprise.

“You?”

He wondered if Pungcheon had noticed his rebirth.

“You’ve gained weight!”

Then he burst into loud laughter.

“If you don’t manage yourself like me, you put it on in no time.”

Now that he looked, Cult Leader Pungcheon did seem to have lost a little weight. Only a very little, though.

“You’ve lost a great deal of weight. Your features are shining.”

“Does it really look that way?”

At the words that he had lost weight, he gave a pleased smile. Geom Mugeuk’s divine art of flattery was displayed for the first time in a while.

“Even now, when you walk through the cult, do you not feel their gazes?”

“Gazes? What gazes?”

“The gazes of women, of course. They’re only being cautious because this is inside the cult. If you went out to the Central Plains, women would follow you in lines.”

“Oh, there’s no need to go that far.”

“Come out to the Central Plains with me sometime. I’ll prove it to you.”

“You always say that and never do.”

“No. The three of you should go get some fresh air with Strategist Go.”

“That’s a promise.”

“Of course.”

Geom Mugeuk subtly revealed his true colors.

“In that spirit, may I ask one favor of you?”

Cult Leader Pungcheon gave him a look that said, I knew it.

“Call it an ulterior motive, not a favor. So, what is it you want?”

For once, Geom Mugeuk could not get the words out and hesitated.

“For a shameless man like you to hesitate this much, it must be a request I absolutely cannot grant.”

With a sigh, Geom Mugeuk revealed what he wanted to ask.

“There is someone I wish to teach the Space-Time Transposition Art to.”

To his surprise, Cult Leader Pungcheon knew who it was.

“To the Cult Leader?”

Startled, Geom Mugeuk asked him,

“How did you know?”

“You held out quite a long time. I thought you would come asking much sooner.”

He spoke as though he had guessed it from the beginning.

“That’s right. From the start, I wanted to teach it to my father.”

“Then why did you not ask?”

“Because it would be a burden to you, Cult Leader.”

“And now?”

“The situation has changed. Now it is time for their leader to appear. As you know, he is no ordinary man.”

Of course, Cult Leader Pungcheon would also assume that opponent was the Dark Palace Lord.

“So I wish to give my father a hidden trump card.”

He had expected him to say absolutely not.

Yet for some reason, Cult Leader Pungcheon readily agreed.

“Very well. Do so.”

“What? You’re giving me permission?”

“I am.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at Cult Leader Pungcheon in surprise.

“May I ask why you are allowing it so readily?”

Then came an unexpected answer.

“For your father’s sake.”

He had thought it was out of goodwill or respect toward his father, but it was not.

“He brought you into this world and allowed me to form a bond with you, so I suppose I can give him this much of a gift.”

Geom Mugeuk was genuinely moved. Could there be any reason more touching than that?

“When it comes to the Space-Time Transposition Art, I am your disciple, am I not? Since my father will be learning from me, that makes you his grandmaster. From now on, I shall call you Grandmaster! Grandmaster of Space-Time!”

Cult Leader Pungcheon asked with an intrigued expression,

“Do you truly think your father will learn it obediently?”

Considering Geom Ujin’s pride, he would never try to learn it willingly. Teaching him would not be easy either.

“I do have one method in mind.”

“What is it?”

If he said it was to prepare for some unforeseen danger, it would only have the opposite effect. If he said he wanted to take a walk with his father inside the Space-Time Transposition Art, the first thing he would get was mockery.

No complicated plan would work. But there was one way to provoke his father—the simplest and most provocative way.

“How long did you say it took you to master the Space-Time Transposition Art?”

Chapter 773: I Will Not Run Away
--------------------------------
“Do you like him that much?”

Dana was lying on the bed, while Seo Daeryong stood gazing at the baby in his arms. He was a man who had raced home the very moment the Hwangcheongak ended.

“I do. More than words.”

Five days ago, Dana had given birth to a son. Only now did Seo Daeryong realize that bearing a child was no ordinary matter. He had thought it a simple thing—one became pregnant, and one simply gave birth—but only after watching from beside her did he understand how truly difficult it was.

“Stop staring already.”

Despite his wife’s attempts to stop him, Seo Daeryong had no intention of putting down the baby in his arms. This was the same man who, on the first day, had trembled all over in fear that he might drop the child.

“You must eat promptly and go to your martial arts training.”

“I shall stay just a moment longer. Ah, the lad just smiled. That’s right. I am your father.”

Even as he held his son, it felt unreal. To think that he had truly become a father? He could no longer tell whether this was a dream or reality.

“It is truly a blessing he takes after you.”

The boy looked exactly like his mother. He had worried about what he would do if the child resembled him and had a gloomy face.

“I had wanted him to look like you.”

“What are you saying! Had he done so, how could you bear the resentment he would feel later?”

“What is so bad about you?”

“I like things as they are. I am truly glad to have such a handsome son who takes after you.”

“The shape of his mouth looks exactly like yours.”

Seo Daeryong mimicked the baby’s mouth. Seeing the child directly like this was entirely different from when he had been in the womb.

“Son! Our handsome son!”

Then a voice came from behind.

“Already showing off your son?”

Seo Daeryong turned around in surprise to see Geom Mugeuk standing there.

“Young Cult Leader!”

“Congratulations, Daeryong.”

“Young Cult Leader! I have become a father!”

Though he should have first asked whether the Young Cult Leader had returned safely, Seo Daeryong could not contain the surge of emotion that welled up at the sight of him. He felt exactly the same emotion that women felt upon seeing their own mothers.

Geom Mugeuk approached and looked at the baby.

“He is worth showing off.”

“Wouldn’t you say I have succeeded?”

“A great success. What is his name?”

“We have named him Yeongha.”

“Seo Yeongha. It is a good name.”

Geom Mugeuk offered congratulations to Dana as well.

“Lady Dana, my congratulations. You have worked hard.”

“It is all thanks to the Young Cult Leader.”

She meant every word. It was also something her husband said every time he opened his mouth—that if not for the Young Cult Leader, she would never have known such happiness in her life.

Geom Mugeuk handed Seo Daeryong some medicine he had procured.

“I asked a favor of Physician Ma to specially prepare this. It will help your wife with her postpartum recovery.”

“Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Seo Daeryong and Dana bowed respectfully together, expressing their gratitude.

“Would you like to hold him?”

“Yes, I ought to hold him, since he is your son.”

Seo Daeryong spoke quickly as he handed over the baby.

“You must support his neck and head well. Cradle him with your arm like this, and use your other hand to support his waist and bottom. Yes, like that. Relax your strength.”

But as if to show that the worry had been needless, Geom Mugeuk held the baby with practiced ease.

“Surely there is nothing you cannot do? You need not be good at this as well.”

“You speak thus because you do not know. I am expending an enormous amount of internal energy right now.”

Just in case, a soft, cloud-like energy swirled around the baby.

Seo Daeryong spoke to the child.

“Little one, greet him. This is the Young Cult Leader.”

As if he had truly understood those words, the baby broke into a bright smile.

A smile naturally formed at Geom Mugeuk’s lips.

“Yeongha, congratulations on being born to good parents.”

After handing the baby back to Dana, Geom Mugeuk asked Seo Daeryong,

“How does it feel to be a father?”

“I am still bewildered.”

Geom Mugeuk promised Seo Daeryong the greatest gift.

“When the child has grown, I shall open his Ren and Du meridians.”

Seo Daeryong and Dana started in surprise. Opening the Ren and Du meridians required the expenditure of internal energy, and because the process was extremely dangerous, it was never done for anyone outside the family.

“Young Cult Leader! Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

It meant that Geom Mugeuk regarded the child as a true nephew. That was why Seo Daeryong was as overjoyed as when Hyeolcheon Doma had opened his own Ren and Du meridians; no, he was even happier than he had been then.

Even in the midst of it, Seo Daeryong did not forget to make a quip.

“I already cannot repay you with generations of loyalty alone, yet now I receive generations of love.”

It was truly a grace he could never repay, even with his death.

“I shall watch my child grow and find the way that suits him to raise him well.”

If those had been the words of advice given before the child was born, now Geom Mugeuk offered new counsel.

“I once heard this saying: raise your child as though they were a guest. Just as we do not treat guests rudely, treat your child with kindness and respect as you raise them. And just as guests eventually depart, regard your child, too, as someone who will one day leave. It means treat them well, but do not be bound by them.”

Seo Daeryong repeated Geom Mugeuk’s words in his heart.

“I shall keep them in mind and raise him well. But as for this guest, I do not know if I can send him away.”

Yes, that is why everyone ends up telling their fifty-year-old children to be careful of carriages.

“Later, you will say this to me: ‘Young Cult Leader, you have no idea how vast the gap is between theory and reality! Children, I tell you!’”

Seo Daeryong and Dana, who were listening, laughed together.

“Come to think of it, I have been so preoccupied with my child that I failed to ask after my master. I heard he did not return with you this time.”

“He decided to tour the martial world a while longer with the Sword Venerable.”

“Please tell him to take his time.”

“Why? So you can shirk your martial arts training and play with the baby?”

Instead, Seo Daeryong steeled his resolve.

“No, I shall train twice as hard from now on. Ten times as hard.”

Geom Mugeuk believed it—that Seo Daeryong would truly become a good parent.

“I shall take my leave now. Lady Dana, take good care of yourself.”

Seo Daeryong caught him with a regretful hand.

“Young Cult Leader, it has been so long. Please stay for dinner.”

“I have a dinner engagement with my father. I shall be on my way.”

Lastly, Geom Mugeuk greeted the baby nestled in Dana’s arms.

“Our little nephew, welcome to the world. Grow up healthy and strong.”

After Geom Mugeuk left, Seo Daeryong said to the baby,

“Do not grow up to be like your father. Grow up to be like the Young Cult Leader.”

Then Dana spoke firmly.

“No, grow up to be like your father. Mother prefers that by far.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk went to his father’s bedchamber.

He waited in the room for a while as his father changed his clothes.

There was something that caught his eye above all else: a hanging scroll on one wall depicting his older brother and himself. Beside it were the floral pajamas he had gifted, neatly folded.

On the display stand next to that stood dolls. From a doll of his father to a doll of the Demon Venerable, and dolls of Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang.

Had he not grown close to his father, this was something he would never have known for his entire life—that his father was the kind of man to hang such things in his room, wear floral pajamas, and buy these dolls to set out.

At that moment, his father entered the room.

“Some conspiracy is afoot. The painting, and these dolls as well—I think my brother was drawn and made to look more handsome.”

“When coincidences overlap, should we not suspect that they may become fact?”

Geom Mugeuk turned to his father, smiling. How much the man adored his firstborn son.

“I am hungry, Father.”

“Let us go eat.”

Because they had made plans in advance, dinner was already prepared.

“It has been a while since I have had the food of the Cheonmajeon chefs.”

It was a dinner with his father after a long time.

While eating, Geom Mugeuk told his father about what had happened during this expedition. The important matters had already been heard through the Tongcheongak, but he added thrilling flesh to the skeleton with details that only he could provide.

“But did you know that the Saber Demon and the Sword Venerable harbored feelings for one another?”

At that, his father nodded.

“Oh, do not speak of it. The Saber Demon elder cared for the Sword Venerable so much that I was treated like a nuisance.”

“You must have been quite the scoundrel yourself. I consider it fortunate if you did not tease the Demon Venerable.”

“You misunderstand me, Father!”

“So, where did the two of them go?”

“They went to Taihu.”

At the word Taihu, a faint smile formed at Geom Ujin’s lips. He remembered the time when four people, together with Gwonma, had met there in the past.

Sensing that there was some story behind that expression, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Have you been to Taihu?”

When his father nodded, he said,

“Next time, let us all go together with Brother. Let us float a boat on the lake and share a drink with your sons.”

Geom Mugeuk continued. This time, it was about the enemies.

“…They summoned the souls of those they had faced until now at the Ghost Soul Valley. They were able to do this because they gave an unprecedented miraculous medicine called the Cheon-ui Spirit Pill to one who uses Yin techniques.”

He then explained in detail the battle that followed. Of all his accounts, his father showed the greatest interest in the stories of combat and martial arts.

“…And so I finished them off completely.”

“You annihilated them with the Heavenly Demon Annihilation?”

“Yes. Even when their heads were severed, their souls did not vanish.”

“You were able to control the Heavenly Demon Annihilation?”

“Yes, to a certain extent.”

Perhaps thinking that his son had not yet reached that level, his father confirmed again.

“Still, it cannot have been easy to use indoors.”

Now was the moment to speak of the Space-Time Transference Technique.

“That is why I used a secret technique.”

“A secret technique?”

Geom Mugeuk subtly began to recite the mnemonic chant.

“Can you guess what kind of mnemonic chant this is?”

Geom Mugeuk began to recite the mnemonic chant of the Space-Time Transference Technique. After he had finished reciting it to the end,

“Do you recognize it?”

“It seems like a demonic art of the Blood Cult.”

“You have guessed roughly correctly. I shall recite it once more.”

“That is enough. I have already memorized it all.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at his father with a shocked expression.

“You memorized it all in one hearing?”

“Yes.”

“Impossible! I cannot believe it.”

At that, Geom Ujin recited the mnemonic chant exactly from beginning to end.

“You are truly remarkable.”

Furthermore, his father had accurately guessed whose martial art it was.

“Is it the Wind Heaven Cult Leader’s martial art?”

“How did you know?”

“Judging by the mnemonic chant, it is clearly a demonic art of extreme ascension, and in the present age, he is the only one who has mastered a Blood Cult demonic art of that level.”

“That is correct. It is the martial art of the Wind Heaven Cult.”

Truly, when it came to martial arts, his father was born with a heaven-gifted talent.

“What martial art’s mnemonic chant is this?”

“The reason I was able to use the Heavenly Demon Annihilation to my heart’s content earlier was precisely because of this martial art.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk revealed the identity of the mnemonic chant.

“It is the Space-Time Transference Technique.”

The Space-Time Transference Technique was a martial art that Geom Ujin knew of as well.

It was the exclusive martial art of the Wind Heaven Cult Leader; he had never imagined his son would know it.

“I know of the Space-Time Transference Technique as well. That person taught this to you?”

“Yes, when the Wind Heaven Cult Leader first came to our cult, I had the opportunity to receive his instruction.”

“You must have had a reason for reciting this mnemonic chant for me.”

How quick-witted he was.

“Yes, I dearly wished to teach this secret technique to you as well, Father. I have already obtained the Wind Heaven Cult Leader’s permission.”

Because if one became proficient enough to open the Space-Time Transference Technique in an instant, it would be the surest method to escape a moment of crisis.

“I refuse.”

Just as expected, his father refused flatly.

“I wish to take a walk with you in a new space.”

“The inside of an illusion technique may look nice, but it is suffocating.”

“That is not so. It is exactly like reality. No, it is more real than reality. And how useful a martial art this is! You will understand if you see it for yourself.”

Snap.

Geom Mugeuk unleashed the Space-Time Transference Technique.

A blue plain unfurled around them. Because he had entered this place alone, he must have looked as if he vanished in an instant before his father’s eyes.

‘Will you still say it is not useful?’

It was precisely at that moment.

Rip!

The space before his eyes tore open, and something burst through.

Astonishingly, it was the Heavenly Demon Sword.

Screech.

The world of the Space-Time Transference Technique was torn straight through, and his father entered through that tear. Astonishingly, he had destroyed the Space-Time Transference Technique.

“Did you see a method to destroy it?”

To Geom Mugeuk’s shocked reaction, Geom Ujin replied with a look that said it was only natural.

“Did you not know that no demonic or heretical art works before the Nine Transformations Demonic Art?”

This was the might of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art that had achieved Twelve-Star Grand Completion. The Nine Transformations Demonic Art at Twelve-Star Grand Completion could even tear through the Space-Time Transference Technique.

Through the torn area behind where his father had entered, the outside world was visible. He had opened the Space-Time Transference Technique countless times until now, but such a sight was completely unfamiliar.

“Father, you can enter, but others cannot, can they? In a moment of crisis, it can become a useful refuge.”

His father spoke with a puzzled look, as if asking why he should hide.

“There is no occasion on which I would flee.”

At his father’s resolute attitude, Geom Mugeuk finally unfolded his strategy: provoking his father’s competitive spirit.

“The Wind Heaven Cult Leader told me he mastered this martial art in three days. He said no one would ever break his record.”

The corners of Geom Ujin’s lips rose in a sneer. Even though he clearly knew he was being provoked.

“How long did it take you?”

“I am the Heavenly Martial Vessel, after all.”

“I asked how long it took.”

Geom Mugeuk had known from the start that the Wind Heaven Cult Leader’s time would not provoke him. Naturally, his father would think he could master it faster than the Wind Heaven Cult Leader. What his father cared about was his son’s record.

Originally, it had taken exactly two double-hours. But mastering it in two double-hours was impossible even for his father. Because he might not even attempt it from the beginning.

“It took six double-hours.”

He presented a time worth challenging for his father.

Forgive me for the white lie! Father, even if you say you will learn it but not use it, that is fine. Please, at least learn it!

“Father, you cannot compare yourself to me. Simply breaking the Wind Heaven Cult Leader’s record would be remarkable enough—”

His father cut him off and set a time.

“Let us meet again in five double-hours.”

Chapter 774: Ugh, Those Dreadful Jians!
---------------------------------------
Late that night, Gwak Yeong was cleaning up the forge with a body that felt as heavy as a thousand pounds.

She wiped the worktable with a damp cloth and swept the ash and iron filings on the floor into one pile.

Once the youngest master artisan of the Murim Alliance, she was now doing menial chores in the forge of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Ever since coming here, she had silently devoted herself only to the work assigned to her.

At first, many had looked upon her with displeasure. They disliked that she had come from the Murim Alliance, and they disliked that she was a woman.

Enduring that disregard and contempt in silence, she never shirked the dirty work. Thanks to that, the way people looked at her these days had changed greatly.

The nature of those who worked iron was like fire. What they hated, they hated with fiery intensity, but what deserved acknowledgment, they acknowledged without fail. Without question, she had worked hard enough to earn everyone’s recognition.

It had also been a time for her to look back on herself.

‘That’s right. This was how I felt when I first learned forge work.’

When had it begun? After becoming a master artisan, her shoulders had unknowingly risen with pride, and she felt she had lost that venomous determination. Perhaps the reason those men who had kidnapped her younger brother had targeted her was because they had slipped into that loosened state of mind.

Just as she finished the exhausting work for the day and was about to be the last to leave the forge, someone called out to her. The one who had not yet gone home and remained in his office was none other than Master Gwak of the forge.

“Starting tomorrow, use the fire.”

To be told to use the fire meant she was being told to make weapons. As the permission she had been waiting for finally fell, Gwak Yeong’s face brightened.

“Thank you.”

Master Gwak had treated her indifferently, but he had been watching her all along.

“There are still people who look upon you with displeasure.”

“I know.”

“Prove yourself with the sword you make.”

“Yes!”

As Master Gwak left the forge, Gwak Yeong bowed her head again and again in thanks.

Gwak Yeong walked over to the extinguished furnace.

At last, she could make a sword! How long had she waited for this moment?

She forced herself to hold back the tears that threatened to spill out. She must not grow weak.

Her younger brother, who had applied as a low-ranking martial artist, had begun his life as a demonic cultist. She could tell just by looking at his face how hard and difficult it was, yet he too was gritting his teeth and enduring.

‘We’ll be able to do this.’

After looking around the empty forge, she stepped out.

As she closed the door and came out, someone spoke to her.

“Congratulations.”

Startled, she turned around to find Geom Muyang standing there.

A look of gladness flickered across her face, but it soon hardened as she asked,

“By any chance?”

“If you are asking whether I asked Master Gwak to let you make swords, then no. If I had done that, you would never be able to work here.”

He was right. The Master Gwak she had experienced was not someone who would accept such external pressure, even from the eldest young master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. On the contrary, if such a request had been made, she would never be allowed to make swords for the rest of her life.

“I simply wanted to tell you congratulations.”

Yesterday, Master Gwak had come to find him and said that the time had come to let Gwak Yeong make swords. That was how he had learned of it, and why he had come today.

The reason Geom Muyang paid attention to her was because she had come all the way to this distant Heavenly Demon Divine Cult because of him.

Having said that, Geom Muyang was about to turn away when Gwak Yeong spoke.

“Buy me a drink.”

When Geom Muyang turned back to her, her old self appeared for the first time in a long while.

“Why are you looking at me like that? You know I was originally this bold, don’t you? I’ve just been holding it in since I came here. Eldest Young Master, please put up with my temper just for today. I was one step away from collapsing from pent-up frustration.”

She spoke so confidently that Geom Muyang ended up letting out a faint laugh. Encouraged by his favorable response, she continued.

“Today, at least, I want someone to congratulate me. As for asking you to buy, as you know, I’m not yet in a position to receive proper monthly pay. A month from now, I’ll buy.”

Geom Muyang nodded. As it happened, he had been debating whether or not to suggest a celebratory drink. He had turned away because he worried it might burden her, but she had spoken first.

Yes, he wanted to congratulate her. She truly had worked hard. He did not regret introducing her to Master Gwak in the slightest.

There was someone watching from afar as the two walked off together. It was none other than Geom Mugeuk. He had gone to see his older brother’s face, only to be told by a subordinate that he had gone to the forge, so he had come looking.

His brother had not forgotten Gwak Yeong and was looking after her.

If it had been his former brother, it would have been something he would not have cared about at all. Seeing how much his brother had changed, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened.

“You have to become even cooler from now on, hyung.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk returned straight to his residence.

During the time he had promised his father, Geom Mugeuk unfolded the Space-Time Transposition Art and trained in martial arts. At this time, his father would also be learning the Space-Time Transposition Art. He himself unleashed the Nine Flames Demonic Art again and again.

Because he had activated the Time-Heaven Secret Art, he was not merely waiting five si-chen.

The Time-Heaven Secret Art, which had once granted him seven times as much time, now granted him nine times as much. Five si-chen in reality was forty-five si-chen for Geom Mugeuk, nearly four days.

Geom Mugeuk had no intention of telling his father about the Time-Heaven Secret Art, which slowed the flow of time within the Space-Time Transposition Art.

If it was his father, he would surely achieve great mastery of the Time-Heaven Secret Art as well, and he might even try to obtain the true pinnacle of martial essence.

In doing so, he might become a Demonic Immortal and ascend, or he might spend the rest of his life absorbed in martial arts training and never come out of the Space-Time Transposition Art. It was not a joke; he truly might lose his father.

‘I’m sorry, Father. I trust that you would control it well, but this son of yours is the sort who worries far too much.’

Geom Mugeuk focused on training the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

‘I will break through the great mastery of the tenth level of the Nine Flames Demonic Art and reach great mastery of the twelfth level!’

Since they had lost the Twelve Branch Kings, there was no telling when Hwa Mugi might appear.

Even so, he intended to struggle until the very last moment. If, despite working this hard, he still could not defeat Hwa Mugi, then at that time he would acknowledge it like a martial artist. Yes, you won.

When he grew tired from training, he opened the hot spring and soaked his body; when he grew tired from training again, he opened the island and rested while feeling the sea breeze.

Throughout his training, he was curious.

Would his father be able to succeed within the time limit?

In that way, five si-chen passed in reality.

Geom Mugeuk went to see his father again. His father was waiting in the private training ground.

First, he gauged his father’s mood.

Had he learned it within the time limit? Or had he failed?

But from his father’s expression, he could not tell whether it was failure or success. At times like this, only one thought came to mind.

‘Father, you truly should have become a gambler!’

For now, there was a chance he had failed.

“Even if you failed, please do not be too ashamed. Five si-chen is an absolutely impossible amount of time. I do not like such an inhuman father. So please speak frankly. ‘My son, five si-chen is too much. Give me a little more time.’”
