One corner of his father’s mouth, filled with confidence, rose slightly.

Snap.

His father flicked his finger.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin were standing in a new space. A white space with nothing in it.

It was his father’s Space-Time Transposition Art.

“You did it!”

Geom Mugeuk jumped up and down in delight. He was happier than when he himself had succeeded. For now, as long as his father could unfold it, whether or not he used it was a matter for later.

That was a matter his father would choose, and Geom Mugeuk was satisfied enough that he now had a hidden move to protect his father.

“I will definitely tell Cult Leader Pungcheon. That Father took five si-chen.”

“Four si-chen.”

“What?”

“I finished it in four si-chen.”

“Cult Leader Pungcheon will probably faint from shock!”

That would not be the case. He already knew that Geom Mugeuk had learned it in two si-chen. He would probably say this instead: Ugh, those dreadful Geom men!

“You are truly amazing! I respect you, Father.”

The fact that his father could open a space so instantly left Geom Mugeuk not merely impressed, but astonished. He himself had learned the Space-Time Transposition Art in two si-chen, half the time his father had taken.

But after that, he had trained and refined it for a long time in order to open a space so instantly. Yet his father had opened this space in an instant.

Was it due to some special enlightenment he had obtained by achieving great mastery of the twelfth level of the Nine Flames Demonic Art? Or was this Space-Time Transposition Art itself a martial art that innately suited his father perfectly? Whatever the reason, it was certain that this was an astonishing result.

Seeing his son sincerely rejoice, a satisfied smile appeared on Geom Ujin’s face.

It felt as though the wholehearted concentration and effort he had poured into learning the Space-Time Transposition Art had been fully rewarded. No, his son was far happier than that.

There was another reason for Geom Mugeuk’s joy.

He knew that his father had accepted this bet that was not quite a bet not simply because of competitive spirit.

He had accepted it because he knew his son was worried. Geom Mugeuk was deeply grateful for that.

Of course, his father likely still did not think a situation would come in which he would have to open the Space-Time Transposition Art and take refuge.

“When you first succeed, you can only make an empty space like this. But as your level in the Space-Time Transposition Art gradually rises, you can create various things. You can even perfectly recreate a place from your memories. I will show you. I do not know whether my Space-Time Transposition Art can unfold inside this place.”

Unfolding the Space-Time Transposition Art within the Space-Time Transposition Art; it was his first attempt.

Snap.

Geom Mugeuk unfolded the Space-Time Transposition Art again inside the Space-Time Transposition Art.

Amazingly, it unfolded even within.

Whoooosh.

The two of them stood together at the top of a snow-covered plain. The wind that blew in whipped their clothes and hair.

Geom Ujin touched the snow on the ground. It was truly cold snow. The ice cliff across from them shone as it reflected the sunlight.

“Isn’t it incredible?”

Geom Mugeuk flicked his finger again.

Then a plum tree appeared beside them, and winter plum blossoms bloomed amid the snow-laden wind.

When he flicked his finger again, a small rock appeared beside it.

“You can create anything you want like this. Ah, and you do not need to flick your finger. I only do it because it looks cool.”

At that moment, his father said something unexpected.

“Like this?”

Snap!

Their surroundings changed again.

This time, within the Space-Time Transposition Art that Geom Mugeuk had created, his father’s Space-Time Transposition space appeared.

And Geom Mugeuk, after looking around, was startled. It was not a white space. Things far too familiar to him were visible.

“No, how?”

The Heavenly Demon Hall had been created. Though it had not yet been perfectly recreated, the three most important things were there. The Path of Blood, the stairs, and the grand chair.

His father had already succeeded in creating the space most familiar to him.

With the gait of a victor, his father climbed the stairs and sat in the grand chair.

Before his father, who looked pleased upon the chair, Geom Mugeuk jumped in place.

“I can’t live like this, I really can’t! Father, how can you sit in the grand chair even inside the Space-Time Transposition Art? I taught you the Space-Time Transposition Art partly so that you would please get away from that grand chair!”

With an expression that seemed to say, Is that so? his father rose from his seat and snapped his finger.

Then the grand chair and the other newly created things disappeared.

Seeing his father handle objects freely, though still in a limited way, Geom Mugeuk was amazed.

“You are truly remarkable. Even though you are not the Heavenly Martial Body, you can do this much. If Father had been born with the Heavenly Martial Body, it truly would have been chaos.”

He meant it sincerely. It was certain that the reason he had been born with the Heavenly Martial Body originated from his father’s innate martial talent.

And his father’s sense was different from the Heavenly Martial Body.

“If you are going to make something, you should make a place like this.”

Snap!

The next moment, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Ujin were standing on a snow-white sandy beach where the blue sea stretched endlessly. Since he could not maintain two spaces at once, the snowfield he had made earlier must have disappeared.

A blue sky and a blazing sun.

On the beach stood a tree with large leaves, and beneath its shade rested a chair that looked comfortable.

It was a place he had intended not to show anyone. Yet he felt he wanted to show it to his father.

“This is the space of my own that I treasure most.”

When he flicked his finger again, another comfortable chair appeared under the broad-leaved tree.

“Try lying down here.”

Geom Ujin lay side by side with his son on the white sand beach. Lying in such a beautiful place, where warm sunlight poured down and soft sand lay beneath him, felt new and strange. In his life, had he ever lain in a chair like this on a beach like this?

“When I lie here and look up at that blue sky, all the cares of the world disappear.”

“What cares could a young fellow like you have so many of?”

“Don’t I go around meddling in every last affair of every sort of person? On top of that, I’m even doing the most pointless thing in the world—trying to look good to everyone.”

“At least you know.”

And so the two of them silently gazed up at the sky. As he looked at the heavens, his father suddenly asked,

“Are you lonely?”

His father knew as well. That loneliness was not something felt when one was alone, but when there were many people around.

“Sometimes.”

At that, Geom Ujin turned his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk looked back at his father and spoke as if it were nothing.

“When I come here, rest a while, and leave, I’m fine again in no time. Father, you have times when you want to be alone too, don’t you? Without Uncle Hwi, without guards—truly alone.”

“If I asked them to leave even now, I could be alone.”

“That’s different. This place is truly and thoroughly my own space. Here, you can curse all you want! You can run around naked if you like!”

His father’s gaze returned to the sky. No matter how real this blue, clear sky felt, to his father it was not real.

“No matter how hard reality is, and no matter how comfortable this place feels, do not become too immersed in illusion.”

“Yes. I’ll keep that in mind.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Father, are there times when you feel lonely?”

“No.”

“You could at least have worries, couldn’t you? What is your greatest worry, Father?”

At that, Geom Ujin turned his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk laughed loudly and answered,

“Besides me.”

I suppose so. A son standing in the way of his father’s dream! To Father, I may be the worst kind of troublesome guest.

“Then I have none.”

“When you put it that way, it makes me sound like too much of a troublemaker.”

Could his father truly have no worries? There was no way.

When would the day come when his father could comfortably say everything in his heart? It felt more difficult than achieving the twelfth-star great completion of the Nine Flames Demon Art. Even so, I’ll keep trying until that day, Father.

Still, it was a relief that he seemed more interested in the Space-Time Transposition Art than expected.

“I’m looking forward to the space you’ll create for yourself, Father. I’m curious where you feel most at ease. Later, please let me have a look as well. Ah, the Heavenly Demon Hall is absolutely not allowed! And not the Grand Preceptor Chair either!”

Geom Ujin gave no answer, but he did not say he would not make one.

What sort of space would his father come to cherish most? Would the day ever come when he would be allowed to see it?

Geom Mugeuk glanced sidelong at his father.

How can it be that seeing you lying comfortably feels this unfamiliar? Please rest as you go, Father.

Still, perhaps he had taken a liking to this place.

“The sea breeze is truly nice.”

Beneath the chair, Geom Mugeuk’s fingers struck swiftly against one another.

A cool wind cutting through the heat blew even stronger, and a flock of seagulls crossed the sky. Beside his father’s chair, crabs scuttled past in a neat single file.

‘Rest comfortably, even if only here, Father.’

Chapter 775: Now That I’ve Learned Good Martial Arts
----------------------------------------------------
It had been truly long for Geom Ujin.

Had he ever enjoyed such a pleasant, comfortable rest?

Of course, he had rested countless times until now.

But this moment felt more precious and more delightful than any rest he had ever taken.

The reason was probably—

Geom Ujin quietly turned his head and looked at the son seated beside him.

Even now, his son was diligently flicking his fingers beneath the chair, trying to improve his mood. And those attempts had not ended with seagulls and crawling crabs.

Swaaaaaash!

From the sea ahead, a snow-white stream of water shot up into the sky.

Puuuuung.

A huge whale appeared above the surface. Geom Ujin could not hide his surprise. He truly had not expected a whale to appear.

“Have you ever seen a whale?”

“Only in pictures in books.”

“And yet you made it so lifelike.”

Living as a martial artist in the Central Plains, seeing a real whale was no easy thing. Geom Ujin had only seen them a few times because, in his youth, he had wandered the Central Plains with the Fist Demon. Even then, after becoming the Cult Leader, it seemed today was the first time he had seen a whale with his own eyes.

Geom Mugeuk, too, had seen whales several times in his life before regression, but most of them had only passed by in fleeting glimpses.

There had been exactly one time when he saw a truly unforgettable whale. He was now recreating that intense memory exactly as it had been.

Woooooo.

The whale let out a low, deep cry.

Geom Ujin looked at his son again. He had never heard one himself, but it truly seemed like a whale would make such a sound. It sounded as though the sea were yearning for the sky.

“There is a trick to this.”

Geom Mugeuk explained to his father how he could freely unfold the imagery in his mind within the Time-Space Illusion Art.

How to circulate the incantation, and how to set his mind and heart.

Geom Mugeuk hoped, by any means, that his father would take a greater interest in this Time-Space Illusion Art. Only then would his father use the Time-Space Illusion Art in an emergency.

Listening to his son, Geom Ujin watched the whale swimming across the sea.

“The whale looks lonely.”

No sooner had those words fallen—

Puuung!

Another stream of water burst upward, and another whale rose to the surface. Two whales swam together, and around them, small dolphins leapt into the air.

Watching the scene, Geom Ujin smiled without realizing it. Truly, this was the first time in his life he had ever seen such a sight.

Geom Ujin was not the only one feeling happiness.

Geom Mugeuk had been happiest when he was alone in this space. But now, he felt even more joy and happiness than when he was alone. To think he would feel such emotions here, of all places. His father was not the only one changing.

The whales swam off somewhere, and the surface of the sea grew calm again.

Geom Ujin silently gazed at the ocean. In the past, he would already have said, “Let us leave now.” But those words did not come.

He sat there thinking they should stay just a little longer, and before he knew it, Geom Ujin drifted off to sleep. And he dreamed.

It was a dream of his younger days. That time before he had even become the Young Cult Leader.

And as though he were having a lucid dream, Geom Ujin saw himself objectively. Had I ever laughed that brightly? Had I ever been that fiery with anger? Had I ever expressed my emotions so well?

How long had he slept like that?

When he opened his eyes, Geom Mugeuk was asleep beside him as well.

Geom Ujin was inwardly surprised. No matter that they were within the Time-Space Illusion Art, and no matter that he was with his son.

It was the first time in his life he had ever taken a nap without even realizing he had fallen asleep.

The warm sunlight and cool breeze were still there.

‘So he can maintain the Time-Space Illusion Art even while sleeping.’

Geom Ujin could tell that the level of the Time-Space Illusion Art his son wielded was truly high.

Naturally, this illusion art must have been created to kill people. It had been an illusion art of the Blood Cult, after all.

It must have been made to draw the opponent in, startle them, confuse them, and kill them with ease.

And yet his son was using that fearsome martial art as a space for rest. He was creating whales.

“When did you wake up?”

Geom Mugeuk belatedly woke from sleep, yawning.

Geom Ujin spoke with feigned sternness. It was not something he had any right to say, since he had slept as well.

“Can a martial artist be this careless?”

“If an enemy comes, Father will stop them.”

His son had clearly seen him fall asleep first and then fallen asleep after him, yet he spoke as if he himself had been asleep first.

“Let us get up now.”

“Yes, Father.”

Before leaving the Time-Space Illusion Art, Geom Mugeuk spoke to his father one last time.

“Father, today was wonderful.”

Ordinarily, he would have merely nodded once, or perhaps not reacted at all.

But today, unlike usual, his father nodded as well and conveyed his own feelings about this place.

“It was good for me too.”

At his father’s words, Geom Mugeuk was moved. More than anyone, he knew that his father was not the sort to say such a thing if he had not truly meant it.

Seeing how genuinely delighted his son was by his words, Geom Ujin suddenly recalled the youth he had seen in his dream. Those days when he could say things like this as much as he wished.

His son had taught him something he had forgotten.

Perhaps what he had learned from his son all this time was how to reveal and express these emotions.

“I will leave first.”

Just as he was about to flick his finger, his father stopped him.

“Wait.”

Today’s time together was not over yet. When he said they should get up, he had not meant they should leave this place.

“Since I have learned a good martial art, I should teach you one technique in return, shouldn’t I?”

His father was never one to simply receive something without giving back.

Geom Mugeuk waited for his next words, wondering if it could possibly be what he thought.

“I will transmit to you the Eighth Form of the Guhwa Demonic Art.”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes trembled. No words could shake his heart like the transmission of the Guhwa Demonic Art.

“Isn’t it too soon?”

From the Seventh Form to the Ninth Form of the Guhwa Demonic Art, they were martial arts used by the Heavenly Demon Soul.

Before he had left the cult, his father had transmitted the Seventh Form, Demon Soul Creation, to him. And now he was already going to transmit the next technique.

“Is that not the way your fate is flowing?”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk understood. It was because he had undergone rebirth that his father was transmitting it to him sooner. Indeed, Geom Mugeuk’s guess was correct.

“Since you have achieved rebirth, you will be able to understand the incantation more easily.”

Geom Ujin looked at his son and spoke calmly.

“From now on, I will transmit the Eighth Form of the Guhwa Demonic Art.”

Geom Mugeuk respectfully gave a formal bow, then knelt and listened attentively.

“Stand up.”

Geom Ujin had his son rise, then gave him an unexpected instruction.

“Make a walking path. A path with many changes along the way.”

Geom Mugeuk pondered for a moment, then flicked his finger.

Snap.

A small trail appeared before the two of them. Once one passed through the trail, there was a stream; beyond it, after crossing a small field, there was a bamboo grove. Beyond the bamboo grove, there was even a waterfall.

“Let us walk together.”

Amazingly, his father transmitted the incantation while they strolled.

The Eighth Form of the Guhwa Demonic Art: Demon Soul Rush.

The second demonic art used by the Heavenly Demon Soul.

“The Demon Soul rushes through a new world, and all bow in reverence and praise. The beginning of true qi starts from the Cheonchu point. The first departure of true qi must be light and powerful; after passing through the Jungwan point, it must become heavy and composed; upon reaching the Daeju point…”

An incantation was originally something one had to memorize with intense concentration. Memorizing an already difficult incantation while walking like this was by no means easy.

Moreover, the Eighth Form’s incantation was more complex and difficult than that of the Seventh Form. The Seventh Form itself had already been more complex and difficult than any incantation he had learned until then, but the Eighth Form was even more difficult than that.

That was why, if it were transmitted while sitting still, he would have had to memorize the whole thing as it was. Its system and progression were completely different from existing martial arts, and there were many parts that defied common sense, so it was not easy to memorize.

But now it was different. His father was transmitting the incantation in a new way.

“When true qi arrives at the Baekhae point, a blue energy will swirl before your eyes…”

When transmitting this part, he plucked a green leaf from a tree and sent it flying toward Geom Mugeuk as though throwing a hidden weapon.

“Turn the true qi in reverse again, but maintain a gentle energy.”

When the true qi had to be reversed, his father turned around and touched the leaves of flowers blooming along the path behind them.

When he transmitted the part where one had to skip across the blood vessels, he spoke the incantation while crossing stepping stones.

“…When true qi passes through the Myeongmun point, that energy must be drawn in as though forming a vortex.”

At that moment, he spun his hand in circles, and palm force whirled from his hand toward Geom Mugeuk’s Myeongmun point.

Swaaaak.

Even as Geom Mugeuk threw himself aside to dodge, he memorized the incantation precisely. Rather, it became easier to memorize.

“…It must move coldly, like autumn frost.”

When reciting this phrase, his father dipped his hand into the stream.

For the part where the true qi had to be carried on gently yet strongly, he bent a bamboo stalk greatly and released it; when it was time to extend a powerful, firm energy, he sat on a rock and transmitted the teaching.

If there was anything connected to the incantation, he made sure to use it.

When that difficult incantation harmonized with his father’s actions and the surrounding environment, it became easy to memorize. No, it was not merely easy; it felt as though he would never forget it, even if years passed.

And into this special act of memorization, his father’s heart for his son was engraved as well.

That precious teaching came to an end when their long walk concluded and they returned to the place where they had begun.

“I think I have memorized the entire incantation.”

To confirm it, Geom Ujin asked him various questions about the incantation. After carefully asking whether he had properly memorized the difficult parts, Geom Ujin wore a satisfied expression. His father’s expression said it clearly. His memorization was perfect.

“No, Father. How did you know this secret method of transmitting an incantation while walking? It was much easier to memorize.”

“Since I was young, whenever I had to memorize something difficult, I memorized it that way. I used other objects around me to memorize it.”

“Thanks to that method, I memorized it all.”

“Even using that method, memorizing such a difficult incantation in so short a time should be impossible. I expected we would have to take this walk three times.”

Of course, memorizing the incantation and manifesting it within the body were different matters.

Because he had to move his internal energy in an entirely new way, Geom Mugeuk asked his father about the parts he did not understand. As always, his father answered his questions with sincerity.

Recently, whenever the two of them discussed martial learning, one of them would attain a deep accomplishment in martial studies.

That was why the contents of their conversation were even more profound, and even if they spoke about the same subject as last time, there was always some new enlightenment and learning.

Once the exchange of questions and answers with his father ended, Geom Mugeuk understood the incantation without obstruction. His memory had certainly improved, and so had his comprehension.

The effects of rebirth were clearly there. Originally, it would have taken several times longer, but he had instead learned it faster than he had learned the Seventh Form.

He did not ask his father how long it had taken him to learn the Eighth Form. He did not want to scratch the pride of his father, who was needlessly competitive.

But it seemed this was not something that wounded his father’s pride, but something that made him proud.

“Among all the past leaders of the Demonic Cult, you are likely the one who learned the Eighth Form the fastest. In both how far you have come, and how long it took you to learn this Eighth Form.”

The emotion clearly visible on his father’s face was closer to pride than astonishment.

Geom Mugeuk gave the credit to his father.

“Among all the past leaders of the Demonic Cult, Father is likely the one who made the Eighth Form easiest to memorize and understand.”

Geom Mugeuk was truly happy. The Guhwa Demonic Art nearing completion meant that he, too, was nearing completion—both as a martial artist and as the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“As I have said before, the power of the Heavenly Demon Soul is determined by how well you understand the incantation. So whenever you have time, you must never neglect understanding and training in the incantation.”

At least for the final three techniques, there was no need to separately summon the Heavenly Demon Soul and train. Everything depended on how deeply he understood.

“I will keep that in mind.”

“Now, let us leave.”

Geom Mugeuk flicked his finger first.

Snap.

Then the world within the Time-Space Illusion Art that his father had created appeared. They had created another and another within the Time-Space Illusion Art.

Snap.

This time, when Geom Ujin flicked his finger, they finally returned to reality. It was his father’s private training ground, where Geom Mugeuk had first come looking for him.

The two left the training ground and walked through the inner palace.

“Father, I have two goals regarding the Heavenly Demon Soul.”

“What are they?”

“I told you before, but the first is to speak with the Heavenly Demon Soul.”

Last time, his father had told him that among the Heavenly Demon Souls of the past, there had been some that spoke.

“The second goal is to make the Heavenly Demon Soul’s size smaller. About the size of a person.”

“And after you make it so?”

“I will go around with it like a friend.”

Because he had never once imagined such a thing, Geom Ujin shook his head with an expression of disbelief.

“Yes. Perhaps if it is you.”

In the past, he would have called it madness, but now he had a premonition that he might see even that absurd sight one day.

“But where are you going now?”

“To take care of the work that has piled up.”

“Just play hooky for the rest of the day. The cult will still run just fine.”

His father answered as he walked ahead.

“And why do you think it runs just fine?”

Leaving unspoken the fact that it was because of such efforts as these, his father headed toward the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Geom Mugeuk stood there watching his father’s back, then spoke loudly.

“Father, since your already clever son has now learned such a precious memorization method, I will soar even higher from now on. Thank you very much!”

His father stopped walking for a moment and answered without looking back.

“Let us say it is repayment for letting me see whales.”

Yes. Among the many unforgettable things from today, that moment when he had watched whales together with his father would never be forgotten.

“Just say the word anytime. I will have those fellows on standby.”

His father resumed walking toward the Heavenly Demon Hall. Geom Mugeuk could not see his face, but perhaps he was smiling.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the sky.

At last, he had learned the Eighth Form of the Guhwa Demonic Art.

Now only the final Ninth Form remained.

Chapter 776: Strike Before Impurities Mix In
--------------------------------------------
After parting from his father, Geom Mugeuk wanted to summon the Heavenly Demon Soul and see what had changed after mastering the Eighth Form of the Ninefold Demonic Art.

But before that, his first priority was to greet the person who, judged by appearance alone, most resembled the Heavenly Demon Soul.

“Master! Have you been well?”

The Fist Demon noticed the change in Geom Mugeuk at once.

“Take off your clothes.”

“To strip your disciple the moment you see him!”

“Stop fooling around!”

Geom Mugeuk took off his upper garment completely.

The Fist Demon stared at him in silence, then his eyes widened.

“Don’t tell me. Did you undergo rebirth?”

The Fist Demon recognized the changes in Geom Mugeuk’s body at a glance. Because they had often trained together bare-chested, he was even more certain.

“Yes. During this trip outside the cult, I was blessed by heavenly fortune and underwent rebirth.”

For an instant, astonishment crossed the Fist Demon’s face. To anyone, rebirth was a miraculous fortune one might not even hear of once in a lifetime.

Just as his father had done, the Fist Demon examined Geom Mugeuk’s body carefully. If his father had looked him over with a parent’s concern, fearing that something might have gone wrong during the rebirth, the Fist Demon was examining his body for a different reason.

“For someone who practices fist arts, rebirth is special beyond comparison to those who practice other martial arts.”

Thinking about it, he was right. Fist arts, which used the entire body to fight, made the body itself the weapon. That weapon had been changed.

To the Fist Demon, rebirth held such great meaning that he could put it this way.

“You, who had been unfolding the martial arts of man, will now unfold the martial arts of Heaven. You have become capable of reaching the realm of the unity of Heaven and man.”

Thinking about it, perhaps the one who had desired rebirth more than anyone was his master. Knowing that feeling, Geom Mugeuk listened to his master’s words seriously.

“With that body, failing to bring the Thunderbolt Asura Fist to full mastery would be far too shameful to both the martial art and that body, wouldn’t it?”

Geom Mugeuk had already gone to the Fist Demon once before and asked him to teach the Thunderbolt Asura Fist from the beginning again. Through that training, Geom Mugeuk had been able to rise to the next stage.

His level of insight into martial learning had risen so much that even full mastery of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist no longer seemed so distant.

“I will make certain to achieve full mastery this time.”

No sooner had he learned the Eighth Form of the Ninefold Demonic Art than the Fist Demon told him to achieve full mastery of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

Geom Mugeuk considered this chain of events to be another fortuitous encounter brought about by rebirth.

In this way, rebirth was organically connected to unforeseen events and influenced them. It was not merely a fortune of mind and body.

“Have you fought since undergoing rebirth?”

“No, I have not.”

He wondered why his master was suddenly asking that.

The Fist Demon pondered something intently for a moment. Though his appearance suggested he would have no worries in life, the Fist Demon was more prudent than he looked.

“Are you busy right now?”

His master, who had just shown himself deep in thought, was asking if he was busy. No matter how busy he was, there was only one answer. Even if his father called him, this was a situation where he would have to say, I cannot go right now.

“No. Time is the one thing I have plenty of!”

“Wait here a moment.”

The Fist Demon went into his residence and came back out shortly after.

He was carrying a box. Geom Mugeuk could not tell what was inside, but from the traces of age upon it, he could tell it had not been used for a very long time.

“Follow me.”

The Fist Demon took Geom Mugeuk and left the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

The place they arrived at after racing with lightness arts was Mount Daecheon. The Fist Demon brought Geom Mugeuk to a small clearing near the largest waterfall on Mount Daecheon.

He set the box down on a rock there, then stood facing Geom Mugeuk.

“Do you trust me?”

“Why are you suddenly saying that?”

“Answer me.”

“I do. I trust you more than I trust myself.”

It was not empty flattery. There was a particular trust that a man like the Fist Demon inspired. He seemed like the sort of person who, if told to wait a hundred years, would silently stand in that place for a hundred years.

“How much do you respect me?”

Geom Mugeuk flinched and took a step back.

“Why are you being so frightening?”

“Answer me.”

“I respect you far more than you think, Master.”

“Then at the very least, you won’t get angry if I beat you for no reason, will you?”

“What?”

The moment the words fell, the Fist Demon’s fist split the air.

Whoosh!

Thud!

At the sudden surprise attack, Geom Mugeuk clutched his stomach and was sent flying. Was it because there had been no killing intent? Or because the attack had not been infused with internal energy? The Heavenly Demon Protective Art did not activate.

“From now on, no matter how I attack, do not use internal energy and do not use any protective art.”

Geom Mugeuk rose to his feet.

“It isn’t anyone else’s fist, but yours, Master. If you say that, you’re basically telling me to die…”

Thud!

Geom Mugeuk was sent flying by the Fist Demon’s fist again.

If internal energy had been infused into his fist, Geom Mugeuk would naturally have died or suffered a fatal wound.

But the Fist Demon’s fist contained only the power of the thunderbolt. He could infuse his fist with the power of thunderbolt without using internal energy.

Of course, did that mean it would not hurt? He was being beaten by the Fist Demon’s fist.

Thud! Thwack!

The Fist Demon’s fists struck him in succession. From the places he was hit, a tingling power of lightning flowed through him.

“No, are you beating me because your disciple underwent rebirth before you did? If you keep this up, I’m going to spread rumors that you hit me out of jealousy…”

Thud, thud, thud, thud!

The fists began flying in earnest. The Fist Demon did not strike only certain parts. He struck his whole body evenly. Not only his torso, but his arms, legs, and even his face.

Gradually, the force of the fists grew stronger, and the flowing power of lightning also intensified.

Even while being beaten senseless, Geom Mugeuk naturally understood. The Fist Demon was tempering his body.

Otherwise, there was no way he would beat his disciple like this.

That was why Geom Mugeuk struggled as hard as he could not to activate his protective art.

But no matter how true it was that no internal energy was infused into the blows, with his body continuously feeling pain, would the Heavenly Demon Protective Art simply remain still? Geom Mugeuk used all his strength to keep it from activating.

‘It hurts this much, and I have to work to keep you from activating!’

Thud! Thwack! Thud!

“No, is it fair to hit me from behind?”

The incoming fists grew even stronger.

“It hurts! Master! Your disciple is going to die!”

It was not an exaggeration. It truly hurt. On top of that, whenever the power of lightning flowed through the place he had been struck, screams burst out of him on their own.

Even while being hit, he could feel it.

The Fist Demon was striking him while concentrating every nerve in his body.

‘Right now, Master is having a harder time than I am.’

He could feel that the Fist Demon was expending a tremendous amount of mental strength. That made sense, because if he struck even slightly wrong, Geom Mugeuk could truly be badly injured. No, if luck was poor, he might even die.

Thud! Thwack! Thud, thud, thud!

When Geom Mugeuk could no longer endure the fists and was about to faint, the Fist Demon shouted loudly.

“Endure it!”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, which had been about to close, snapped open.

“This process is like hammering iron to make it stronger. Right now, the material is ten-thousand-year cold iron. I am hammering this material before impurities can mix into it.”

The ten-thousand-year cold iron was his own body, and the hammer striking it was the Fist Demon’s fist.

It was the fist of the person in the martial world who used fists better than anyone else, a fist into which he poured every nerve, and even lightning dwelled within it. It was, quite literally, the most precious hammer in the world.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

If he had never become the Fist Demon’s disciple, there would never have been such a fortuitous encounter. He would not have done such harsh fist-work on the young cult leader.

But now, the Fist Demon was throwing his fists while regarding him as his disciple.

After being beaten with all his might, the Fist Demon’s fists finally stopped.

Geom Mugeuk collapsed just like that, but it was not over.

“Go under the waterfall right now.”

“With this body?”

As if to say there must be no delay, the Fist Demon threw Geom Mugeuk under the waterfall.

“Absolutely do not use internal energy!”

“Then I’ll die! Aaargh!”

Contrary to his words, however, Geom Mugeuk did not use internal energy and took the waterfall with his bare body. It hurt so much he thought he might die, but he tried to let the waterfall strike every part of his body evenly.

That had to be why his master had thrown him in here. From the fact that they had started here in the first place, this process was likely just as important as being beaten.

Rooooooar.

When the falling waterfall hammered his injured body, it truly felt as if his entire body were melting away. It hurt more now than the pain of rebirth.

But Geom Mugeuk gritted his teeth and endured.

How much did he trust the Fist Demon?

He trusted him enough to think it would be all right even if something went wrong and he died.

He endured and endured, until in the end, Geom Mugeuk fainted in the waterfall.

How much time had passed?

When Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes, the Fist Demon was looking down at him.

The terrifying Fist Demon looked haggard, as though he had exhausted an immense amount of mental strength.

“…Master.”

“You’re awake?”

He was lying on a rock beside the waterfall, and an unfamiliar medicinal scent was coming from his body.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. The medicine now smeared over his body had been inside the box the Fist Demon had brought earlier.

“You endured well. I believed that you would be able to withstand it.”

Seeing the smile on that blunt face, Geom Mugeuk felt that things had gone well.

“A body that has undergone rebirth is truly excellent in and of itself. Usually, one would call it perfect. But if that excellent body is properly broken in, it becomes even more perfect.”

The Fist Demon revealed that what had happened earlier had not been mere punching.

“The method I used on you was the Thunderstrike Tendon-Forging Method. It destroys the skin and muscles with the power of lightning, then lets them recover anew. It is a secret art unique to our line, passed down together with the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. It is a secret art that can be used only once, and only on a body that has undergone rebirth. I never thought the day would come when I would use it in my generation.”

Geom Mugeuk raised himself up. He had thought he would be in so much pain he could not even get up, but as if it were a lie, his body felt light.

“Go wash yourself clean in the waterfall.”

Geom Mugeuk went under the waterfall and washed his body.

After coming out, he examined himself. He had been beaten so terribly, yet there was not a single bruise left on his body.

His skin had become clearer and cleaner, and the quality of his muscles had also changed. He could feel with his entire body that he had become stronger. The ten-thousand-year cold iron had now become a treasured sword forged of ten-thousand-year cold iron.

“It would have been better if someone superior to me had performed the Thunderstrike Tendon-Forging Method, but this was the best I could do.”

“Where in this world could there be a fist better than yours, Master?”

Geom Mugeuk sprang to his feet and gave the Fist Demon a deep bow.

“Master, thank you. Thank you truly for connecting my fortuitous encounter to an even greater one.”

The Fist Demon smiled with satisfaction, pleased that his effort had not been in vain.

“Make certain you achieve full mastery of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist as well.”

The fortune of rebirth did not end there.

“Master, please teach me again.”

Geom Mugeuk did not postpone training in the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. When the Fist Demon had devoted himself to him to such an extent, he could not delay full mastery.

Rather, this was an opportunity for him.

And Geom Mugeuk knew. The door of opportunity never remained open for long.

“I will come to see you every day until the sound of thunder disappears from my fist.”

Geom Mugeuk did not set full mastery alone as his goal. He aimed for a deeper realm: achieving full mastery while also making the thunder vanish from his fist. In martial learning, there existed a momentum that could not be explained by common sense. He intended not to let that momentum slip away.

‘I’ll break through everything in one go!’

Geom Mugeuk thrust his fist forward, and the Fist Demon thrust his own fist before it. The size of their fists was still that of an adult and an infant.

“Good. Now let’s make that fist into an adult’s fist!”

From that day on, Geom Mugeuk began to cultivate the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

After finishing the training that had begun that very day and returning home, he opened the Space-Time Transference Art, employed the Heavenly Time Secret Art, and swung his fists.

The next day, when he went to see the Fist Demon and trained together with him, the Fist Demon was inwardly surprised.

‘He changed this much in just one day?’

He thought it was because rebirth and the secret art he had performed had influenced him, but that was not the only reason. Geom Mugeuk’s training time had not been merely one day.

Training with Geom Mugeuk after his rebirth gave the Fist Demon a profound sense of inspiration as well.

How could it not?

The one who had undergone rebirth was none other than Geom Mugeuk, and he had even received the Thunderstrike Tendon-Forging Method. This was training done together with such a person.

Whenever they paused to rest, the questions Geom Mugeuk asked touched upon the core of fist arts, and for the Fist Demon, who was advancing toward the ultimate principles of fist arts, they became opportunities to reflect on fist arts and on his own training. The two shared the deep martial principles of martial learning without holding anything back.

The Fist Demon completely abandoned the notion that he was teaching.

They were training together.

With that feeling, he immersed himself in training with Geom Mugeuk. The fortune of rebirth continued, reaching even into the Fist Demon’s heart.

When it came to martial training, training with the Fist Demon suited Geom Mugeuk best.

The Fist Demon was a man who did not know how to give up. Once he began throwing fists, he was a man who would keep throwing them until his arms fell off.

The same was true for Geom Mugeuk.

No sound came from the Fist Demon’s fist, while thunder resounded from Geom Mugeuk’s fist.

When he had trained with Geom Mugeuk before, the Fist Demon had said this.

—I do not know whether sound will ever come from my fist again.

The thunder had no longer sounded from his fist, but it meant that if he broke through that stage again, sound would be heard.

One man threw his fists to make the sound of thunder ring out once more, while the other threw his fists to stop the sound of thunder from coming from his own.

Every day, the sound of thunder made by Geom Mugeuk echoed from the training ground of the Eastern Fist School, the Fist Demon’s sect. The Iron Fists of the Eastern Fist School trained while listening to that thunder.

Cheon Sohui, the next Fist Demon, gazed toward the place where the thunder sounded with deepened eyes.

‘Senior Brother is truly training as if his life depends on it.’

For thunder to continue sounding like this meant that he was endlessly throwing his fists. At that endless sound of thunder, all of them grew solemn.

When their master and eldest senior brother were training that hard, how could they be negligent in their own training?

The Iron Fists all trained together as if their lives depended on it. Even after they all collapsed from exhaustion, Cheon Sohui continued throwing her fists alone.

Even after Cheon Sohui collapsed, the sound of thunder continued.

And so, as his bloody training with the Fist Demon went on, one day…

Geom Mugeuk’s fist cut through the air. In that instant, Geom Mugeuk’s form froze.

From the fist that flew out and struck as it always did, no thunder sounded.

‘I did it!’

Just as Geom Mugeuk looked at the Fist Demon with a face filled with joy, the fruits of their efforts, which had begun together, fell together as if by fate.

Ruuuuumble!

From the Fist Demon’s fist burst forth a heaven-and-earth-shaking thunderclap unlike anything they had ever heard until now.

The sound, which shook even the axis of the earth, was an enormous roar that could be heard anywhere in the vast Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

It was the moment the master of the thunder’s sound changed.

Chapter 777: Enough to Bulldoze Through Any Trap
------------------------------------------------
At the tremendous peal of thunder, the demonic practitioners of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult started in fright.

Those drinking spilled their liquor, and those sleeping sprang awake. Those carrying luggage dropped their loads, and the faint of heart screamed.

Everyone’s gaze turned toward the place the thunder had come from.

Never in their lives had they heard thunder so loud, so they all assumed something had exploded.

Deng! Deeeeng!

Bells announcing an emergency rang out one after another, and the demonic practitioners of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult ran busily across the training grounds.

The thunder had even reached the Heavenly Demon Hall, and Chief Strategist Sama Myeong, who had been reporting various pieces of information from Tongcheon Pavilion to the Heavenly Demon, flinched in surprise.

He wondered if something truly had happened within the cult.

By contrast, a smile formed on Geom Woojin’s lips. Geom Woojin knew exactly what that sound was.

“The Fist Demon’s fist has touched the heavens.”

At those words, Sama Myeong understood. That thunder just now had been the sound of the Fist Demon’s fist.

The Fist Demon’s martial arts had reached the realm of startling heaven and shaking earth.

And what he had just been reporting to Geom Woojin was that Geom Mugeuk had devoted himself to fist arts training with the Fist Demon again today.

‘The Young Cult Leader influenced even the Fist Demon’s realm.’

Sama Myeong knew better than anyone that the reason the Demon Venerables had been growing endlessly stronger of late was because of Geom Mugeuk.

Was it because of the Fist Demon’s thunder?

Geom Woojin spoke, letting slip an inner thought he rarely revealed.

“I miss the days when I roamed the Central Plains with that man.”

Sama Myeong smiled as he recalled those days.

“The two of you were truly extraordinary.”

While they briefly reminisced about the past, the next Fist Demon, Cheon Sohui, was staring in the direction of her master’s training ground, overcome with emotion.

“Master!”

Just as Geom Woojin had known, she knew as well. That sound had come from her master’s fist.

The fact that such a sound could come from a human fist sent a shudder through her. And the hope that the same sound could one day come from her own fist made her heart swell.

‘Someday, I will too!’

It was the moment Little Fist Demon Cheon Sohui’s goal in life was decided.

The very person who had produced that tremendous sound, however, was calm.

The Fist Demon withdrew the fist he had thrust forward.

“Congratulations, disciple.”

He knew that just before he had thrown his own punch, no sound had come from Geom Mugeuk’s fist.

“I should be the one congratulating you. Truly, congratulations, Master.”

The Fist Demon looked down at his own fist. It was the moment he had longed for so desperately, yet his heart was unexpectedly calm. He was so happy that, rather, he felt dazed.

“I would like to hear that thunder again, but if you did that, everyone would think a real war had broken out, wouldn’t they?”

Even now, the bells announcing the emergency were ringing out in the distance.

“Master, how do you feel?”

A smile formed on the Fist Demon’s blunt, fearsome face.

“I feel good.”

When had he ever shown his joy so honestly? Long ago, he had shown it to the Cult Leader.

The Fist Demon’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk. This much was certain. If not for the training he had done together with Geom Mugeuk, he would never have reached this realm.

“It is thanks to you.”

“Of course it is. It’s thanks to me.”

The Fist Demon clenched his fist again and asked,

“Did you say you wanted to hear that sound again?”

“No! It is all thanks to you, Master!”

To think that his taciturn master would even make a joke like this! Geom Mugeuk could tell just how pleased he was.

Looking down at his own fist, Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude, this time in earnest.

“I succeeded thanks to you, Master.”

Not only had Geom Mugeuk achieved great mastery of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist, but beyond that, the thunder sound had vanished from his fist. Truly, Geom Mugeuk had reached a terrifying realm even in fist arts.

“We should gather everyone in the cult and hold a banquet. Ah, I’ll call Ian first. Ian, your father has grown even stronger! In fists, he is the strongest in the history of the martial world!”

Of course, such fussing would never work on the Fist Demon.

The Fist Demon moved his feet and left the training ground first.

“Go and rest. You worked hard.”

After taking a few steps, the Fist Demon stopped.

“Congratulations.”

From his mouth came the words Geom Mugeuk had so desperately wanted to hear.

“Now your fist has become an adult’s fist.”

* * *

After returning to his quarters, Geom Mugeuk immediately opened the Spacetime Transposition Art.

The place he created today was a hot spring in a snowy plain.

“Ah, this is nice.”

Geom Mugeuk stripped off his clothes and submerged himself in the hot spring. Ever since he had begun training in fist arts, he had devoted himself to training even at the expense of sleep.

After gaining the fruit of that bloody effort, the joy of resting like this for a moment could not be compared to any other pleasure.

Snap.

Snow began to fall over the snowy plain. Soaking in the hot spring amid the beautiful snowfield, Geom Mugeuk silently watched the falling snow.

He spent time alone like that, relieving the fatigue that had built up.

The speed at which his body recovered was incomparably faster than before. Not only because of the effects of his rebirth of flesh and bone, but also because of the mysterious energy in his dantian.

After leaving the hot spring, Geom Mugeuk raised his blazing yang energy, dried his body, and put on his clothes.

Then he opened a new space.

Whooooosh.

In a windswept wasteland, he summoned the Heavenly Demon Soul.

Originally, when he had learned the Eighth Form of the Nine Calamities Demonic Art, Demon Soul Rush, he had wanted to summon it immediately and check.

But once he had begun his fist arts training in earnest, he had postponed this reunion.

What sort of change had come over the Heavenly Demon Soul?

As Geom Mugeuk’s will was conveyed, the Heavenly Demon Soul was suddenly standing before his eyes.

No matter how many times he summoned it, it was wondrous that such an enormous Heavenly Demon Soul could manifest without even a sound of wind.

Geom Mugeuk treated the Heavenly Demon Soul comfortably, like a friend.

“I told you last time, didn’t I? This is the world inside the Spacetime Transposition Art. I opened it in a different place from last time for a change of pace. I didn’t know what sort of space you might like.”

Last time he had opened a field, but today he opened a wasteland. A place where sandstorms from beyond the borders blew in.

Somehow, he thought this lonely space might suit it better.

“If you don’t like the sandstorm, I’ll change it to somewhere else.”

Of course, the Heavenly Demon Soul showed no reaction.

But Geom Mugeuk could feel it.

‘It doesn’t like this place.’

Geom Mugeuk sensed the Heavenly Demon Soul’s emotions. He could commune with feelings on the level of like and dislike.

Snap.

As their surroundings changed, Geom Mugeuk and the Heavenly Demon Soul were once again standing on a plain.

Swaaaaaaah.

Geom Mugeuk made rain fall. The streams of rain washed the dirt and dust clinging to the Heavenly Demon Soul clean away.

Soon after, the rain stopped and the sun rose. A cool breeze blew in. The sunlight and wind dried the rain on the Heavenly Demon Soul’s body.

Then he felt that the Heavenly Demon Soul’s mood had improved compared to earlier.

‘It really does have emotions.’

And the reason he could feel those emotions was likely because he had learned the Eighth Form. The more forms he learned, the closer he grew to the Heavenly Demon Soul!

“You’ve changed again!”

There was a change in the Heavenly Demon Soul’s appearance.

When he had learned the Seventh Form, a sword had appeared on the Heavenly Demon Soul, and a faint, indecipherable pattern had appeared on its chest.

Now that he had learned the Eighth Form, that once-faint pattern on its chest could be seen clearly.

The pattern embedded in its chest like a heart was in the shape of a flame tinged with blue light. Was it because this was embedded in the Heavenly Demon Soul’s chest? It looked like the flames of hell.

And upon closer inspection, it was not a simple flame. It was the face of a blazing evil spirit—the very face of the Heavenly Demon Soul, shaped from fire.

That was why the flame was frightening as well. It blazed fiercely, glaring terrifyingly at its opponent.

“When I learn the final Ninth Form, I hope I’ll be able to talk with you then.”

The very moment he spoke his regret.

Sssssk.

The Heavenly Demon Soul slowly began to bend its body.

Geom Mugeuk watched its actions with a startled heart.

The Heavenly Demon Soul bent at the waist and extended its hand.

Its palm was held out to him.

It meant for him to climb onto it.

Geom Mugeuk carefully stepped onto that palm.

Sssssk.

The Heavenly Demon Soul straightened its back and stood. Its hand was held out in front of its chest.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze and the Heavenly Demon Soul’s met in midair.

“Thank you, friend.”

Geom Mugeuk was moved. It was the first time the Heavenly Demon Soul had extended its hand toward him.

The fact that it placed him on its hand like this surely meant it was a being with reason.

But the Heavenly Demon Soul expressed no further action or emotion and simply looked silently at Geom Mugeuk.

“Even this much means we’ve become quite close, doesn’t it?”

Seen up close, the Heavenly Demon Soul’s face was terrifying beyond measure.

“And don’t worry that I’ll be too frightened of your face. I’ve just come from spending all my time with someone every bit as frightening as you.”

Geom Mugeuk turned around on its palm.

Far away, the sun was setting over the horizon. The boundary between sky and earth grew indistinct as crimson light dyed the surroundings, and before it, trees with the sun at their backs stood in a row like an ink painting.

The spreading light of sunset colored Geom Mugeuk and the Heavenly Demon Soul. And then they, too, became one scene within that landscape.

The two stood there silently for a long time. Though he exchanged no words with the Heavenly Demon Soul, his heart was at ease.

“Later, let’s talk, and I’ll make your body smaller so we can go out together into the real world, not this fake one.”

Just then, outside the Spacetime Transposition Art, he sensed someone’s presence.

Geom Mugeuk turned to the Heavenly Demon Soul and bid it farewell.

“See you again later, friend!”

When he said goodbye, the Heavenly Demon Soul disappeared. Was it because of the mood today? Normally it vanished in an instant, but today it seemed to disappear a little more slowly.

Once the Heavenly Demon Soul disappeared, Geom Mugeuk, who had been on its palm, slowly descended to the ground by treading the void and released the Spacetime Transposition Art. When he landed, he was in his own room.

“Young Cult Leader, are you there?”

At that voice, which was far too welcome, Geom Mugeuk ran outside.

The Extreme Evil Soma, wearing a pure white mask, stood in the courtyard. It was an unexpected visit from him.

“Soma!”

“Young Cult Leader!”

The two eyes within the Extreme Evil Soma’s mask were smiling with delight.

Truly, there was only one emotion he felt whenever he saw those smiling eyes within the Extreme Evil Soma’s mask. A deep and special feeling that could not be expressed in words.

And today, the Extreme Evil Soma had not come alone. The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master, who had come with him, greeted him.

“Have you been well? Young Cult Leader.”

“So you were in the cult as well, Pavilion Master.”

“Young Cult Leader, you’ve become even more handsome while I haven’t seen you.”

If that change was only something the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master sensed vaguely, the Extreme Evil Soma grasped Geom Mugeuk’s change precisely.

“I know you trained with the Fist Demon. Is it because of that thunder?”

He was asking whether this change now was because of that training. Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“No. This time, when I left the cult, I underwent a rebirth of flesh and bone.”

Since everyone was noticing the change like this, he had no choice but to tell them about the rebirth.

“And on top of that, Master added an even greater fortuitous encounter.”

The Extreme Evil Soma’s eyes filled with joy. Seeing those eyes, which were happier than if he himself had undergone the rebirth, what other words were needed?

The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master also congratulated him with a surprised expression. How could she not know that rebirth of flesh and bone was an immense fortune?

“Truly, congratulations, Young Cult Leader.”

“Thank you. Rather than standing here, please come inside.”

Geom Mugeuk guided the two of them in.

“Pavilion Master, would you like some tea?”

“If it’s tea brewed by you, Young Cult Leader, how could I refuse?”

Geom Mugeuk brewed tea and brought it out. It was the finest tea among the several varieties he had.

“Perhaps because you brewed it, Young Cult Leader, this is the most delicious tea I’ve ever had.”

“Of course. It is steeped full of my respect and affection as well.”

After they drank tea for a while and chatted about this and that, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“By the way, what brings you to me?”

If nothing had happened, there was no reason the two of them would have come to see him together.

As expected, something serious enough to bring them had happened to the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master.

“I was contacted in secret.”

The problem was the identity of the person who had sent that message.

“The Divine Maiden Palace Master sent word that she wants to meet me.”

The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master, who had first received the message, and the Extreme Evil Soma, who had heard it from her, had both judged that this was not a matter to handle alone.

“Did she state her business?”

“No. She said she would tell me when we met.”

“Where did she ask to meet?”

“She asked to meet at Dongting Lake in Hunan Province.”

Hunan Province was a region where the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s influence was strong. But that did not mean they could feel at ease. The other party might have been aiming for precisely that sort of complacency.

“She told me to come alone, but I thought I shouldn’t, so I informed my elder brother.”

“You did well.”

It was a message that had arrived after the Sword King and Fire King had been taken away and all the remaining Twelve Zodiac Kings had died.

Of course, it was possible that the real Divine Maiden Palace Master had something to say to the Cheonhwa Pavilion Master and had sent word for that reason, but it was more likely to be a trap.

After all, it would not be strange at all for the Dark Palace Master to join hands with the Divine Maiden Palace Master and struggle desperately in any way he could.

The Cheonhwa Pavilion Master felt her own limits.

“It would be good if a prophecy descended at a time like this.”

No prophecy had come down. Then had a prophecy perhaps descended upon the Divine Maiden Palace Master? Was that why she wanted to meet?

Geom Mugeuk recalled the Divine Maiden Palace Master who had delivered a prophecy to him three hundred years ago. This would be a meeting between that Divine Maiden Palace Master’s descendants.

In that case, their decision was set.

“If the Divine Maiden Palace Master wants to meet, then you must meet. However!”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes gleamed sharply.

“I intend to move differently from before. If this matter is a scheme, they will expect me and Soma to come with you, Pavilion Master. That is how it has always been until now.”

Until now, with Hwa Mugi in mind, they had been careful and then careful again, but now the situation was far too different from before his regression.

So he intended to respond differently from how he had until now. He had no intention of moving according to their wishes any longer. Even if it was a trap, he would be prepared to sweep it all away.

“This time, we will go properly equipped.”

Chapter 778: I Should Have at Least One Pushover Too
----------------------------------------------------
Geommugeuk went straight to his father.

Since he needed his father's permission to leave the sect together with the Demon Venerables. Especially in this case, he had to receive proper permission.

"Father! Your son's fists have become adult fists now. The days of being teased by Master for having baby fists are over!"

Geommugeuk spouted such nonsense as he walked the Path of Blood.

"I have finally achieved Grand Mastery of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. If there are any unruly fellows, just say the word!"

"You!"

"Ah, that's right. There aren't any aside from me, are there?"

Geommugeuk stopped beneath the Taesa.

Geomujin silently gazed at his son.

"So that person bestowed a precious secret technique upon you."

Remarkably, Geomujin had noticed that his son had grown stronger through the Thunderclap Tendon Technique.

"How did you know?"

Indeed, if it were his father, he could recognize Rebirth at a glance.

But the changes brought by the Thunderclap Tendon Technique were largely internal; there weren't that many external changes. And yet, he recognized it with a single glance?

"That's correct. Master used a secret, exclusive technique to make my body stronger."

There was a reason Geomujin recognized that fact immediately. He had already known about that secret technique.

"Do you know what kind of secret technique it is?"

"What kind of secret technique is it?"

"It is the technique that person dreamed of. To achieve Rebirth and receive the Thunderclap Tendon Technique as well—that is the long-cherished dream of those who learn the Thunderbolt Asura Fist."

It hadn't been merely passing down a technique. Fist Demon had willingly handed his dream over to his disciple.

No matter how slim the possibility of achieving Rebirth, giving up a lifelong wish couldn't have been easy. Yet Fist Demon hadn't hesitated in making that choice.

"If that was the case, he should have boasted about it a little while bestowing it."

At those words, a smile formed on Geomujin's lips toward one who was not present.

"That is the kind of person he is."

That man and he alike had lived their lives without ever knowing how to boast.

"Oh, Master has also reached a new realm."

"I know."

As expected. His father had known that Fist Demon had reached a new realm from the sound of thunder alone.

"Truly amazing. What in the world don't you know, Father?"

"If you're done showing off, be on your way."

"No, I didn't come today just to show off."

Geommugeuk relayed to his father exactly what the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord had conveyed.

"Therefore, I believe I must leave the sect immediately tomorrow. And this time, I intend to go out in proper formation. I will take all the Demon Venerables except for two."

Since Blood Heaven Blade Demon and One Flower Sword Venerable were outside, there were six Demon Venerables within the sect. He was saying he would take four of them.

At the mention of leaving two behind, Geomujin spoke.

"Since you're taking them out, take them all."

It was a sentiment for his son.

"Then you would have to guard the main sect alone, Father?"

One corner of Geomujin's mouth rose slightly.

"Why? Do you think I cannot guard it alone?"

"Of course you can guard it."

"Then why?"

"If Father goes out to unify Murim while I'm away, someone would have to stop you, wouldn't they?"

He spoke as if joking, but it wasn't entirely an exaggeration.

The Guhwamagong of Twelve Stars Grand Mastery.

Not even Geommugeuk could imagine what that power would be like.

When he had asked his father, who walked out of the Heavenly Demon Hall alone, where he was going.

"I go to unify Murim."

Who could dare laugh at that?

"And we must consider the Strategist. If all the Demon Venerables leave the sect, he will be greatly worried. Uncle Hwi and the guard unit will also have to worry several times more. So I will leave two behind and go."

Having put it in terms of other people's concerns, his father said no more about taking them.

Instead, he seemed curious about whom he would take and whom he would leave behind.

"So whom will you leave behind?"

* * *

Chwima was thoroughly drunk.

When he saw Geommugeuk, he slurred his words and waved his hand.

"Our little brother! Come here and drink this hyung's liquor first."

He had drunk so much that the area around the Drunken Dream Tower was filled with intoxicating energy like fog.

"Why are you so drunk from broad daylight?"

Chwima shook the liquor bottle.

"In this lonely life, this fellow is my only friend!"

The moment the words "lonely life" came out, Geommugeuk knew.

Ah, he's sulking!

Yes, he had every right to sulk. Because Geommugeuk had come to see him last again this time.

"It makes the friend listening feel bad, calling me your only friend!"

"Who might this be? Oh my, isn't this our busy Young Cult Leader? What brings such a noble person to this shabby place?"

"Hyung, where did that magnanimous demeanor you showed me last time go?"

Geommugeuk imitated Chwima.

"After it passes, it's all useless. Would I die regretting that you didn't come see me?"

As he said this, Chwima even looked up at the sky and laughed hollowly.

"Where did our great-man-like hyung go?"

Chwima pouted his lips.

"That great-man hyung withered away from loneliness. Now only the narrow-minded, petty hyung remains. No matter how late I come, I said it's fine, but you really come last?"

"Hyung, this is sincere. I'm not coming late because you're easy, but because I think you'll understand the best."

"That's exactly what being easy means!"

When Chwima shouted in anger, Geommugeuk employed a brazen reversal.

"So what if I'm a little easy!"

"What?"

"I need to have at least one easy person too!"

"What is this? Such shamelessness? I'm sobering right up."

Geommugeuk filled his cup with the liquor bottle before him and downed it in one shot.

"Blade Demon Elder is difficult because he's an elder; Fist Demon is difficult because he's my master; the Sword Venerable is too elegant and noble, so he's difficult; Seophon Majon is too young, so she's difficult; Mabul only likes you, so he's difficult; Dokwang is immersed in poison, so he's difficult; Soma is too precious, so that's difficult. For someone like me, I need at least an easy hyung like Chwima in this world! Right? Be my easy hyung for life!"

Listening with a dumbfounded expression, Chwima eventually burst into laughter.

"Right, that's right. One needs to have at least one easy person in life. That's how you breathe and live. Fine! I shall be that easy person."

"The petty hyung has left, and the great-man hyung has returned!"

"This is exactly the magnanimity of drinkers! Come, let's drink!"

The two men clinked their cups vigorously and emptied them.

"Come last from now on too!"

"There are good points to it! When you come last, there's plenty of material to badmouth the other Demon Venerables. No need for side dishes!"

"Excellent! Let's badmouth those people and drink to our hearts' content!"

The two men hit it off and emptied their cups.

Of course, Geommugeuk knew. That Chwima wasn't drunk because Geommugeuk came late. Despite saying it hurt his feelings, Chwima was no longer the same Chwima he used to be.

Feeling the intoxicating energy filling the surroundings, Geommugeuk asked casually.

"Were you training?"

As expected, Chwima nodded. The reason he was so thoroughly drunk was because he had been training in martial arts.

"It's all because of you. The good thing about being Chwima was that I could drink and live recklessly."

But at some point, the other Demon Venerables began to grow stronger. At the forefront of that effort was his friend, Ilhwa Geomjon. The other Demon Venerables were the same.

"Thunder strikes a dry sky; how can I drink with a peaceful mind?"

Then Chwima tilted his head.

"What? Is it because I'm drunk? You, something seems different?"

Chwima blinked his drunken eyes and looked at Geommugeuk. Geommugeuk told Chwima that he had achieved Rebirth too.

"If my little brother achieved Rebirth, I can't just let that pass. We must properly drink a celebratory toast. Yeobin, bring the best liquor I've been saving."

Yeobin really brought the liquor. It was liquor Chwima had cherished and saved.

"When did you hide this again?"

"I hid it away tightly for a day like this."

"Bring out the liquor you hid to drink when you achieve Rebirth, not this!"

The two drank. The taste was truly superb.

"The liquor is really delicious."

"On a day like this, one must drink good liquor! Drink more!"

It was truly the noisiest congratulation he had received to date. Yes, it should be this much for a Rebirth celebration. One should dance drunk.

"Does Rebirth change your organs too? I want to drink with the heart of starting anew with clean insides!"

"Don't worry. People like hyung unexpectedly have clean insides. You're probably cleaner inside than I am."

Chwima laughed loudly saying that would be the case. Geommugeuk drank comfortably with Chwima for the first time in a while.

"What was that thunder yesterday?"

"It was the sound of Master reaching a new realm."

"Then what about you?"

"I also achieved Grand Mastery of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist."

"Already?"

How long had it been since he became a disciple, and he already achieved Grand Mastery?

"Why are you becoming this strong? Are you perhaps dreaming of unifying Murim? You rascal! Am I right?"

Unifying Murim.

Although Chwima said it as a joke, Geommugeuk thought that someone who really didn't know might indeed misunderstand.

Although the Martial Alliance and the Evil Alliance weren't showing it outwardly, he felt perhaps they were nervous.

"Does the hangover from Daechwirim still hurt?"

Chwima silently nodded.

"That's why I can't help but train."

"Fortunately, it hurts less than before."

"Really?"

The drunkenness and playfulness briefly vanished from Chwima's face.

"That's why you can come late to me. It's proof that you're really doing well now."

Chwima emptied his cup alone. The blowing wind fluttered Chwima's disheveled hair.

They were noisy and boisterous. Although the Demon Venerables had changed a lot because of him lately.

Thinking about it, they had all been lonely people.

Hyeolcheon Doma, who had believed he lived with misfortune his whole life; Gwonma, who stood alone before a cliff; Ilhwa Geomjon, who had hidden his heart with thick makeup; Geugak Soma, who stood alone in an empty room looking at a white wall; Dokwang, who didn't leave Cheondok Rim while immersed in poison insects; Mabul, who ran around on his short legs to make his brother the Young Cult Leader; and this Chwima, who was always drunk alone. Yes, they had all been lonely people.

"I'm leaving the sect tomorrow. I wish you'd come with me, Hyung."

"Where?"

"Hunan, Dongjeong Lake."

"Who all is going?"

"We'll be going with quite a few this time."

Chwima didn't bother asking who.

He leaned against the pillar behind him and drank.

"I shall go and drink with you."

* * *

The next person Geommugeuk visited was Seophon Majon.

Seophon Majon was immersed in martial arts training in her personal training ground. His visit was entirely unexpected to her.

"Young Cult Leader?"

"Have you been well?"

Geommugeuk treated her politely.

"I have been well, thanks to you. I heard you returned to the sect. But what brings you here?"

"I came to greet you after a long time."

Seophon Majon hadn't expected the Young Cult Leader to come find her like this.

Moreover, it wasn't just a greeting.

Geommugeuk told her what had happened during his departure from the sect.

Seophon Majon knew. That when the Young Cult Leader returned from leaving the sect, he would visit the Demon Venerables and tell them what had happened. She had vaguely hoped such a day would come for her too.

Geommugeuk was now beginning to tell her those things too. It meant he properly recognized her as one of the Demon Venerables.

He also relayed the matter of the Heavenly Flower Pavilion Lord.

"So I intend to leave the sect tomorrow. If it is alright, I would like you to come out and help me."

"Of course I must go."

Seophon Majon was so grateful that Geommugeuk had come to her and asked for help like this.

"Thank you, Young Cult Leader."

If she had still lacked skill and seemed like she would be a burden, he would not have asked her to come. He would have told her to accumulate more skill.

Fortunately, Seophon Majon had been growing every time he saw her. Now she was gradually growing into the Seophon Majon that martial artists had once feared the most.

And before leaving, Geommugeuk told her the words she most wished to hear.

"It is reassuring to have my Demon Venerable accompany me."

* * *

"Me too?"

Mabul looked at Geommugeuk with a surprised face.

This is how the four Demon Venerables to go together were decided.

Naturally, those to be left behind were also decided.

Gwonma and Dokwang.

If these two were guarding the father, there was no need to worry about the main sect.

And Mabul was quicker to catch on than anyone.

"Are you perhaps worried about the Cult Leader?"

How could one deceive Mabul, who had navigated politics among people?

"What do you mean?"

"Why leave those two behind?"

Mabul also considered those two to be the strongest among the current Demon Venerables.

"It's inconvenient to go out with Master. I'm the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Cult! I can't put on airs."

"You probably want to do just that. Then what about Dokwang?"

"You know Dokwang hates going out to the Central Plains the most."

But Mabul didn't believe Geommugeuk's words. He thought there had to be a reason those two were left behind.

"You've already killed so many enemies; who in the world are you afraid of?"

Mabul had sensed it for a while. Geommugeuk was more concerned about these enemies than the Martial Alliance or Evil Alliance.

Of course, the enemies from Amheuk Palace that had lasted three hundred years were stronger than imagined. But they were already shattered.

"As you know, I have anxiety. I worry about all sorts of things that won't happen in advance. When you look into it, nothing happens anyway. Anyway, Mabul-nim, you'll come with me, right?"

Mabul, who had been staring at Geommugeuk for a moment, nodded.

"I, who lost in the power struggle, have no strength. If you say go, I must go."

"Now you sound like Mabul-nim."

"Are you saying I sound like myself because you're mocking me?"

Mabul had given his advice to act like a Buddha who emptied his mind, and Geommugeuk was following it.

Geommugeuk, Geugak Soma, Chwima, and Seophon Majon. And Mabul.

Because he thought this was too excessive, Mabul made this joke.

"Are you going to attack the Martial Alliance? Or the Evil Alliance?"

Geommugeuk answered without hesitation.

"I'll hit both sides at the same time."

There was a reason for this absurd joke.

"It's not over yet. I'll take more out."

Chapter 779: Even If We Each Say One Word, the Sun Will Set, Right?
-------------------------------------------------------------------
“I intend to assign the first mission to the Ghost Shadow Corps.”

At Geom Mugik’s words, Ian could not hide her excitement. The moment she had first taken the position of Ghost Shadow Corps Commander came to mind. The time she had fussed about how she would adorn her uniform felt like just yesterday.

“Every squad of the Ghost Shadow Corps is deploying.”

The squad members looked at one another. It was their first official mission since becoming the Ghost Shadow Corps. Until now, they had undergone training akin to actual combat numerous times, but it had been nothing more than training.

The four squad leaders standing before them.

The eyes behind the mask of First Squad Leader Cheongmyeon gleamed sharply, and the ghostly aura of Second Squad Leader Seojin grew denser. Third Squad Leader Chairan was serene, while Fourth Squad Leader Jihan stared at Geom Mugik with a rigid expression.

Their reactions differed, but their hearts were as one. They had to carry out this mission well.

“What is the mission?”

An unexpected answer came to Ian’s question.

“I don’t know either.”

As if it were no joke, Geom Mugik added with a solemn expression.

“The mission will be decided once we arrive. We might set out grandly and return after merely taking in the breeze, or most of the people here might return as corpses.”

Ian could tell that Geom Mugik was speaking from the heart. He was not one to easily use expressions like “you might return dead.”

Geom Mugik wanted to leave them behind if he could help it. But it is a law that a calm sea does not produce skilled sailors; now was the time to cross the waves.

“I believe you will pull through.”

“Leave it to us!”

The warriors of the Ghost Shadow Corps answered thunderously. They could not hide their thrill and excitement for their first mission.

“We will depart early tomorrow, so everyone prepare. All except the squad leaders are dismissed.”

The Ghost Shadow Corps warriors dispersed, leaving only Ian and the four squad leaders.

Geom Mugik looked at each of the four squad leaders one by one.

Cheongmyeon and Seojin, Chairan and Jihan.

Bound deeply by their respective karmic ties. Because they could guess one another’s hearts with just a glance, Geom Mugik did not speak at length.

“If I were worried about an organization whose leaders are the likes of you, I shouldn’t be leading it in the first place. Go and prepare for departure. The Commander will stay.”

The squad leaders left as well, and now only Ian remained.

Geom Mugik relaxed his serious expression, as if he had deliberately stoked the tension.

“You really took a beating keeping that frown on.”

But Ian did not relax her rigid expression.

“Do you know what I was just thinking?”

“What were you thinking?”

“It might be discouraging to hear before a mission, but is it alright?”

“By all means.”

Ian honestly revealed her feelings.

“Ah, this is a job that doesn’t suit my aptitude!”

She had truly thought that.

“The moment I heard that most of us might die, my heart sank and my vision went white.”

To her, who had lived a life protecting others, the death of her subordinates was an unbearable thing.

Even if they returned safely this time, what about next time? A life of losing subordinates one by one—could she truly accept that with composure?

She had pondered similar concerns before, but hearing those words with a real mission looming before her, the dimension of fear was different.

Geom Mugik spoke to her.

“Do you know what I was thinking while saying that?”

“What were you thinking?”

“Ah, this is a job that doesn’t suit my aptitude!”

Ian laughed at hearing the exact same words as her own.

I know. How could I not know? That the Young Master is happiest when he freely roams the Central Plains, punishing villains and laughing merrily with good people.

It was the same for Geom Mugik. How could he not know Ian’s heart?

“Ian.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

“I don’t tell you to live this life to the end. But you must live this life too, so that when you live the life you want, you will know how precious that life is.”

Even now, it was an incredibly precious life to Ian.

For it was a life serving the Young Cult Leader, who provided such a breathing hole in a world full of leaders who suffocated their subordinates. That was why she could hold her breath until her chest felt ready to burst.

“I’ll do my best until the end.”

“Prepare well, and see you tomorrow. Oh, there will be others accompanying us.”

“Who?”

“You’ll see tomorrow.”

Geom Mugik did not tell her specifically who it was.

“Pack everything, even things you think you might not need! Once you’re out there, you’ll regret not having anything.”

And so, even Ian left, and Geom Mugik remained alone. No, strictly speaking, he was not alone.

“Why don’t you ask me to go with you?”

The Sword King silently alighted beside Geom Mugik.

“Do you know where we’re going that you’d take Brother Ak?”

“Why?”

“What if the Dark Palace Lord appears? Could you kill her?”

“I could.”
